# TRUTH App -- Debate Engine Manual

## 0. How to read this manual
- The active version is `SELECT max(version) FROM engine_manual`. `SELECT engine_manual_toc()` gives the live map. The revision history is `engine_manual_log`.
- This manual follows the body law of operational_doc §0 (body law): rules and the mechanics needed to follow them; no provenance in the body; one home per rule and per changeable value. Nothing in this manual is certified; the operator certifies.
- The live record corrects this manual. Where this manual and the live flow or table disagree, the live flow or table is right. Read the node code before acting on any sentence here.
- Source of truth is SQL. The Drive copy is a mirror written by the Daily Drive Sync (operational_doc §7, Daily Drive Sync); never edit it. To revise: INSERT version+1 and its `engine_manual_log` row.
- Retired flows (the W1 and Defends-vs-Claude families, DS-W1, the Kimi flows and the other archived debate flows): engine_manual v84. Their rows stand in `debate_results` as history and are read by their own variant (`debate_variant_canon`).

## 1. What the engine is
- One n8n flow, ORACLE_DEBATE_V1 (ODV1), workflow `moMxiV4Irg1uRBZN`, on the n8n host and Supabase project in the register (operational_doc §11, Quick reference). Its error workflow is the Error Handler `v02XXRlIhoHzMTjJ`, which writes `debate_errors`. Two sub-workflows serve it: WALL-2024 Panel Judge Call (webhook `wall2024-panel-judge-call`, §10 PARALLEL JUDGES) and ODV1 Cure Call (`c7366RPLb8vdMQg6`, §9 CURE CALLS).
- A debate has two debaters and a bench. The DEFENDER holds a declared chain on the topic; the CHALLENGER presses the opposing view and wins only by breaking that chain. A single judge gives a second opinion; a three-seat panel gives the verdict.
- THE FRAMEWORK IS NOT A COMBATANT. Isaiah 28:10 as method, the L1-L4 layer rules and the burden rule are the rules of engagement and bind both debaters and every judge alike.
- Layer tags: [L1] KJV text, [L2] Hebrew or Greek, [L3] cross-reference, [L4] inference.

## 2. Firing a run and reading it
- POST `https://<n8n host>/webhook/oracle-debate-v1`, JSON body `{topic, defender:{engine, profile}, challenger:{engine, profile}}`; optional `queue_id`, `fence_override_id` (§3), and the regrade inputs (§14). Engine and profile are trimmed and lower-cased.
- Respond Accepted answers after Init Seats, the topic fence, Fetch Models, Fetch Drift Guards, Fetch Tensions and Resolve Seats, and before every model call. The body carries `status:'accepted'`, `variant`, `run_token`, both seats and `panel_expected`. Everything after runs without the caller.
- Run token: `ODV1-<execution id>-<8 characters>`. Every refusal ends ` run_token=<token>`. Read the result from `debate_results` by `run_token`; a failed production run also leaves a `debate_errors` row whose message carries the token.
- Dashboard launcher: `dash_launch_odv1(topic, def_engine, def_profile, ch_engine, ch_profile, fence_reason)` posts to the webhook; `dash_odv1_launch_status(request_id)` reads the reply. A passing call to `dash_launch_odv1` IS a launch: verify a change to it by reading its source or by its refusal paths only. Its engine list must equal Init Seats' engine list.
- Manual Start -> Dry Run Config feeds Init Seats a configuration it refuses (two Oracle seats). There is no dry-run flag on the webhook.
- Do not read a debate execution with includeData (operational_doc §7, Editing gotchas). Read rows.
- A full run can exceed an hour once cures and alternates run (ODV1-32255: 71 minutes, without close rounds). Do not read a run as failed by elapsed time alone; read §10, Panel latency.

## 3. Request rules (Init Seats, in this order)
Refused first: a `regrade_run_token` that is not an ODV1 token; `regrade_dry` not boolean, or true without a regrade token; `rulebook` not `current`, `draft` or `chain`, or not `current` outside a dry regrade. Then:
1. topic missing or blank;
2. a seat missing or not `{engine, profile}`; MiniMax or DeepSeek as either debater (neither debates; DeepSeek is the enforcer, md_notes_rulings 421);
3. engine not in Init Seats' engine map (read `ENG` in Init Seats);
4. profile not `oracle` or `plain`;
5. both seats `oracle` (at most one Oracle);
6. the derived panel is not exactly three seats.
After the panel is derived: test inputs (`test_fail_seat`, `test_fail_reserve`, `test_no_fallback`, `qualify_alternate`) outside a dry regrade, a `test_fail_seat` not on the panel, the other two without it; a `fence_override_id` that is not a UUID.
- TOPIC FENCE: Topic Fence posts the topic to `rpc/debate_topic_gate`; Fence Check stops the run unless the gate answers `ok:true`. A fenced topic passes only with a one-use override id from `debate_fence_overrides`, made less than one hour earlier for the same topic; the gate marks it used. A gate error or no answer stops the run.
- There is no same-engine refusal: one engine may hold both seats.
- Resolve Seats refuses a seat with no ACTIVE `model_config` row, no `api_model`, `max_tokens <= 0`, or no `family`; a debater of family `minimax`; a panel judge or single judge whose family debates in this debate. It always requires the ACTIVE `deepseek` row (the enforcer's model, §9).
- Retrieval Check stops the run before any engine call when held-record retrieval fails on a run with an Oracle seat; with no Oracle seat the error is recorded and the run continues.

## 4. Seats, models, tokens, caching
- ENGINE -> SEAT: each debater engine reads one `model_config` seat (map `ENG` in Init Seats; claude reads `claude_synthesis`; haiku and opus read their own rows). A debater whose `api_model` starts with `anthropic/` is called direct to Anthropic (Resolve Seats: the direct id is the api_model without the prefix, with dots as hyphens; any other form fails closed), and each such call writes a `[CACHE DIAG <round>: ...]` line to `review_notes`. Every other debater and every judge is called through OpenRouter. DeepSeek is called on its own endpoint, only as the enforcer (§9). Judge-only seats (`sol`) and the alternates are read the same way.
- CURRENT VALUES (models, token limits, families): `SELECT seat, api_model, max_tokens, family, provider, active FROM model_config WHERE active ORDER BY seat;` Models are read once per run at Fetch Models; a change takes effect at the next run.
- TOKEN RULE: each call sends the seat's `max_tokens` as stored, with no doubling and no added thinking allowance. The code floors (Resolve Seats, Panel Build, Build Synthesis, Build Independent Verdict, Build Diagnostic Prompt) are: synthesis 20,000; single judge 8,000; diagnostic 6,000; panel, reserve and alternate judges 8,000; DeepSeek and MiMo as panel or reserve judges 65,536 (neither judges now); the enforcer call sends the `deepseek` row's max_tokens, at least 16,000. A floor applies only when the stored value is lower.
- NO REASONING FIELD: no call sends a reasoning, effort or thinking field, except for hydrogen (`google/gemini-4-hydrogen`: reasoning effort low on every call; §10, ALTERNATE HYDROGEN), for spark (`meta/muse-spark-1.3`: effort low on every call) and gemini (`google/gemini-3.8-flash`: effort low on every call that is not a debater turn; §10, ALTERNATES GEMINI AND SPARK) and for MiMo (`xiaomi/mimo-v2.6-pro`): Panel Build, Accept Build and the library send it medium effort and 131,072 output tokens, and Reserve Build sends a `xiaomi/` model with reasoning turned off. MiMo is in no judge order now (§10). Node comments that speak of "effort medium" or "max(mt*2,8000)" describe removed code.
- TEMPERATURE: debate rounds send none; panel judges send 0 for DeepSeek, Grok (x-ai) and Gemini (google) bodies.
- CACHING, every model: Anthropic-model bodies (claude) carry cache markers (ephemeral, 1 hour) on the system prompt, split at the Defense Discipline block, and on the last message; every OpenRouter body carries `session_id` = run token plus role, so one run's calls stick to one provider cache; Gemini and Qwen bodies mark the system message for caching; DeepSeek caches on its own. The token columns record cache writes and reads as each provider reports them (§11).
- TIME RULE: a model that cannot answer within the 900-second call limit is removed, as debater and judge, until its vendor releases a newer model. Every model call carries a 900 s timeout option. Removed: qwen as judge (`JUDGE_TIME_REMOVED` in Init Seats). The timeout option is not a hard stop (§17).
- RETRY: the round call nodes in use (OpenRouter and DeepSeek routes) 3 tries, 5 s apart; Fetch nodes 3 x 3 s; Save to DB 4 x 5 s; Retrieve Held Conclusions 3 x 5 s and continues on error; Write Judgment 1-3, Write Enforcer Log and Derive Outcome 3 x 3 s. Panel, reserve, cure and alternate calls carry no retry: a failed judge is replaced (§10). The DeepSeek enforcer calls are split and resent once when they fail (§9, THE SECOND READING and THE DEEPSEEK ENFORCER). When cloning a node, copy its node-level settings (retry, executeOnce, onError); they are not part of `parameters`.
- Credentials are n8n's. A header-auth credential value is `Bearer <key>`.
- CALL FAMILIES: the flow holds call nodes for three families only: anthropic (direct, debaters only), openrouter and deepseek; there are no direct openai, gemini, grok or mimo call nodes. Every Route keeps its output numbers, so `jidx` and `fam_idx` are positional: debater Routes anthropic 0, openai 1, gemini 2, grok 3, mimo 4, openrouter 5, deepseek 6; judge, reserve and single-judge Routes and the cure Cure Call Route (§9, CURE CALLS) deepseek 0, openai 1, gemini 2, grok 3, mimo 4, openrouter 5, skip 6. Outputs 1 to 4 go to Provider Removed Stop, which throws "FAIL-CLOSED (pkgRPN): an item was routed to a removed direct provider call (openai, gemini, grok or mimo): <family> <model>" and fails the run. No seat reaches them: Init Seats `ENG` maps every engine to openrouter except deepseek; Resolve Seats switches a debater to anthropic only on the direct path, and `JFAM` maps every judge seat to openrouter except deepseek; alternates are filtered to openrouter; cure Builds copy the judge's family. The node-name lists in Panel Payloads, Alternate Payloads, R2 Alternate Payloads, Panel Check, Panel Tokens and Build Save Body name only nodes that exist.
- RULES WRITTEN AFTER s82 (each from the package named; where one differs from the text above it in this section, the later rule is right; library line numbers are those of the version the package was written on, so find a line by its text):
  - CHALLENGER CACHE MARKERS (pkgCCM):
    - Where: C2 Build, C3 Build, C4 Build. Nothing a debater reads changes; a cache marker is request data.
    - What it does: C2 Build marks its first message, which is C1's opening already in the 1-hour cache, in place of its last; C2 reads that entry and writes nothing new. C3 Build and C4 Build send their messages with no marker, in the direct Anthropic branch and the OpenRouter anthropic branch. The system marker is unchanged, so the system read stays.
    - Limits:
      - C3G and C4G (the gate re-sends that add to the C3 and C4 body) no longer read that round's message write; they fired in 4 and 5 of 36 stored runs.
      - C2B, C3B and C4B Build (the barred-move re-send) also add to those bodies: C3B and C4B read the system only, C2B the system and the first message only. None fired in 34 readable stored outputs.
      - Not shown by any run. The usage rows of the next run show it: C2 cache write about 0; C3 and C4 cache write 0 and read about 70,000 tokens.
  - ONE RETRY OF A FAILED CLOSE-GROUP REPLY, AND ROUND LABELS IGNORED IN QUOTATION MATCHING (pkgCGR rev 2; Close Group Retry Build, Close Group Retry Needed?, Close Group Retry Call deepseek; the readers Close Enforcer Call deepseek, Anticheat Call deepseek and Assume Check Call deepseek; Shared Library `krulNorm`):
    - The retry: `Close Group Batch Call deepseek` -> `Close Group Retry Build` -> `Close Group Retry Needed?` -> `Close Group Retry Call deepseek` -> `Close Enforcer Resume`; with nothing to retry, Needed? goes straight to Close Enforcer Resume. Retry Build takes each batch reply that came back as an error and sends the same request body once more, in the same order (timeout 900 s). Each of the three readers takes a retry reply in place of the failed reply at the same position when the retry reply is not an error; otherwise the failed reply stands and is handled as before.
    - The labels: `krulNorm`, the normalizer of quotation matching in the cure checks, drops a round label in any of its forms ([CR1], (CR1), bare CR1; CR, DR and GR with one digit) from both the quotation and the record before comparing, as it drops the [L<n>] and [R<n>] tags. A quotation that differs from the record only by a round label is found.
    - Size: code +1,812 (Shared Library stored text +30; each of the three readers +261; three new nodes); prompt text 0; one more DeepSeek request for each failed batch reply.
    - Limits: a quotation of one word after a label is no longer found; no log row says a reply was retried; where a label stood inside a quotation, the word counts of FAILS and contradiction quotations are one lower; the retry path has not fired in a run, and the reply order on a failed retry was not run.
  - A CAP-CUT RETRY HALF IS SENT ONCE MORE WHEN IT HOLDS NOTHING (pkgERC rev 2; the 4 Resend Build nodes: Enforcer, Judge Enforcer, Alt Judge Enforcer, R2 Alt Judge Enforcer; not the older E-PREM package of the same name):
    - Why: in run 36677 an Alt Judge Enforcer retry half hit the DeepSeek output cap (64,000) and came back with choices, finish_reason length and empty content. The resend stage (pkgERS) resent only a half with no choices, so its 11 items stayed NO VERDICT.
    - What it does: a retry half is resent when its reply has no choices, or its content is empty after trim (with or without finish_reason length). A half with any content is never resent, so the Record's swap (RR[s.ers_k]=SR[j]) can only replace a reply that held nothing. ers_why is "no choices", "empty content" or "empty content (finish_reason length)". Record nodes, library and wiring unchanged.
    - Size: code +1,144 (+286 per node); prompt 0; one more DeepSeek call per empty half (at 36677's figures about 13,400 input tokens and up to 64,000 output).
    - Limits: a cap-cut half with partial content keeps its parsed lines, and its items after the cut stay NO VERDICT; a resent half that hits the cap again stays NO VERDICT with no further try. Not shown by a run: run 36791 had no empty half.
  - TWO GROUPS OF DEEPSEEK CHECKS SENT AS ONE HTTP NODE EACH (pkgPDS; Close Enforcer, Anticheat, Assume Check, Enforcer and Judge Enforcer):
    - pkgPDS. Two groups of DeepSeek checks sent as one HTTP node each. Code +3,834 (measured, jsCode), node JSON other than code -817 (five HTTP nodes become Code nodes; two IF and two HTTP nodes are added), 11 more nodes, prompt 0 (every request body is unchanged).
    - Measured first: the calls inside one DeepSeek HTTP node already run side by side (each reply's "created" time is within 1 second of the node's start, in all 13 stored runs). The nodes run one after another, so the saving comes from putting independent nodes' calls in one node.
    - Group 1, at Close Enforcer's place right after Quote Report: Close Enforcer, Anticheat and Assume Check. Their Build nodes read only Init Seats, Resolve Seats, Regrade Load and Final Output, so Anticheat Build and Assume Check Build now run right after Close Enforcer Build. Group 2, at Enforcer's place right after Panel Cure Check: Enforcer and Judge Enforcer. Judge Enforcer Build does not read Enforcer Record or Enforcer Call (it was only wired after them), so it now runs right after Enforcer Build.
    - For each group: "G Batch Build" lists every non-skipped item of the member Builds with a tag; "G Batch Needed?" and "G Batch Call deepseek" (a copy of the old Close Enforcer Call node) send them in one node. Each member's "X Call deepseek" is now a Code node of the same name that hands back its own replies from the batch, in order. So the Records, the retry paths and Panel Tokens read the same items as before, and pkgERS's resend paths are unchanged. Each moved member's old entry point (Check Save, Regrade After Quotes?, Judge Contradiction Record, Enforcer Record) now feeds "X Resume", which hands on that Build's items to X Needed?.
    - Time (measured from stored node times; each group takes its longest member's time): saving per run 2.0 to 5.8 minutes over the 13 stored runs, mean 4.0; run 35067 4.0, run 35296 4.6.
    - How it combines with pkgERS: only the first call of each check moves into the batch. The retry halves and pkgERS's resend still run after the member's Call node, one path at a time, as before.
    - Limits: a run that stops before Anticheat or Assume Check (for example a panel failure, or an anticheat-only run before Assume Check) now still pays for those calls. The time saving is a bound from stored times; DeepSeek may answer more slowly with more calls at once (not measured; a live run shows it). A batch call that does not run makes each member's Call give error items, never another member's reply. Only a live run shows the paths ran.
    - Time context (run 35296, WALL-5086): judging took 60 minutes of the 84; four long calls (Reread 10.5, Alternate 10.9, Alt Judge Enforcer 10.5, Retry 4.0) took 35.9 minutes. The saving of pkgPDS is measured from stored node times (2.0 to 5.8 minutes per run, mean 4.0; run 35296 4.6); the long single replies are not shortened by it.
    - Limits: a run that stops before Anticheat or Assume Check (a panel failure, or an anticheat-only run before Assume Check) still pays for those calls; up to about 10 DeepSeek calls at once, and the effect on DeepSeek's speed is not measured; the long single replies (35296 Reread, 10.5 minutes) are not shortened; the 2.0 to 5.8 minute bound is from stored node times; Judge Enforcer Call on live has retryOnFail false and an explicit batching option (size 1, interval 0), while its batch node is a copy of the Close Enforcer Call node (retryOnFail true, 3 tries, 10 s wait, no batching option); no effect is expected from that, not measured on a live run. Only a live run shows the new paths ran.
    - See the s147 entry in section 18. Limits: see section 17. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 42).

## 5. What each seat is given
- THE ORACLE profile: identity line, role line, layer rules, the held record served for the topic (`debate_arsenal_guarded`: the guard set the topic triggers, then `debate_arsenal_linked`'s conclusions, sights and linked rows), the burden rule, "the text corrects the commentator", the drift guards and open tensions. Guard filter: `rule_num <= 12 OR priority = 'CRITICAL' OR always_inject`, deduped by `rule_num`; the guard text tells the seat never to name or number a guard in the debate.
- ROW CAP: Init with Guards' row cap equals the sum of the five retrieval limits (`debate_arsenal` conclusions and sights, the link cap, the guard-set cap, the link-sight cap); a change to any of them re-sizes the cap in the same change.
- PLAIN profile: "a rigorous biblical scholar", role line, the case for its position from the KJV text, layer rules and burden rule. No guards, tensions or held record.
- POSITION: the Oracle holds the record's reading from either seat; a plain seat facing it holds the standard reading. With no Oracle seated, the defender holds the record's reading. Grading is by role, not position. Prompts name no engine.
- BURDEN AND LAYER-ADMISSIBILITY (both seats, Init with Guards): tag every load-bearing claim with its layer. An [L4] counts as evidence only if it exhibits at least two prior layers ([L1] verse, [L2] lexical ground, [L3] cross-reference) and shows the chain from them; fewer floats free and carries zero weight. No verse is required to state a conclusion (DG-61); a premise resting on the demand that a verse state an exclusion is that retired test. Cite every verse by book, chapter and verse and quote it exactly in the KJV.
- DEFENSE DISCIPLINE DD-1 to DD-3 go to the defender in D1 and D2 only.
- SEED, NOT BLOOD: DG-60 (`drift_guards`, always injected) owns the rule; it reaches ODV1 only as a served guard to the Oracle seat. No seat prompt or judge prompt carries a separate seed-not-blood block.
- CARRY THE VERSE'S OWN CAUSE (Oracle seat only; Init with Guards, the last sentence of the PROVE IT FROM THE TEXT block, which only a seat with profile `oracle` is given): "When a verse states an outcome, carry the cause the verse itself gives; never pin the outcome on a cause a nearby verse names and this one does not (Ezra 2:62 gives its cause, "they were not found: therefore were they, as polluted, put from the priesthood"; it does not say a name taken through a wife's house was refused)." The block is a constant, so the sentence is on every Oracle call on every topic (324 characters). The other seat, the judges and the enforcers do not see it.
- TWO NAMESPACES: conclusions are `#N`, sights are `S-N`; a seat cites only labels in its served list. Init with Guards also lists, for an Oracle seat, the labels named inside its served text (`d_named`, `c_named`); a named label is not a citation error and carries only what the naming sentence says. The judges read the rounds with record labels stripped.
- The posline (who opened, who holds which reading) goes to the synthesis and diagnostic, not to the judges.
- THE DG-8 INSTRUCTION TO THE ORACLE NO LONGER OFFERS DEUTERONOMY 22:24 AS FOOTING FOR THE UNION (pkgD22; node Init with Guards; ASK 256; removal only):
  - Why: in run 36842 the Oracle's record carried a Deuteronomy 22 framing (19 mentions). The source was the engine's own guard text: the DG-8 instruction "state the union as the record holds it ... with its footing" listed "his neighbour's wife," Deuteronomy 22:24 among the footing texts. ASK 250 had removed the same clause from Conclusion 76.
  - What it does: the words ` "his neighbour's wife," Deuteronomy 22:24;` (with the leading space) are deleted. The footing now reads ("Joseph her husband," Matthew 1:19; "Mary thy wife," Matthew 1:20; "till," Matthew 1:25). Nothing is added. No other node of 455 holds "neighbour's wife" or "Deuteronomy 22".
  - Size: Init with Guards 31,520 to 31,475 characters (-45 source, -43 prompt characters per prompt that carries the sentence); calls 0; logic 0; node md5 e5d4500c4eebcadd5eb86275b2f5f1d1.
  - Shown in run 36916: the Deuteronomy 22:24 lines in all three verdicts are the defender's own [DR3] citation; the Oracle's guard text no longer carries it.
  - Limits: the defender's own citation of Deuteronomy 22 is not touched.

## 6. Rounds
D1 -> C1 -> D2 -> C2 -> D3 -> C3 -> D4 -> C4; each Build -> Route (by family) -> Call -> Extract.
- D1 OPENING ends with DECLARED CHAIN: 3 to 6 numbered links, each with its footing; the last is the conclusion link. Every later round is scored against this list.
- C1 CHALLENGE; D2 RESPONSE.
- C2 CLOSING ends with UNSUPPORTED-L4 PREMISES (at most 6): each line `LINK n:` (or `LINK none:`), the defender's verbatim quote, and `PRESSED WITH:` the verses pressed against it; then `PREMISE COUNT: <n>`.
- D3 LINK ROUND: each premise `LINK n: SUPPORTED` (two prior layers exhibited), `LINK n: NARROWED TO: <limb kept>` (with its footing; the link is judged narrowed) or `LINK n: CONCEDED`. Then CHALLENGER'S UNSUPPORTED-L4 PREMISES in the same form.
- C3 LINK ROUND answers that list the same way.
- D4 DEFENDER CLOSE: the defender's final defense. It is shown C3 and a system list of what is still unanswered: (a) each [DR2] premise's verses that no defender round names; (b) every verse the challenger cited that no later defender round names. It answers each as `ANSWER: <book chapter:verse> -- D<n> --` with the verse quoted and the reason it does not overturn the link; it may keep, narrow or concede a link; it may not add links or press. Its request carries its round 1 DECLARED CHAIN verbatim and the [DR3] C list, both read by code, with the instruction to keep those numbers; the C4 request carries the same numbering instruction.
- C4 CHALLENGER CLOSE: final words on its own position from the case already made. NO NEW PRESS: a verse reference that no earlier challenger round names is a new press, marked NOT COUNTED for the judges and recorded (§9, C4). It answers nothing new.
- BARRED MOVES (both debaters; Init with Guards adds the block to every round prompt): (a) an answer from silence, answering a press from what a verse does not name, state or say; (b) a requirement the verse never sets, asking a verse to state what it never sets out to state and counting its silence against the other side; (c) answering the verse but not the press's MEANS; (d) granting a row and then acting as if it were not granted. A sentence that only denies a requirement the other side imposed is written with `NOT A REQUIREMENT -- rebuts [<round>] "<their words, 6 or more>"` under it. Code finds (a) and (b): a round that carries one is sent back to its seat once (the B nodes of each round, `<round>B Build` to `<round>B Extract`); a sentence that still carries one after that is VOID, struck from the round, the press it answered counts as unanswered, and the offense is named in the run report. The block ends "Repeated offenses will result in disqualification."
  - THE FINDER'S PHRASES (pkgBMH rev 2; one identical change in the 14 B nodes, C1B to C4B and D2B to D4B, Build and Extract): the absence phrase "no verse (row, text, word, witness) states (says, names, denies, excludes)" is also read with "that" or "which" between ("no verse that states"). A heading that names the other side's item ("against C<n>" or "against D<n>") opens an answer block as an ANSWER heading does; the block ends at the next answer heading, which is then read by the rules for answer headings. Inside an "against" block a sentence is harvested only when its absence phrase opens with "no". "I rest on no ..." is a give, like "I rest nothing", and is not harvested. The harvest lists candidates; the later check decides each. Size: code +175 in each node, +2,450; prompt 0. Limits: see section 17.
- C3G, THE LINK-ROUND MEANS CHECK: C3 Extract reads each defender press of [CR3] that the challenger's link round must answer (`press_check`). When the round does not quote a press's MEANS, or quotes it and gives no layer that answers it, C3G Build continues the same seat's conversation once and asks for the whole link round again; C3G Extract keeps the resent round. A passing check, no press harvested or a check error passes the round through unchanged.
- C4G, THE OPPONENT'S GRANTS CHECK: the challenger's close round opens with an OPPONENT'S GRANTS section, every grant of the defender's rounds 2 to 4 as a `G<n> [<round>] "<the grant, quoted>"` line with a SUPPORTS line. C4 Extract checks it against the grants code harvested (`grants_check`); a missing section, a missing G line or a misquoted grant makes C4G Build ask once for the whole close round again, section first. The judges read the section; it moves no count.
  - THE GRANT WORDS (pkgGAV; C4 Build, C4 Extract, C4G Extract, C4B Extract; the same three harvester lines in each): a sentence of the defender's rounds is a grant when it holds "I" followed by one of the grant verbs: grant, concede, withdraw, drop, admit, retract, give up, do not claim, no longer claim, no longer rest. Up to two adverbs from a fixed list may stand between "I" and the verb: also, now, further, readily, freely, fully, plainly, openly, likewise, therefore, thus, hereby, still, so. A line-opening or sentence-opening "Granted" or "Conceded" is a grant as before. Not a grant: a verb, or a list of verbs, followed by "none", "nothing" or "no" ("I grant and concede nothing"); a sentence that ends in a question mark; a sentence opened by If, Unless, Should or Nor with no comma, semicolon or colon before the verb ("If I grant this the chain falls"); a heading "What I <verb>" with up to two words before the verb, for any of the verbs above and for "set aside" and "release", alone or two joined by "and". "Set aside" is not a grant verb.
    - Sizes (measured): code +2,412 (603 per node); the grants block +845 characters in total over 81 stored runs (9 runs gain one grant each, none loses one). Nodes +0, connections +0.
    - Limits: a conditional sentence with no comma before the grant is skipped whole ("If so I grant it."); a dash or a bracket is not taken as the break ("Nor is that all - I also concede ..."). A conditional with a comma before the grant is listed ("If he shows a row, I readily concede the limb."). "I set aside ..." of the writer's own limb is not read. Three adverbs in a row are not read. "I so grant." and reported speech without quotation marks are listed. The verbs accept, agree, yield, release and the forms "I do not rest", "I rest nothing" are not read. The key-word reader (kwLines421) reads the same verb line and also "I accept that" and "I agree that" (section 9, pkgKGL, pkgKAG). A sentence opened by "Granted" or "Conceded" is taken by the grants list and not by the key-word reader.
  - THE GRANTS LIST (pkgSGJ; C4 Build, C4 Extract, C4G Extract, C4B Extract; the same harvester line in each): the grants list given to the challenger before its close and re-read by the three Extract nodes takes each grant as the whole sentence that holds the grant word, from that sentence's start within its line. Leading list marks and bracket tags such as [L4] are left off; a sentence start more than 160 characters before the grant word is cut back at a word. A grant of fewer than seven words is joined only to the next sentence on the same line, never to the next line or bullet. A heading that is only "What I grant" or "What I withdraw" (or both joined by "and") is not a grant. Two grant words in one sentence give one item.
    - Sizes (measured): code +1,660 (415 per node); the grants block given to the challenger about +138 characters per run (+4.3%; 38,599 to 40,250 characters in total over 12 stored runs). Nodes +0, connections +0.
    - Limits: a grant whose sentence starts on an earlier line starts at its own line. A short grant that ends its line stands alone ("I grant it."). A quoted sentence earlier on the line ends the lead early, and an abbreviation stop in the lead ("Matt. 1:20") starts the item after it; each item is still word for word on one line. G lines grow by up to 160 characters and the challenger must quote them word for word, so one more grants resend (C4G) is possible.
- VERSE SHEET: before D2, C2, D3, C3, D4 and C4, the KJV text of every verse either side has cited so far is read from `kjv_verses` in one request (node `<round> Verse Sheet`) and given to that round's seat as stored (bracketed italics and margin notes kept), in order of first citation. A range of more than 12 verses gives its first verse only; the sheet is capped by `SHEET_CAP_VERSES` and `SHEET_CAP_CHARS` (Init with Guards), and every verse left off is listed. Round 1 has no sheet of cited verses; the Oracle seat's round 1 gets the verses its served rows name.
- EXTRACT: empty text fails the round (`VALIDATION: ... returned no text: finish_reason=..; output_tokens=..; reasoning_tokens=..`); a length stop keeps the text and appends `[TRUNCATED: the provider reported the token limit -- raise max_tokens]`. A provider refusal (content filter, refusal field, Anthropic `refusal`, Gemini safety reasons) is a failed call, not a round.
- ROUND HEADING NORMALIZER (pkgRHN, the 17 round Extract nodes): among the first three non-empty lines of a round, a line that names a wrong "Round N" has its number corrected, with a ROUND HEADING NORMALIZED review note, only when the line is a heading. A body sentence is never rewritten. Any line is not a heading when my, our, your, his, its, their, the, this, that, in, of, from, to, at, by, before, after or since stands before "Round". A line that does not begin with "#" is also not a heading when a possessive ("defender's", "sides'") or under, during, as, with, for, on or through stands before "Round". A line that neither begins with "#" nor is bold or underlined whole is also not a heading when it is over 160 characters, has over 40 characters before "Round", holds a sentence break, ends in . : ; ! ?, or carries words after the number that do not begin with a dash, colon, bar, bracket, comma or equals sign, or run past 40 characters. A whole-bold line that ends in . ; ! ? inside the bold is a sentence. A line left alone does not stop the search: a true heading on a later line of the first three is still corrected.
- FAILURE SALVAGE: the error outputs of every round call and Extract, the synthesis, single-judge and diagnostic calls, the Normalizers, Final Output, Extract Diagnostic, Save to DB and Check Save go to Failure Salvage. It saves the rounds produced as a contested row (`framework_verdict` null, `quarantine_reason` and a REVIEW THIS ROW line naming the node) and a RUN INCOMPLETE file in the study folder; Salvage Stop then fails the run. The Fetch nodes' error outputs go to Stop: Call Failed.
- RULES WRITTEN AFTER s82 (each from the package named; where one differs from the text above it in this section, the later rule is right; library line numbers are those of the version the package was written on, so find a line by its text):
  - CLOSE-ROUND BOOK CHECK (pkgBRF rev 4): a bare chapter:verse in a close round is not marked NEW under a borrowed book, and is not dropped.
    - Where: C4P, the close-round check code, in 12 nodes (Build Synthesis, Build Independent Verdict, Build Save Body, Panel Build, Format Markdown, D4 Build, D4 Extract, C4 Build, C4 Extract, C4G Extract, D4B Extract, C4B Extract). It governs the challenger's CLOSE-ROUND PRESS CHECK on [DR4] and the defender's close-round new-verse check on [CR4]. The reference parser is unchanged.
    - What it does: a reference headed by a capitalised word that is not a book ("So 1:18", "Then 1:18") whose chapter and verse an earlier round names under another book is not marked NEW. A separate system line names it: "[CLOSE-ROUND BOOK CHECK -- by code: [DR4] writes 1:18 (Matthew 1:18 named earlier) with no book; not marked NEW, not cleared: read the sentence for its book.]" [CR4] gets the same line. The line is left out when the same reference is already marked NEW. Every other reference is read as before, "and" lists ("Numbers 1:2 and 1:18") and bracketed bare references included.
    - Limits:
      - Code does not decide which book the writer meant. A false flag and a true new verse both go to the book line; the judge reads the sentence.
      - A second bare reference after a first bare one is read by neither check (unchanged from before this package).
      - A bare new verse not named earlier is still printed with the borrowed book.
      - The parser fault remains in other scoped uses, the ledger in the Shared Library among them.
  - CLOSE-ROUND BOOK CHECK, EVERY EARLIER BOOK (pkgBRF rev 5):
    - What it does: the book line names every earlier verse that has the bare reference's chapter and verse, not only the first: "1:18 (Numbers 1:18, Matthew 1:18 named earlier)". The names stand in the order of first naming across the earlier rounds; a name repeated in two rounds is listed once; a bare range gives each verse's names in verse order. Where one earlier book names the verse, the line is as under rev 4.
    - Where: C4P, the close-round check code, in the same 12 nodes (two expressions: the earlier names are kept as a list per chapter and verse, and the line joins every name). The Shared Library is not touched.
    - Limits:
      - An earlier round's own bare reference under a borrowed book is still listed as named earlier, beside the true name; code does not decide which book the writer meant.
      - The line has no cap: a wide bare range that two earlier books cover names every verse of each book (a planted case: the press note grows from 435 to 1,179 characters). Not seen in a stored debate.
      - A second bare reference after "And", and a bare reference after a bracket, are read by neither check, as before.
  - CLOSE-ROUND BOOK CHECK, EVERY REFERENCE WITH NO BOOK OF ITS OWN (pkgBRS):
    - Where: C4P.refs, in the same 12 nodes. One expression: a reference is bare when it has no book of its own.
    - What it does: every reference written without its own book, and not only one headed by a capitalised word, goes to the book line when an earlier round names its chapter and verse under any book: "1:18 (Matthew 1:18, Numbers 1:18 named earlier)". This covers a bare reference that opens a sentence, where the book came from the sentence before.
    - Still marked NEW: a reference with its book written out; a bare reference whose chapter and verse no earlier round names; the comma forms "Matthew 1:18, 1:20" and "Matthew 1:18, 20", which carry the book.
    - Size: code -10 in each of the 12 nodes; prompt 0.
  - THE CLOSE REQUEST LISTS THE SIDE'S OWN RECORD (pkgOWR; D4 Build and C4 Build):
    - Each close request carries a block headed "YOUR OWN RECORD", placed just before "CLOSE ROUND --" and computed by code from the transcript. It says the close may keep these or go further and may not deny any of them, or restore what they narrowed, conceded, withdrew or dropped.
    - Listed, word for word with the round: every line of the side's rounds 1 to 3 that begins "LINK n" or "D<n>" / "C<n>" (an optional bracket or parenthesis after it), then a colon and NARROWED TO, CONCEDED, WITHDRAWN or DROPPED, cut at 260 characters; and every grant sentence the grants finder (the harvester of THE GRANTS LIST) takes from those rounds. For this block only, the finder also takes a sentence holding "is" or "are", an optional adverb (readily, freely, fully, plainly, also, therefore, thus, so), then "granted" or "conceded" (pkgOWR rev 3; "was" and "were" are not read). Such a sentence is skipped when its lead begins with nothing, none, no, neither, nor or what; when its lead holds where, if, unless, once, when, whether, until, after, since, because, though or although; when "by the defender", "by the challenger", "by the other side" or "by the opponent" follows the word; or when its lead already holds an "I grant" form (grant, concede, withdraw, drop, admit, retract). When the lead begins with that, this, it, these, those, both, each or which, the sentence before it is carried in front, cut at 200 characters, and the entry is cut at 420 characters in place of 260. THE GRANTS LIST and the close extract checks do not use the wider form. Size of rev 3 (measured): D4 Build +1,028, C4 Build +1,028; on 94 stored debates 55 entries are added and none lost, +5,629 prompt characters over 26 close requests (at most 10 entries and 1,517 characters in one request).
    - Sources: the defender's [CR1] to [CR3] from D1 Extract, D2B Extract and D3B Extract; the challenger's [DR1] to [DR3] from C1B Extract, C2B Extract and C3B Extract. D4 Build holds its own copy of the grants finder.
    - With nothing to list, or on an error, no block is sent and the request is as before.
    - Size: code +4,541 (D4 Build +3,415, C4 Build +1,126); prompt 0 to +4,327 characters per close request on the stored runs replayed.
  - CLOSE PROMPT STATES THE CONTRADICTION RULE (pkgCPR; D4 Build and C4 Build):
    - Text:
      - Purpose: the debater is told the ruling environment in the close round.
      - Change: D4 Build (defender close) and C4 Build (challenger close) append the same fixed text to the close-round instruction:
      - "Narrowing or conceding carries no conduct penalty: it is weighed on the merits, and a part left out of a narrowed restatement counts as dropped. A close sentence that denies what your earlier rounds granted, or restores a link or a part you narrowed, conceded or dropped, contradicts your own record: your chain is broken by default, the system sends no request to correct it, and a later withdrawal does not undo it."
      - Sizes (measured): code +836 (418 per node); prompt +418 characters per close request. D4B Build, C4B Build and C4G Build carry the close request, so each adds +418 when it fires. System prompts unchanged, so system-prompt caches are kept. Nodes +0, connections +0.
      - Limits: The engine breaks the contradicted link, which breaks the defender's chain but for the challenger loses that link's collisions. "Broken" applies to contradictions the Close Enforcer confirms or the judges find. A barred-move or grants resend rewrites the whole close, and the Close Enforcer reads only the kept version, so a contradiction made in a first version and gone from the rewrite is not caught. "no request to correct it" refers to contradictions only; the barred-moves resend (D4B, C4B) still asks for a rewritten round when a barred move is found. The text informs the debater only; enforcement is unchanged. Its effect on debater conduct is not measured.
      - Proof: a debater-input change is proven only by debater output. Either a paid replay of the stored D4 and C4 close requests (for example run 35067) with the new text, compared with the stored closes, or the next run's closes graded for contradictions and drops. A live run alone does not isolate the effect.
    - The two sentences are added to both close prompts. They follow "narrow it ... do not add links or re-seat the chain." in D4 Build and "this round answers nothing new." in C4 Build.
    - The close text is a literal in D4 Build and in C4 Build, not in Shared Library and not in a table; the resends copy the body (D4B Build from D4 Build; C4B Build and C4G Build from C4 Build).
    - Size: code +836 (418 per node), prompt +418 characters per close request; D4B Build, C4B Build and C4G Build read the close request, so each adds +418 when it fires; system prompts unchanged, so system caches are kept; nodes +0, connections +0, settings equal. The code total of s139 is 2,928,162.
    - Unproven. A debater-input change is proven only by debater output, by a paid replay of the stored D4 and C4 close requests (for example 35067) compared with the stored closes, which needs the operator's word, or by the next run's closes graded for contradictions and drops; a live run alone does not isolate the effect. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 34).

## 7. Synthesis, single judge, diagnostic
- THE SINGLE JUDGE IS SWITCHED OFF (pkgSJO; node SJ Off): the single judge (second opinion) does not run. The synthesis still runs, on the seat named single_judge in Init Seats; that seat must stay named.
  - The node SJ Off stands on the main line between "Regrade After Quotes?" (output 1) and Build Save Body. It hands Build Save Body the debate item of Final Output (topic, the eight rounds, synthesis, guards, date) with no single-judge field.
  - The nodes from Build Independent Verdict to Extract Diagnostic (the verdict call, its re-ask, its cure round and the diagnostic call) keep their code, receive no input and do not run. Every rule in this manual about the single verdict, its re-ask, its cure and the diagnostic describes those nodes and is not in effect while SJ Off stands.
  - The stored row holds: `independent_verdict_raw` null; `judge_model` null; both chain marks null; out-of-round concessions and detail null; `data_class` "contested" in every run (only the single judge's clean reading set "study"); synthesis without the diagnostic block; no SINGLE JUDGE line in the review notes. The standings and counts come from the panel.
  - The panel, the enforcers, the gate and the alternates are unchanged. Where they read the single judge (the same-model comparison in the three Payloads nodes, the closing-contradiction candidates of Judge Contradiction Build, Assume Check Record, the enforcer log merge) they find none and add nothing.
- The single judge is the first engine of the judge order (§10) that is not seated, not time-removed and not of a debater's `FAM_PANEL` family; it may also sit on the panel. It writes the synthesis, gives the single verdict and writes the diagnostic, each built for its family and routed (Synthesis / Verdict / Diagnostic Judge Route -> Call -> Normalizer).
- SYNTHESIS: what held, both link rounds, the close rounds, drift-guard violations (Oracle seat only), how each side engaged; no verdict. A point either side's list contests is listed under the concessions item as unresolved. The synthesis is not shown to the judges. Build Save Body checks it (§9, S-A to S-H).
- SINGLE JUDGE: the judges' rulebook (§8) with the line output form; its text, cure lines and review lines are stored in `independent_verdict_raw`. It is a SECOND OPINION: its grades do not enter `framework_verdict` or the standings columns. It passes through the enforcers (§9).
- DIAGNOSTIC: on a BROKEN single verdict a layer-break diagnostic over all rounds; otherwise a brief confirmation from the verdict and synthesis. Appended to `synthesis` after `--- ORACLE_DEBATE_V1 BROKEN DIAGNOSTIC ---` or `--- ORACLE_DEBATE_V1 CONFIRMATION ---`.
- RULES WRITTEN AFTER s82 (each from the package named; where one differs from the text above it in this section, the later rule is right; library line numbers are those of the version the package was written on, so find a line by its text):
  - SINGLE-JUDGE RE-ASK: when the single judge's reply has no verdict, or a chain-mark line is absent under ODV1-A2, an OpenRouter single judge is asked once more in its own conversation (SJ Re-ask? -> SJ Re-ask Build -> SJ Re-ask Call openrouter -> SJ Re-ask Pick -> Verdict Normalizer -> Extract Independent Verdict). With no verdict, it rewrites the complete judgment and the re-ask reply is read. With chain marks absent, it writes only those lines, and they are added to the first reply. A failed or empty re-ask leaves the first reply. Cure Check records an AUDIT v36 SJR line (seat, model, what was missing, the outcome) in review_notes.
  - SINGLE JUDGE'S FULL RE-ASK ALSO COVERS A JUDGMENT WITH NO COLLISION POINTS SECTION (pkgSJF; SJ Re-ask? and SJ Re-ask Build):
    - Text:
      - Nodes: SJ Re-ask? (If) and SJ Re-ask Build (Code). No other node, no connection, no library line changes.
      - What it does:
      - The single judge's first Verdict is read by Extract Independent Verdict. When that judgment has no COLLISION POINTS section, the extractor writes the audit line "COLLISION MAP: NO COLLISION POINTS SECTION" into independent_verdict_raw.
      - On the single judge (jidx 5) under audit prompt ODV1-A2:
        - SJ Re-ask? also fires when that audit line is present.
        - SJ Re-ask Build adds "no COLLISION POINTS section" to the miss list and uses FULL mode: the judge is asked to write the whole judgment again. The fixed ask text is unchanged.
        - SJ Re-ask Pick, unchanged, takes the re-ask reply whole in FULL mode. The re-ask still runs at most once per run.
      - Forms it catches:
        - A verdict-word decline (for example "Provisional result: BROKEN ..." with no rulings).
        - A judgment wrapped in JSON ({"reasoning": ..., "verdict": ...}, plain or fenced). The verdict reader does not unwrap it, so its collision points are not read.
        - A collision heading the extractor does not read, such as "4. COLLISION POINTS:".
      - Forms it leaves alone:
        - A full judgment, and a partial judgment with a LIMITATION paragraph that still has COLLISION POINTS.
        - "## COLLISION POINTS", "**COLLISION POINTS:**" and "COLLISION POINTS: NONE": the extractor reads these headings, so the audit line is not written.
        - Panel judges (jidx other than 5).
      - Limits:
        - If the judge declines the FULL re-ask too, the record holds the second reply whole and can lose its chain marks. One call is spent either way.
        - A numbered heading ("4. COLLISION POINTS:") on an otherwise full judgment fires a FULL re-ask. Today the collision points of such a judgment are not read either.
        - It reads the audit line's exact wording. If the extractor's wording changes, this package stops firing.
      - Size (MEASURED against s136):
      - SJ Re-ask Build +184 code characters; SJ Re-ask? expression +90 characters. Prompt 0 fixed. When it fires, the miss list carries "no COLLISION POINTS section" (27 characters), and the FULL ask replaces the MARKS ask.
    - Why: a judge that declines the full form but names a verdict word got the MARKS re-ask and the record kept no collision points from that judge. WALL-4910
    - Size (measured): SJ Re-ask Build +184 code, SJ Re-ask? expression +90 characters, prompt 0 fixed, 418 nodes, connections equal, no library line; code total 2,927,142 to 2,927,326. When it fires, the miss list gains 27 characters and the FULL ask (562 characters) replaces the MARKS ask (476). One terra call, which pkgSJR already allows, about $0.045 and about 110 s (an estimate from the billed rates and times of 35067, not measured).
    - Unproven: the paid proof, a re-ask on 35067 (the replay conversation plus the FULL ask, about $0.05, an estimate), has not been run. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 34).
  - SAVED SYNTHESIS LABEL FOR A CONFIRMED CLOSING CONTRADICTION (pkgSYL; Build Save Body only): the synthesis is built before the closing enforcer runs, so a link broken by a confirmed closing contradiction could be labelled NARROWED in the saved synthesis.
    - What the code does: Build Save Body reads $('Close Enforcer Record').first().json.cec.confirmed (one new literal read) and saves saveBody.synthesis as __SYL(d.synthesis). For each confirmed closing contradiction it adds one line to the synthesis saved in the debate row.
    - If the synthesis has a table row whose first cell starts with the link token (for example "| **D3: ..."), a row is inserted directly under it: "| D3 (engine) | **BROKEN** | confirmed closing contradiction (Xn): earlier [round] \"...\"; closing [round] \"...\". Added by code after the closing check. |". The token is the link the engine itself names (side and link copied from each confirmed row of Close Enforcer Record), so the label reports the engine's link and does not move any NARROWED mark the model wrote.
    - If the synthesis has no such row, the line "**ENGINE: D3 BROKEN** -- ..." is added at the end of the synthesis, before any "--- ORACLE_DEBATE_V1 ..." marker.
    - Pipes and newlines in a quoted text are cleaned (a pipe becomes "/", a newline a space).
    - If Close Enforcer Record did not run or confirmed nothing, the synthesis is saved unchanged (byte-equal text). The audit checks of Build Save Body (S-A to S-H, section 9) still read the synthesis as the model wrote it.
    - Who reads the saved synthesis: no model reads the added line. Build Diagnostic Prompt takes its synthesis from Cure Check, upstream of the save; Panel and Alternate Payloads read only verdict fields of saveBody; on a regrade, Regrade Load reads the saved synthesis only to check that it is not empty. Format Markdown prints the added line, and readers outside the workflow (the database row, the scoreboard, the dashboard) see it as written.
    - Size: Build Save Body only; +895 code, all code 3,246,124 to 3,247,019, measured from the s125 and s126 fetch-backs; connections equal. Fixed prompt 0. Most of the size is the label text and the fallback for a synthesis without a link table.
    - Shown offline on stored runs: of 10 stored runs, 33149, 33371, 33490 and 33497 are equal; 34299, 34626, 34703 and 34780 do not reach Build Save Body; 34852 and 34726 gain one line each and no line is removed. In 34852, with the live Close Enforcer Record rerun, X2 is confirmed on D2 and the engine row sits under the D2 row; in 34726, X1 is confirmed on D1 and, with no link table in that synthesis, the fallback line is placed before the ORACLE_DEBATE_V1 marker. Plants (none confirmed, Record not run, D1, D3, C2, a D10 row, a six-column table, a link given as a string) came out as expected.
    - Limits: two confirmations on one link come out in reverse order (X5 above X2); the first line beginning "| D3" takes the row even if it is not in the table (not seen in stored text); the row goes under the FIRST table row that starts with the link token, so an earlier table whose rows begin "| D3 vs C1 |" would take it (no stored synthesis has such a row; of the 6 that reach the save only 34852 and 34726 have link rows, and none starts with "Dn vs"); under a two-column table the reason falls in a third cell, which Markdown viewers drop (no stored link table has two columns); an entry with no side or link would print "Dundefined" or "NaN", which cannot occur on live because Close Enforcer Record copies side and link from each confirmed row. Each limit is a misplaced or cosmetic line, never a changed verdict.
    - Reader note: in 34852 the quoted drop in the engine row is the one the synthesis marks NARROWED under D3, while the engine row sits under D2, because pkgCLK rev 3 names the link of the closing sentence's block. The label does not move the D3 NARROWED mark.
  - SINGLE JUDGE'S CURED K-W AND K-S3 FLAGS STORED AS CURED IN review_notes (pkgSJK; Build Save Body only):
    - Text:
      - Node: Build Save Body only. Prompt: none.
      - What it does:
      - Build Save Body copies the single judge's REVIEW THIS ROW flags into review_notes. A flag is stored with "CURED by the single judge's cure reply; recorded, not for review." when its key is in the set read from that judge's CURED rule reports (AUDIT v21 DEBATE RULE VIOLATION REPORT).
        - K-W key: the item "<SIDE ROUND ...> #N" before the quotation, matched to the report ITEM (same key as the export, pkgRVF).
        - K-S3 key: side and round plus the first 40 letters and digits of the conceded sentence, matched to the report's LINE field (same as pkgRVF).
        - The report reader now also reads a report whose ITEM holds a closed bracket pair ("LINK 6 [premise 1] #5"). It tries that form first, else the old form, so every report the old reader read is still read; an ITEM with a lone "[" is still read.
      - Limits:
        - Two K-S3 flags of one single judge whose sentences share the first 40 letters and digits share one key (as in pkgRVF).
        - Reports other than AUDIT v21 are not read (all stored K-W and K-S3 reports are v21).
        - Record and display only: review_notes is shown by the dashboard and appended to by derive_debate_outcome and Failure Salvage; no grade, count or verdict reads these flags.
      - Size:
      - +586 code characters (measured); prompt 0.
    - Size: Build Save Body only; +586 code (code 2,926,972 to 2,927,558); fixed prompt 0. With pkgNBR rev 2 the two writes add +790 against s133.
    - Unproven until two clean runs; not yet run on the n8n runner. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 32).
  - DEAD-FUNCTION TRIM (pkgDCT; code only):
    - What it does: removes code that no live path calls. Across 95 of the 176 Code nodes it deletes whole function declarations, unused variable declarations and object properties, and their entries in returned lists.
      - The removed functions were found with acorn: a name is dead when nothing reachable reads it.
      - The Shared Library stub nodes (42 nodes) are rebuilt from their trimmed sources by gate/pack.cjs. 58 plain Code nodes are trimmed in place.
      - 79 workflow nodes are changed, in jsCode only.
      - No prompt text, node, connection or setting changes. Nothing that ran before stops running. Every removed value is free of side effects.
    - Size: code goes from 3,247,019 to 2,921,162, a cut of 325,857 characters. Prompt change is 0.
    - Limits: (1) Some changed nodes have no clean stored replay and rest on the static and AST checks alone: 17 nodes, of which 14 never ran (Accept Return Build, Accept DS Return Build, Reserve Build, Failure Salvage and the ten R2 nodes) and 3 error in both versions on every stored export (Init with Guards, Accept Build, Accept Final), because those runs predate the argon seat. (2) It was not run on the n8n runner before the write; the first run on s127 is the first live test of the trimmed code. (3) Run time is not measured. (4) The AST proof assumes no code reads a function's own text (toString) or inspects its scope; no such use was found. (5) Not proven on a live run; like every change since s91, it needs two clean runs.
    - For readers of the code: function names that appear in older Wall posts or older manual sections may no longer exist in the live code. For example, Build Save Body lost note, U, noteD, score, noteR, noteL and noteP. For any line cited from s126 or earlier, find the line by its text in the s127 fetch-back.
    - The s127 fetch-back has 418 nodes and equals the s126 version except in jsCode of 79 nodes; connections and settings are equal; all code is 2,921,162. The counts in the text above differ by what they count: 79 workflow nodes with changed jsCode, 95 Code nodes with a changed effective source (stubs read the Shared Library), 94 nodes parsed (static check).

## 8. The judges' rulebook
The single judge (Build Independent Verdict) and every panel, reserve and alternate seat (Panel Build; Reserve Build and Alternate Build reuse its prompt) read the same rulebook. Only the output form differs: the panel answers strict JSON `{verdict_tag, defense_standing, challenge_standing, conceded_links, challenger_conceded_links, counted_premises_*, reasoning}` with the worked lines inside `reasoning`; after a cure or change rewrites a count line, the stored counted_premises_* arrays are derived from the final count lines (CPD, record-only). Format Markdown carries a reader's copy of the rules.
- THE AGREEMENT: before any judge reads the debate, every seat that may judge (the judge order less the debaters and the time-removed seat) is sent the posture alone (Accept Build): Commitment 1 the record is the only evidence; 2 I repair nothing; 3 meaning, not words; 4 one standard for both sides; 5 silence is not neutral; 6 I void only by quoting; 7 I judge, I do not only count; 8 the method is Isaiah 28:10; 9 I answer in the form each request gives (judgment as strict JSON; a cure or send-back answered only in its plain lines, every item answered); 10 I capture contradictions (CONTRADICTION, NOT HELD, CONTRADICTIONS: NONE FOUND, and for each K-W item STANDS WITHOUT IT because <layer>, CONCEDED, or REASSERTED IN <round> "<quote>"); 11 I show every ruling that decides (the five-line ruling card RULING, CLAIM, NAMED BY, RULE, APPLIED for each counted premise, each collision graded broken or withdrawn, and the keystone grade); 12 I judge by the 1611 KJV alone; 13 I give the layers of every break I grade ([L1] quoted, [L2] when the break turns on a word's meaning, [L3] when relied on, after GROUND:); 14 I flag every requirement a debater imposes on scripture (an `IMPOSED:` line for each sentence resting on a verse failing to state, name or say something: NO ROW SETS IT, SET BY [L1] <reference> "<words>", or NOT A REQUIREMENT -- rebuts ...); 15 my keystone does not stand on a premise I count unexhibited or conceded. After Commitment 15 the text states that a sentence the system marks VOID under the barred moves (§6) is struck from its round, the press it answered counts as unanswered and no ANSWER CHECK grades it MEETS, and ends "Repeated offenses will result in disqualification." Accept Check requires every heading; the number of headings is the number in `PART2`. It replies with each heading verbatim, one line in its own words, and the line I ACCEPT THIS POSTURE AND WILL BE HELD TO IT.; one return is allowed (Accept Check, Accept Final). A seat whose final status is not ACCEPT, HEDGE or their on-return forms does not judge (Init Seats drops it). The accepted exchange heads that seat's judging conversation, and a cure quotes back the seat's own line for the commitment the flagged rule breaks. The commitments' text is `PART2` in Accept Build and is identical in every node that carries it.
- COMMITMENT 3, THE READING RULE: a link that cannot agree with the meaning of a verse pressed against it, as the debaters show that meaning with its layers, fails; the verse stands. The wording of one verse alone never decides a link, and no side may demand that one verse state the point. "Scripture, read precept upon precept, does not give [the fact]" is a ground when it names the fact and exhibits the scripture that does speak to it, one verse or many read together; no count of verses decides it (md_notes_rulings 421, 422).
- TRANSCRIPT: `[CR1] [DR1] [CR2] [DR2] [CR3] [DR3] [CR4] [DR4]`; CR = defender, DR = challenger. Under [DR4] the system lists any new press. Also given: a pre-check of each listed premise's charge words absent from its quote (decides nothing), the pressed-witness ledger computed by code, the KJV citation report, and the per-seat citation lists.
- THE PRESSED-WITNESS LEDGER lists each pressed witness and whether the answering side NAMED IT IN AN ANSWER: a later round of that side names the verse reference, or names the press by its id (`P3.1`), including a joint header `MEANS [CR3] P3.1, P3.2 and P3.3: "<MEANS>"` that answers each listed id. Only answer sections count, not any mention: headings such as "Presses not answered", "none" or "nothing" are not answers. A judge grading "unanswered" a witness the ledger shows NAMED IN AN ANSWER must say why that answer fails.
- CHAIN SCOPE (Isaiah 28:10): judge whether the defender's chain survived as a chain. No single verse is required to state a link or its contrary, for either side; "no single verse states it" is never a ground. An [L4] floats only when its block exhibits fewer than two prior layers; a judge naming one names the layers shown and the layer lacking. An LXX reading is a citation error for either side.
- COUNTING RULE: counts come only from the link rounds [CR3] and [DR3]. An ANSWER to a pressed witness is read from any later round of the side that holds the link.
- UNANSWERED WITNESS (md_notes_rulings 373): a verse the other side quoted against a link or listed premise (against the defender: [DR1], [DR2]; against the challenger: [CR1], [CR2], [CR3]) is ANSWERED when a later round of the holding side (defender: [CR2], [CR3], [CR4]; challenger: [DR1], [DR2], [DR3], the challenger's round 1 included) quotes it or names it by reference and shows from the text why it does not overturn the link. Re-citing the link's own footing is not an answer.
- AN UNANSWERED PRESSED WITNESS (md_notes_rulings 372): the premise is UNEXHIBITED and counts toward CONCEDED LINKS and the standings. A non-response is never neutral. Why: a side could otherwise win a round by not responding.
- ANSWER CHECK (md_notes_rulings 412): for every verse the ledger lists under a premise of either list, one line `ANSWER CHECK: <ref> -- [<round>] -- MEANS: ".." -- ANSWER: ".." -- MEETS | MISSES | FAILS -- ".."`; an answer that MISSES or FAILS leaves the witness unanswered.
- UNEXHIBITED BY ASSUMPTION: a link is UNEXHIBITED, whatever its label, when it ASSUMES as given a fact about a person, house, time or act that no quoted row gives, or when a narrowed link keeps words that still assert what its side gave up; written `STEP: .. -- ASSUMES: .. -- ROWS GIVE: ..`. What a link infers from its rows is its [L4], graded by the two-layer rule and the meaning of the pressed verses, never as a missing row (DG-61).
- GLOSS CHECK (code, served to every judge): each lexicon meaning a debater quotes is set against the record's entry for its Strong's number: GLOSS TRIMMED (a listed entry with senses left out) and GLOSS ADDED (a gloss given beside a Strong's number carrying words the entry's definition and renderings do not hold), both sides alike.
- CITED-IN RULING: the ledger's "cited in" line lists, for each side, the verses the other side cited that no later round of the holding side names. Every judge rules on every listed verse, one line each: `PRESSED: <book chapter:verse> -- [<pressing round>] -- "<the pressing sentence, quoted>" -- UNANSWERED`, `PRESSED: ... -- ANSWERED IN [<round>]`, or `NOT PRESSED: <book chapter:verse>`. A verse ruled PRESSED and UNANSWERED counts under ruling 372 and is named in that side's CONCEDED LINKS line (enforcer K-G9, §9).
- WHO IS BOUND (md_notes_rulings 382, 383): only the Debate Oracle seat is bound by the drift guards. The judges grade the text presented to them alone; no judge prompt, enforcer or cure carries the record's guards or states what the record holds.
- VERSE CITATION (md_notes_rulings 376, ruling 3): a verse counts as evidence, footing, witness or answer only with book, chapter and verse stated and the words quoted are exactly the words of the KJV; a quotation may be part of a verse (ruling 3). A hyphen inside a word counts as a mismatch while `HYPHEN_JOIN` is off (§12). An answer in substance without the reference does not answer a pressed witness. A quotation the citation report marks NOT COUNTED is not counted.
- CLOSE ROUNDS (md_notes_rulings 377): [CR4] answers witnesses still pressed against the defender and presses nothing; a verse it names against the challenger is not a pressed witness. A verse [CR4] names that no earlier round of either side names is NEW IN CLOSE, NOT COUNTED. [DR4] answers nothing and may not press; a verse it names that no earlier challenger round names is a new press, NOT COUNTED. A sentence in either close that grades or presses the other side's links is disregarded. Both closes keep the D numbers of the DECLARED CHAIN in [CR1] and the C numbers of the [DR3] LINK blocks; a numbering made in a close does not govern. The system lists each of these under the close it found them in (§9, close-round checks). [DR4] is structurally last: the defender's silence after it is procedural, never a challenger concession.
- NARROWING: a block that drops a limb and keeps the link narrowed is not a concession; the narrowed link is graded. A limb the link round narrows counts in its narrowed form.
- VOID: a premise listed without the opponent's verbatim quote, or charged beyond it (a negation, a universal, or a claim the quote does not make), is VOID and counts for neither side. Dropping, reordering or paraphrasing the quote is not stronger. A verse under PRESSED WITH: is a witness, never part of the charge. The quote is checked against every earlier round of the opponent. A charge written as ASSUMES: <fact> -- ROWS GIVE: <what the rows give> is never voided for its words; it is checked against the rows (md_notes_rulings 421).
- UNCHALLENGED: a link or witness the other side never challenged stands for the side that presented it. A claim restated in a side's own link round is graded at the layer and footing shown there.
- TERMS: D1..Dn and the keystone Dn come from the DECLARED CHAIN in [CR1] only; a later re-declaration never changes them. A challenger premise `LINK i:` is graded against Di; `LINK none:` is judged but is outside n. w = declared links the defender's [CR3] states as withdrawn or conceded. k = links graded DEFENDER LINK BROKEN.
- VERDICT DEFINITIONS, first match wins: BROKEN-1 k >= 1; BROKEN-2 2w > n, or the keystone WITHDRAWN; PARTIAL-1 w >= 1; PARTIAL-2 DEFENSE STANDING COLLAPSED; HELD-1 none of these.
- RESULT line: DEFENDER WINS | CHALLENGER WINS | UNDERDETERMINED. BROKEN gives CHALLENGER WINS. HELD gives DEFENDER WINS only if the challenge is not SELF-SUPPORTED or a collision is graded CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN; every other case gives UNDERDETERMINED. A RESULT naming a winner when no collision line is graded BROKEN for either side and the two standings and counts are equal is rewritten UNDERDETERMINED by code after the cure (KB-0030, a tie is not a HELD; §9).
- STANDINGS, per side, decided by their own definitions only: SELF-SUPPORTED requires a count of 0 for that side; PROP-DEPENDENT when the count is 1 or more; COLLAPSED when 2c > L (L = premises the other side listed against this side; c = those conceded or unexhibited; a tie is not COLLAPSED). A side's own outgoing list never enters its own L or c. FORFEIT: an empty list against a side gives L = 0, c = 0 and the line LINK ROUND FORFEITED. Each standing line carries `-- L=<L> c=<c> --`. A standing or count is never chosen to agree with another line.
- CONCEDED LINKS: itemized, each tagged CONCEDED (stated) (the block surrenders the link itself) or UNEXHIBITED (no footing offered, or a pressed witness left unanswered). A block exhibiting two prior layers and the chain is SUPPORTED, even when no verse states the conclusion.
- COLLISION POINTS: one line per declared link. `D<i>: WITHDRAWN -- GROUND: ..`; `D<i> vs C<j> (<verse>): DEFENDER LINK BROKEN | CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN | UNDERDETERMINED -- .. -- GROUND: ..`; `D<i>: STANDS -- GROUND: ..`. GROUND: every collision line, whatever its grade, ends with GROUND and one sentence naming the reason; a line without GROUND is incomplete. A STANDS or UNDERDETERMINED line against an exhibited witness names the round that answered it. BROKEN when the link cannot agree with the meaning of the witnesses pressed against it, read together and shown with their layers, and the broken side shows no fit, whether or not it answered them; a FORCED line carries that grade: `FORCED: D<i> vs C<j> -- MEANS [<round>]: ".." -- ANSWER [<round>]: ".." -- FAILS: ".."`, each quotation standing in the round it names (ruling 411). A proven press left unanswered by the broken side's later rounds is also a BROKEN ground. UNDERDETERMINED only when the witnesses and the answers, read together, still leave the point open; an answer that names a witness without showing a fit does not (md_notes_rulings 419). A judge's ground that the quoted words exclude or deny something is the judge's own [L4] and needs two exhibited lower layers.
- DEBATE RULE VIOLATION (rule B, the judge's own BROKEN lines): every LINK BROKEN line exhibits, on the line or the line under it, two lower layers of different kinds: [L1] with the KJV words quoted; [L2] with the source word in its script, with transliteration marks, with a Strong's number, or in plain letters with its English meaning beside it, the tag directly before the word (or directly after it and its meaning); [L3] with the cross-reference named. Two quotations at one layer are one layer.
- Mandatory lines: verdict word, RESULT, CLAUSE (n, w, k), KEYSTONE (`D<n> -- WITHDRAWN | BROKEN | STANDS`, n equal to the CLAUSE n), DEFENSE STANDING, CONCEDED LINKS, CHALLENGE STANDING, CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS, COLLISION POINTS, CITATION CHECK per seat, OUT-OF-ROUND CONCESSIONS (ground granted in [CR1], [DR1], [CR2], [DR2], [CR4] or [DR4]; recorded, never summed with the link-round counts), DEFENDER CHAIN MARK and CHALLENGER CHAIN MARK (FOOTED | STRAINED | SLACK; descriptive only, never a verdict).
- CITATION CHECK, PER SEAT: a label a side cites that is in neither its served nor its named list is a citation error and carries no weight. The system counts served labels cited and unserved labels cited from the raw rounds.
- `rulebook:'draft'` (dry regrade only) splices the contested-exclusivity, supplied-words and let-stand wording into the panel prompt. `chain` is accepted by Init Seats and read by no node.
- RULES WRITTEN AFTER s82 (each from the package named; where one differs from the text above it in this section, the later rule is right; library line numbers are those of the version the package was written on, so find a line by its text):
  - CONTRADICTION IN THE CLOSING ROUND (the perjury rule; pkgCEC):
    - A debater that states in its closing round something that cannot be true together with something it stated in an earlier round of its own has contradicted itself.
    - Once the closing enforcer confirms it, the chain link the contradiction belongs to is BROKEN by default, for every judge, whatever grade a judge gave that link.
      - THE CLOSING BREAK KEEPS THE OTHER SIDE'S BREAK (pkgCMX; Shared Library):
        - Text:
            - The code sets the break on every collision line that names the broken link, except a line that already carries the OTHER side's break. That covers both a break the judge graded (DEFENDER LINK BROKEN or CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN) and a break that the other side's confirmed contradiction set. Such a line keeps its grade, and the recompute records "<side> break not set: the line already carries the <other side> break".
            - When no line takes the break, it is counted once as "<side> LINK BROKEN" ("no collision line names it; counted as one ..."), so both sides' breaks enter the counts, the standings and the RESULT.
            - Why: a challenger contradiction could otherwise erase a defender break by meaning and turn BROKEN into HELD. WALL-4818
            - The gate's reading of collision lines (gateAnsOpen) follows the same rule.
          - Size: Shared Library +395 (lines 1323, 1484, 1671); prompt 0.
          - Limits:
            - The count line reads "no collision line names it" even when a line names the link but carries the other side's break; the "not set" line beside it says which.
            - A judge's keystone reason text is not rewritten when a non-keystone link breaks (record-only; WALL-4808).
          - First-run check: on a run with a confirmed closing contradiction, no recompute line reads "(CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN to DEFENDER LINK BROKEN)" or "(DEFENDER LINK BROKEN to CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN)"; each such line reads "break not set". No Panel Cure Check, Cure Check, Gate or Alternate Merge error.
        - Size: Shared Library only; code +395; prompt 0. The s132 write as a whole is +558 code (163 pkgNVR + 395 pkgCMX).
        - Unproven until two clean runs; not yet run on the n8n runner. The first-run check is the last paragraph of the text above and is in the grade plan (section 29).
    - A withdrawal, concession or narrowing written after the contradiction does not undo it.
    - Withdrawals, concessions and narrowings made before it are read under the rules that already govern them.
    - A concession or a narrowing made in the closing round is not a contradiction: it is read as a concession (K-S3) or a narrowing.
    - A closing sentence that asserts again, as the side's own, what the same side withdrew, dropped or gave up earlier is a contradiction.
    - A contradiction the enforcer does not confirm has no effect beyond what the rules already give.
  - COMMITMENT 9, last sentence (pkgQRC): a contradiction question quotes the earlier and the closing passage and asks how they agree; the answer cannot withdraw either; the closing enforcer confirms.
- THE JUDGE IS TOLD THAT A STEP BORROWED FROM ITS SIDE'S OWN CONTESTED LINKS CARRIES NOTHING (pkgJBC rev 2; nodes Panel Build and Build Independent Verdict; ASK 262):
  - Why: in run 36968 terra and sol kept premises 5 and 6 spared on the judge's own rule "SELF-SUPPORTED = ... (at least two prior layers exhibited; a shown chain counts equally)". The pkgBCL sentence sits only in LIB 3204, which only the Judge Enforcer Builds read.
  - What it does: in each node (1) the layer sentence reads "... a shown chain counts equally, save a step borrowed from its own side's contested links, which carries nothing (pkgBCL))."; (2) "shows the chain from them to the link is SUPPORTED" reads "shows the chain from them to the link, with no borrowed step, is SUPPORTED" (2 places); (3) after "...which is never applied." (2 places, the defender and the challenger UNEXHIBITED paragraphs) the borrowed-footing rule is added with the count form "Premise <N>: UNEXHIBITED ([L4] footing borrowed from contested Links <n>)", the step quoted after it, NARROWED or not; not a missing-row charge (DG-61). Nothing deleted.
  - Size: Panel Build 147,168 to 148,811; Build Independent Verdict 121,761 to 123,404 (+1,643 each).
  - Shown offline: of 24 stored judge-premise pairs, 6 are already COUNTED and 18 would flip (defender 11, challenger 7), assuming no borrowed link was granted.
  - Shown in run 37000: judges wrote the count unprompted: defender premise 6 (sol, chatgpt; luna after send-back S8) and challenger premise 3 (all three); stored defender count 1 per judge. The operator ruled that Oracle premise 3, whose step names Link 1 and lists its verses inline, stays as written.
  - Limits: symmetric by ruling; the count met K-UNX-A and pkgRCT (see pkgBFX).
- THE JUDGE IS TOLD THAT THE NUMBERED LIST IN [DR1] IS NOT THE COUNT LIST (pkgCLN, part of pkgEPC rev 2; nodes Panel Build and Build Independent Verdict; ASK 269):
  - Why: in run 37000 luna counted "Premise 7" and "Premise 8" from the 8-entry [DR1] list, but the count list is the 6-entry list that closes [DR2]. Judges are served both lists and no line said which counts.
  - What it does: after [DR1], when it holds an UNSUPPORTED-L4 PREMISES list, the system appends "[COUNT LIST NOTE -- computed by the system, not by a model (pkgCLN, ASK 269): the UNSUPPORTED-L4 PREMISES list in [DR1] (numbered 1 to 8) is not the count list. L, c, every count of CONCEDED or UNEXHIBITED premises and every "Premise <p>" read only the list that closes [DR2] (numbered 1 to 6).]" The two numbers are computed for each debate.
  - Size: inside the pkgEPC rev 2 node deltas given under pkgPWS.
  - Shown offline: 52 of 60 stored debates get the note (24 where both lists number 1 to 6).
  - Shown in run 37032: the note in 38591 reads "[DR1] (numbered 1 to 7) ... [DR2] (numbered 1 to 6)"; a SQL count of the rounds agrees; no judge counted a premise above 6.
  - Limits: a later prose "12." in [DR1] could inflate its number; not changed, since no stored case shows it.

## 9. Enforcers
Every judge answer is checked by code after it is given (Extract Independent Verdict for the single judge; Panel Payloads for the panel and reserve; Alternate Payloads for alternates). A violation is a MARK (an AUDIT line in the stored text) and, for the rules with a cure, an ITEM sent back to the same judge in one cure call (Cure Build / Panel Cure Build / Alternate Cure Build) with its own request, its answer and the cure rule. The reply is read only in the fixed ANSWER FORM: `RESTATED`, `RETRACTED`, `CORRECTED RESULT`, `CORRECTED COUNT`, `CORRECTED STANDING`, `PRESSED`, `SHORT`, `STATEMENT`, `RESPONSE`. Prose cures nothing. For the K-CON and K-W contradiction cures, a STATEMENT line's answer is read after its item label as a RESPONSE line's is, so NOT HELD, CONTRADICTION and REASSERTED IN are found on either line.
- CURE CALLS: every cure call and follow-up call goes through the sub-workflow ODV1 Cure Call (workflow `c7366RPLb8vdMQg6`). The parent's call nodes are Execute Workflow nodes (one item at a time, waiting for the reply) with the old names: Cure Call openrouter, Panel Cure Call 1 to 3 openrouter, Alternate Cure Call openrouter, R2 Alternate Cure Call openrouter, and FUM Call Cure, Panel 1 to 3, Alternate and R2 Alternate openrouter. Each sends `route` (`cure.skip ? 6 : cure.jidx` on the Cure and Panel paths; 5 on the Alternate and follow-up paths), `body` (`cure.body`, or `fum.body` on a follow-up), `parent_exec` (the parent execution id) and `item`. In the child, Cure Call Route sends 0 to Cure Call deepseek, 5 to Cure Call openrouter, 6 to Skip Return (the Build item comes back unchanged) and 1 to 4 to Provider Removed Stop, which fails the run. The calls keep the bodies, credentials, 900 s timeout and continue-on-error; the header `x-grok-conv-id` is `odv1-<parent execution id>`. The parent node returns the raw reply, so the Normalize, FUM Needed? and FUM Merge nodes and the Shared Library read it as before. The x-ai reasoning relay cannot read parent nodes from the child: a cure body sent to an x-ai model carries no relayed reasoning. A change to a cure call body is made once, in the child.
