- ALTERNATE CURE REPLIES ACROSS THE FOLLOW-UP LOOP (pkgFLR; Alternate Merge Seat and R2 Alternate Merge Seat): the follow-up path for alternates is a loop (Cure Normalize -> FUM Needed? -> FUM Call -> FUM Merge -> Cure Normalize). Each Merge Seat reads every run of its Cure Normalize node and keeps, for each alternate (cure.who and cure.k), the item of the latest run; an alternate that took no follow-up keeps its first-run item. An alternate has no cure reply only when no run of the Normalize node holds an item for it. Size: code +888, prompt 0.
- ALTERNATE SEAT CHAIN RUNS ONCE (pkgMSO; Alternate Merge Seat and R2 Alternate Merge Seat, after the pkgFLR line):
  - Alternate Merge Seat and R2 Alternate Merge Seat go on only for the last delivery from their follow-up check.
  - A delivery goes on when both hold:
    - no alternate's latest cure state still waits on a follow-up;
    - the delivery holds an item whose latest cure state comes from the last Cure Normalize run.
  - Any other delivery returns no item, so nothing below the Merge Seat runs for it.
  - A delivery with no cure item (an alternate that needed no cure call), or a run where Cure Normalize did not run, goes on as before.
  - Size: Shared Library code +1,508 (Alternate Merge Seat +750, R2 Alternate Merge Seat +756); prompt 0.
- WRITTEN RESPONSE (md_notes_rulings 371): every flagged item needs one `RESPONSE: <item> | <whether the mark is accepted and why, naming the round and quoting its words>` line beside its cure line. An item with no RESPONSE is UNCURED. The RESPONSE is matched to the item by rule and label.
- A CURE LINE WITH A DASH IN PLACE OF "|" (pkgSDS; Shared Library, the cure line reader cureLines): a cure reply line that has no "|", starts "STATEMENT:" or "RESPONSE:", and whose label starts with a K rule (K-W, K-S3, K-KJV and the like) is read with its first spaced dash after the label (" -- ", an em dash or an en dash, spaced) taken as the "|". Every other line is read as before; a line that has "|" is not changed. Size: code +318, prompt 0.
- A RETRACTED line counts only beside exactly one CORRECTED RESULT and a reason naming the round and its words or verse.
- CONSEQUENCE: a panel, reserve or alternate seat with any uncured item has `drv_uncured > 0` and is NOT COUNTED (aggregate: "not counted: debate rule"); an alternate is then sought (§10). A cured CORRECTED COUNT, STANDING or RESULT is written to that seat's row; the original stays in its text as an AUDIT line. RECOMPUTE: after a cure that retracts or regrades a collision line or corrects a count, the seat's counts, standings, verdict and RESULT are recomputed in code from the cured lines by §8's definitions and written to its fields and text. A RETRACTED line that carries its regrade, with no RESTATED line, is that collision's restated line. The single judge's cure is recorded in its text; it moves no verdict. Also in code after a cure, written to the seat's fields and text with an AUDIT line: the TIE rule (§8, RESULT line); `counted_premises` rebuilt from the items a cure counted, never adding a premise the same judge ruled VOID (the AUDIT line names the count that includes it); a judgment object that is not valid JSON is read for its reasoning text, so its lines split. A seat with no flagged item is still held to the tie rule. A MARK with no item: SELF-CONTRADICTION, a premise counted UNEXHIBITED on a verse whose ANSWER CHECK on that same side's answer reads MEETS. A FORCED line that cures a K-G4 item in the cure is written into the judge's stored text, so the send-back and the gate see it. After a cure or a send-back change, a count, standing or verdict line that no longer agrees with the corrected lines is recomputed.
- GATE RULES ON AN ENGINE-BROKEN LINK ARE NOT ASKED (pkgECG; Panel Payloads, Alternate Payloads and R2 Alternate Payloads, and their record nodes): a K-B, K-G4 or K-G7 item is not asked and not counted when its line is "Dn vs Cm (...): DEFENDER LINK BROKEN" or "... CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN" and that side's link (Dn for DEFENDER, Cm for CHALLENGER) is in the confirmed set of Close Enforcer Record (cec) or Judge Contradiction Record (jcc). This applies at the Payloads stage, before drv_uncured is counted. Each such item is recorded as an enforcer_check row with cure_status null and cure_reason "NOT ASKED (code, pkgECG)" (in Panel Cure Check, and in Alternate and R2 Alternate Merge Seat when the seat is ok). Panel Payloads runs before Judge Contradiction Record, so for panel seats only cec applies. Other rules, FORCED lines and STANDS lines are asked as before. Size: code +2,386, prompt 0.

| Mark | Catches | Cure (besides RESPONSE) |
|---|---|---|
| K-B | a LINK BROKEN line exhibiting fewer than two layer kinds (rule B). The layer reader does not let a quoted span run across a `|` mark, reads a 1 to 3 letter word in quotation marks as a word, not a quotation, and reads [L2] in the forms word (G1234, meaning) and word (meaning, H1234) | RESTATED line, same grade, two kinds exhibited; or RETRACTED; a RESTATED or RETRACTED answer is checked for the grade and the two kinds |
| K-G1 | a BROKEN line resting on an unanswered witness while that side's count is 0 | CORRECTED COUNT with a PRESSED line quoting the pressing round (and a SHORT line quoting the answer where a later round names the verse); or RETRACTED |
| K-G2 | the CITATION CHECK line absent | recorded only; no item |
| K-G3 | a side SELF-SUPPORTED while any of its links is BROKEN | CORRECTED STANDING (not SELF-SUPPORTED); or RETRACTED for each BROKEN link |
| K-G4 | any other BROKEN line counting a witness unanswered | PRESSED line quoting the pressing round (the system then searches every later round of the holding side; if any names the witness the grade is unanswered-false); or one FORCED line where the link cannot take the witness's meaning though it was answered (ruling 411); or RETRACTED (its reason, naming the round and quoting words that stand in it, may stand on the RETRACTED line or on the item's own RESPONSE or STATEMENT line; RXR), but only when the line's GROUND names a witness left unanswered: a BROKEN grade resting on meaning is cured only by a FORCED line (md_notes_rulings 421). A BROKEN line resting on the holder's own concession, naming no witness unanswered, or already carried by a FORCED line, is not K-G4 ("K-G4 NOT RAISED") |
| K-G5 | (cure-side test, not a mark) the verses of a K-G1 line that the answering round names; a SHORT line must quote that round's answer | -- |
| K-G6 | a premise the pressed-witness ledger marks NOT NAMED that the count does not include | CORRECTED COUNT and STANDING including it; or a STATEMENT quoting the words that show the verse is not a pressed witness of that premise |
| K-G7 | DEFENDER LINK BROKEN on Dn while the count omits premise n (or a CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN for an unanswered witness at count 0) | CORRECTED COUNT and STANDING; or RETRACTED with one CORRECTED RESULT. When the line does not say the witness went unanswered, or a later round of that side names every pressed witness it quotes, the break is one of meaning (ruling 421): only a FORCED line for the pair, or RETRACTED, cures it, and the premise is not required in the side's corrected count |
| K-G8 | `counted_premises` lists a different number than the count | CORRECTED COUNT equal to the array plus K-G6/K-G7 premises, with its STANDING |
| K-S | STANDS or UNDERDETERMINED on the ground that a verse does not state the link or its contrary, naming no answering round | RESTATED line naming the answering round ([CR2]-[CR4]; [DR1]-[DR3]), or regraded BROKEN with one CORRECTED RESULT |
| K-S2 | the same ground beside a round it names (bracketed or not) | mark only; never a ground |
| K-C | a line (collision, RESULT, REASONING, KEYSTONE, STANDING, CONCEDED, CHAIN MARK) that sets aside a chained premise or a side's rows read together because no one verse states the link | RESTATED line graded on the chain as a whole, the ground dropped, two layer kinds exhibited, rounds named in brackets. ANSWER-TEST MASK: before the test, quoted words, a round's words in single quotes, and answer-checks ("[CR3] does not name ..", "left .. unstated", "went unstated in [DR2]") are masked; a clause reporting what a side stated, granted or conceded, or a CONCEDED (stated) tag, is not the judge's ground |
| K-U | STANDS or UNDERDETERMINED that itself says a witness went unanswered | mark only (an unanswered witness is a BROKEN ground) |
| K-N | a count item tagged CONCEDED (stated) on words that narrow a limb | RESTATED count line that does not count the narrowing |
| K-SC | `Dn: STANDS` while its own line says the defender conceded the link | RESTATED line not STANDS; or RESTATED count line; or a STATEMENT naming [CR3], [CR4] or [DR3] and quoting the words that show the link stands |
| K-G9 | a "cited in" verse with no valid ruling line (no line; a quotation not found in the round named; a round that does not press that side; ANSWERED IN a round that does not name the verse by book, chapter and verse), or a verse ruled PRESSED and UNANSWERED that the count does not include | the ruling line; or CORRECTED COUNT and STANDING including it |
| K-LB | a link-round block, not CONCEDED, that exhibits fewer than two layer kinds (rule B's test applied to [CR3] and [DR3] blocks) while the count does not include its premise | CORRECTED COUNT and STANDING; or a STATEMENT quoting words of the block that exhibit two layer kinds |
| K-K | KEYSTONE line with a reason that names no ground | defined, not wired |
| K-G10 | a premise counted UNEXHIBITED on a verse no listed premise of that side's list carries under PRESSED WITH: (K-G9 is retired: such a verse is not a witness) | answered in the cure |
| K-G11 | a LINK BROKEN line every one of whose verses said to be unanswered is under no listed premise's PRESSED WITH: | answered in the cure |
| K-P | a premise counted UNEXHIBITED, or a link graded BROKEN as unanswered, on a press the ledger marks UNPROVEN (ruling 398) | answered in the cure |
| K-F | a FORCED line whose three quotations do not each stand in the round named, or a malformed FORCED line (rulings 396, 411). A quotation that joins a numbered list onto one line is found when it stands with the inline list markers ("2. ", "3) ") taken out of both the quotation and the round | a corrected FORCED line; a RESTATED line regrading it; or RETRACTED |
| K-FF (TRIAL) | a FORCED line whose FAILS quotation, 4 words or more, stands wholly inside one KJV verse the run read and the ANSWER round quotes that verse with its reference: a verse does not fail, the answer's reading does (ruling 411). A verse the run did not read is NOT CHECKED. The FAILS form in Panel Build and Build Independent Verdict asks for the answer's own words that fail to show the fit, not only the words of a verse it quotes | the FORCED line again with FAILS quoting the answer's own words outside its verse quotations; RETRACTED with one CORRECTED RESULT; or a RESTATED line |
| K-R | a press marked PROVEN BY REFERENCE with no REFERENCE line, or a REFERENCE line whose quotations do not stand (ruling 399) | a REFERENCE line in form |
| K-L2 | an [L2] of the judge's own, on a collision line, whose word is in no verse that line cites | RETRACTED; or a RESTATED line whose [L2] words stand in a cited verse |
| K-LXX | a judge's text saying no Septuagint reading occurred while the scan found one | a STATEMENT naming the reading with its round and words |
| K-V | a VOID ruling that does not quote the charge words beyond the quote and name the kind (the kind test reads "goes beyond the quote" and "a claim the quote, quoted words or quotation does not make"; a quote counts when it stands in the whole charge text and not wholly inside one of the charge's own quoted passages) | a STATEMENT quoting them and naming the kind; the item's STATEMENT and RESPONSE lines are read together, so the charge quote and the kind may stand on either (KVR) |
| K-W | each sentence of a side's own round 2, 3 or 4 that withdraws or grants footing ("I do not claim", "I no longer claim", "I withdraw", "I grant", "I concede", "I drop"), set against its link heading; not inside a quotation on its line, on a title heading, or negated by its object ("I concede none") | RESPONSE: STANDS WITHOUT IT because <a layer, [L1]-[L3]>; CONCEDED (at a sentence start, then up to 4 words and a colon or dash, or an "as" or "as to" qualifier to the sentence end); REASSERTED IN <round> "<quote>"; or NOT HELD with a reason quoting the flagged sentence (6 words in a row, or all when shorter; a parenthesis holding only verse references, such as "(Isaiah 8:18)", is dropped from the flagged sentence first; parentheses holding words are kept; the runs are read both with and without verse references, on both the sentence and the reply; inner quotation marks do not end the six words: the quote runs across them and may leave them out). The flagged sentence: when only a pronoun (it, that, this, so, them, these, those) or nothing follows the verb, the flagged line is the sentence before it plus the grant; when nothing but punctuation follows the verb, it is the whole sentence that holds it ("..., which I grant."); with no words before the verb either, no item is raised. A sentence cut whose double quotation marks do not balance ends at the first sentence end outside quotations. Runs a NOT HELD reason may quote also include the body without a leading "that", "how" or "whether", and the words before a trailing "which/as/that/this I grant", each when 3 or more words remain The quote tests of K-S3, K-W and K-CON read the full flagged sentence from its round |
| K-S3 | a close-round concession the count does not mark COUNTED (ruling 412) | RESPONSE: COUNTED, or TOUCHES NO LINK: <why> |
| K-CON | a contradiction the engine verified (a withdrawn or conceded premise reasserted later) that the judge neither lists nor answers NOT HELD, or CONTRADICTIONS: NONE FOUND written where one was verified (Commitment 10). A contradiction verified across the panel charges another seat only when at least two seats wrote the same pair independently | the CONTRADICTION line; NOT HELD -- <mark> -- "<quote>" -- <why>; or REASSERTED IN <round> "<quote>" |
| K-CMT | a contested grade line (BROKEN, FORCED, MISSES, VOID, UNPROVEN) in the reasoning that names no Commitment N. A FORCED line that grades nothing (`FORCED: none graded.`, `FORCED: none`) raises no K-F or K-CMT item | the line restated with its (Commitment N) |
| K-CMTL | a contested grade line, counted by line, naming no commitment | RESPONSE: K-CMTL line <n> \| (Commitment N), or NOT A GRADE |
| K-RUL | a deciding ruling (a counted premise, a collision graded broken or withdrawn, the keystone grade) with no ruling card, a NAMED BY quote that does not stand, or a RULE line that does not match the seat's accepted line (Commitment 11). A card written on one line is read as the five-line card. When the judge regrades its own collision line in the cure (RESTATED or RETRACTED to STANDS or UNDERDETERMINED), the card for that pair is read against the regraded line | the five-line card; every RESTATED line in the same reply carries its card. A valid card answers the item without a RESPONSE line |
| K-KJV | a quotation in the judge's own text that is not in the words of the 1611 KJV verse it names (Commitment 12); quotes of verses the run did not read, quotes that are not verse quotes and other versions named are listed only (NOT CHECKED) | the KJV words, or the ruling that rests on the judge's wording withdrawn |
| K-AG | an ANSWER CHECK grade the reader cannot map (not MEETS, MISSES, FAILS or UNPROVEN, or no grade), or an ANSWER: NONE row graded MEETS (FAILS is read as MISSES; ANSWER: NONE graded MISSES, FAILS or UNPROVEN raises nothing) | the ANSWER CHECK line in form |
| K-FC | a FORCED line with no `CARRIED BY: "<words of the pressed witness's own verse that state its MEANS>"` between FAILS and GROUND, or whose CARRIED BY words (3 or more) do not stand in that verse as the run read it; a verse not read is NOT CHECKED | the FORCED line again with a passing CARRIED BY; RETRACTED; or a RESTATED line not graded BROKEN |
| K-UNX | a premise counted UNEXHIBITED whose answering link-round block exhibits two or more layer kinds, where the count names no layer the block lacks. Not raised when the count says the answer does not meet the press's MEANS, names a press of that premise from the other side's list, and the answering block does not quote that MEANS. Never raised on a ground holding any word of `UNX_OTHER` (the K-UNX library): among them unanswered, no answer, not answered, ASSUMES, ROWS GIVE, NARROWED, NOT COUNTED, VOID, struck and barred, whether or not the block answers the press, except the ASSUMES and unanswered grounds K-UNX-A reads (next row). The reader (unxSegs) reads "Premise N" entries and bare single-digit entries before UNEXHIBITED, CONCEDED or WITHDRAWN, and ends an entry at "Spared" (UNXS). The gate's K re-read (gateNewK2024) also runs K-UNX, K-UNX-A and E-ASM on a count line a send-back or gate change wrote (RCK, TRIAL): at the send-back stage a new item goes to the gate as a G item; after the gate it is recorded only; no change is refused and no count is written that the judge did not write. A count that says the block lacks only layers the block exhibits also raises K-UNX (UNXL, TRIAL). No K-UNX item stands on a count of 0: a stray UNEXHIBITED line left under a CORRECTED COUNT 0 raises nothing, so a later change that restores the count is read as new by the K re-read (UNX0, TRIAL). A count that names premise j above the highest listed block of the side's round raises item a:'RANGE' (TRIAL; pkgBFX rev 2), cured by a CORRECTED COUNT below the count and cleared by a CHANGE TO that renumbers | a STATEMENT naming the missing layer: a layer counts as named missing only with a lack word before it on its clause (no, not, nothing, lacks, without, missing, absent, never, nor, fails), or "missing", "absent", "lacking" or "not exhibited" (with or without "is"), "does not ..." or "fails" after it, and only when the block does not exhibit it ([L4] always) (UNXW, UNXW2); or a CORRECTED COUNT without the premise, its fit graded at the collision (a FORCED line). A CURED CORRECTED COUNT is written to the count line and recomputed, as for K-G1 and K-G3, when every item of that side is cured (UNXW) |
| K-UNX-A (TRIAL; raised as K-UNX) | a premise counted UNEXHIBITED, on either side's count line, whose answering block exhibits two or more layer kinds, where (A) the count rests on an ASSUMES ground, the block has a counted [L1], and the ASSUMES clause with its ROWS GIVE part holds no 6 words in a row of that [L1] quotation; or (B) the count says a named press is unanswered, has no answer or is not answered, and the block quotes that press's MEANS on a line holding "MEANS". The other non-layer grounds stay exempt, read only up to the entry's last stop. A count carrying "footing borrowed from contested Link(s) <n>" is also exempt (pkgBFX rev 2) | a STATEMENT quoting the block's [L1] (6 words in a row) and naming a layer the block does not exhibit, by the K-UNX lack test (a failure of fit is not a missing layer; UNXA2), or a CORRECTED COUNT below the count read; naming a layer or a KEEP does not cure |
| E-ASM (TRIAL; raised as K-UNX) | a premise counted UNEXHIBITED on an ASSUMES ground, on a block of two or more layer kinds, whose assumed fact names a knowing, begetting or conceiving: an inferred act is never an ASSUMES. With K-UNX-A on the same premise, one item names both and counts once | a CORRECTED COUNT below the count read, or a STATEMENT whose ASSUMES names a given fact with no such act (and, with K-UNX-A, quotes the [L1] with its lack); a KEEP does not cure |

- LIVE AND TRIAL: each K rule's status is the `ENF_STATUS` table in the cure-library nodes (read it in Extract Independent Verdict; the rows above give no status). LIVE: an uncured item counts in `drv_uncured`. TRIAL: the item is sent in the cure, answered and logged; an uncured TRIAL item removes no seat. SILENCE COUNTS: an item of any rule, TRIAL included, that gets no written response at all counts in `drv_uncured` like a LIVE item; a refused (malformed) line is a written response. The single judge's silent items are named in one review line in `review_notes`.
- ONE RULE TABLE: the cure rules of the K-W family, the RETRACTED family, the "unanswered" family and K-G11/K-P are read from one rule table in the library (what each item needs and which line kinds cure it), and the cure ask states the same table.
- CURE LABELS: RESPONSE and STATEMENT labels are matched with case, colons and bars folded, ending at a word edge ("#1" is not "#10"); a RESPONSE headed by the rule alone answers the only item of that rule. EXACT ITEM KEY: when several lines match an item, the reader takes the line whose label names the fewest distinct item keys (an item key is its link, or SIDE STANDING; two rules on one key count once); a line naming no item key under its own rule ranks last; on a tie the first line wins, so a line naming two items never takes an item from its own line. The same pick chooses a K-RUL card: among the cards that match the item, the card whose RULING target names the fewest K-RUL items of the build.
- K-FC, WHICH VERSE IS THE PRESSED VERSE (`kfcVerse`, Shared Library; pkgKFV): when the MEANS sentence of the FORCED line opens with a reference, that is the verse. Otherwise the first 12 words of the MEANS sentence are sought in the lines of the pressing round (list marks and emphasis marks removed): first a line where those words stand after "MEANS:"; with no such line, the first line that holds them anywhere. On the chosen line the verse is the last reference before "MEANS:", or, with none there, the first reference of the line. With no line or no reference on it, no verse is found. So a prose line that repeats the MEANS words beside another verse no longer names that other verse as the pressed one when a press line carries the words after "MEANS:".
- JSON CURE ANSWERS: a cure call to terra, sol, luna or Haiku (`xkJsonModels()` in the library) carries `response_format` json_schema `cure_lines` (removed by pkgCRF, entry below) and an ANSWER FORMAT note; the reply is `{"cure_lines":[{"kind":"RESPONSE"|"STATEMENT"|"OTHER","item":...,"text":...}]}` in order. cureText turns it back into the same lines (`RESPONSE: <item> | <text>`, `STATEMENT: <item> | <text>`, OTHER as the whole line), so one line reader reads every reply. A reply cut off inside the cure_lines array keeps every complete entry and drops the cut entry and everything after it; with no complete entry it is read as text. A reply that is not cure_lines JSON is read as text. Other models are sent as before. The follow-up repeats the note. A cure reply written inside another JSON object (`{"reasoning": "..."}`, `answer`, `reason`), valid or not, is unwrapped before the line reader reads it.
- CURE CALLS CARRY NO cure_lines SCHEMA, SO THEY CAN READ THE PROMPT CACHE (pkgCRF; Shared Library; the six cure Builds and six cure Normalize nodes):
  - Text:
    - Nodes: Shared Library only (three library lines). Stubs that rebuild them: the six cure builders (Cure Build, Panel Cure Build 1 to 3, Alternate Cure Build, R2 Alternate Cure Build) and the six cure readers (Cure Normalize, Panel Cure Normalize 1 to 3, Alternate Cure Normalize, R2 Alternate Cure Normalize). Prompt: none (the text sent to every model is unchanged).
    - What it does:
      - A cure call to terra, sol, luna or Haiku (`xkJsonModels()`) no longer carries `response_format` json_schema `cure_lines`. The ANSWER FORMAT note stays on the cure ask exactly as before, so the model is still asked for `{"cure_lines":[...]}` in the same words.
      - Why: the provider places a structured-output schema ahead of the messages in the cached prefix. A cure request with the schema therefore matched nothing the judge's earlier call had cached and read 0 cached tokens, although its messages begin with that earlier request. Without the schema the cure request starts with the judge's earlier request (the panel judge call, or the single judge's Verdict call) and can read it.
      - The follow-up ask (pkgFUM) adds the ANSWER FORMAT note when the cure ask it follows ended with that note.
      - `xkRF()` (the schema) is no longer called; its library line is left empty.
      - Reading is unchanged: cureText reads cure_lines JSON (fenced or not, extra keys ignored, a reply cut off mid-array keeps every complete entry), and reads any other reply as plain lines.
    - Limits:
      - Without the schema the model is not held to the JSON form. A reply that opens with prose before the JSON, JSON with single quotes, or entries without "item" is read as text; its items count as unanswered and the follow-up asks them once (one more call). Stored panel verdicts of terra, sol, luna and Haiku, asked for JSON with no schema, opened with a complete valid JSON object in 116 of 116 cases (30 days).
      - A cache read is not guaranteed: the provider keeps a prefix for a limited time and routes by its own rules. Stored runs before the schema read the panel prefix on every Panel Cure call (WALL-4871).
    - Size:
    - Code -416 characters (measured, whole workflow); prompt 0.
  - Size: Shared Library only; code -416 (2,927,558 to 2,927,142); 418 nodes; fixed prompt 0. Each cure request drops exactly 1 response_format (-437 characters) and keeps exactly 1 ANSWER FORMAT note.
  - Benefit, an estimate: about 276,218 prompt tokens per run become readable (208,770 panel plus 67,448 single judge) and about $0.34 saved per run of about $0.50 for the four cure calls (from the billed costs of 35067). The cache gain is shown only by a run: whether the cure calls now read the cache is shown only by a paid call or the next run.
  - Unproven until two clean runs; the first run shows it on the 4 cure calls: whether they read the cache and whether their replies are read as cure_lines. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 33).
- KEYED LINES: the cure, the recompute, the send-back and the gate read collision lines, count lines (CONCEDED LINKS and its head in psGrades), STANDING lines, FORCED lines and line labels through one shared line reader first; where the reader reads no such form, or reads it RESTATED for a label, the older pattern decides. The four Send Back nodes read ANSWER CHECK lines (MEANS, ANSWER and grade fields) and Extract Independent Verdict reads its verdict lines through the same reader; an ANSWER CHECK line the reader refuses keeps the old read. A collision line the reader refuses keeps grade null (never STANDS or "not broken") and is named unread; with no broken line read, verdict, RESULT, CLAUSE and a SELF-SUPPORTED standing are not derived from it. A RESTATED line may carry the REASONING label. A RESTATED line in no form the line reader reads goes to the label branch only when the reader found no form at all; a KEYSTONE, RESULT or STANDING line the reader refused stays malformed. A KEEP does not hold a line that says "contradicted by" or "cannot agree with" followed by [L1] and two or more layer kinds.

- SYNTHESIS CHECKS (Build Save Body, review lines in `review_notes`, no model call, no cure): S-A a "both" sentence not quoting both sides' words (or naming a verse one side never names); S-B "every link answered" while a closing list carries a verse the answering side never names; S-C an item listed as charged by neither side that stands in a closing list; S-D the guards said to bind, or to need running against, the defender's seat (they bind the Oracle seat only, md_notes_rulings 383); S-E the synthesis says a side withdrew or conceded where no round of that side holds those words affirmed; S-F the synthesis says a close pressed nothing new while the close-round checks found a press; S-G the Debate Oracle seat quotes Psalm 2:7, by reference or by its words "this day have I begotten thee", without all three of Acts 13:33, Hebrews 5:5 and Hebrews 1:5 (DG-18); S-H a Debate Oracle round quotes Matthew 1:20's "that which is conceived in her is of the Holy Ghost" without "Joseph, thou son of David" just before it (DG-8). S-G and S-H check the synthesis's guard test; they change no verdict.
- S-E, WHICH WORDS OF THE SENTENCE ARE ITS OBJECT (pkgSEG rev 3; Build Save Body):
  - S-E takes the object words of a synthesis sentence ("withdrew <object>", "conceded <object>") and asks whether a round of that side holds the verb affirmed with one of those words near it.
  - The object is read only up to the first semicolon, or the first comma followed by "and", "but", "while" or "then". The cut is not made when that word is followed by the, its, his, her, their, a, an, this, that, any, all or both: there the stretch after the comma is still read as object.
  - These words are not object words: narrowed, narrows, narrowing, specified, certain, several, various, limbs, claims, points, parts, others, withdrawing, conceding, retracting, dropping, qualified. A sentence left with no object word ("The defender withdrew several points.") is checked by the verb only: it passes when a round of that side holds the verb affirmed.
  - Size: code +302 in Build Save Body; prompt 0.
  - Limits: a new clause that opens with a determiner after the comma is read as object and can raise a line where none is due (", and the challenger pressed the Barzillai register"; ", but the Barzillai register it kept"); the check raised these before the change too. A negated synthesis sentence ("conceded no premises") still raises a line. A claim with no object word says nothing about what was withdrawn, and the check cannot test it.
- CLOSE-ROUND CHECKS (D4 and C4 Build and Extract, Panel Build, Build Independent Verdict, Build Synthesis, Build Save Body, Format Markdown), computed by code and printed under the close for the judges and in the transcript, and recorded in `review_notes`; the close text itself is not altered:
  - CLOSE-ROUND NEW-VERSE CHECK: references in [CR4] that no round [CR1]-[DR3] names: NEW IN CLOSE, NOT COUNTED.
  - CLOSE-ROUND PRESS CHECK: references in [DR4] that no [DR1]-[DR3] names: NEW PRESS, NOT COUNTED.
  - CLOSE-ROUND GRADING CHECK: a sentence in either close that names the other side or one of its links and grades or presses it: DISREGARDED.
  - CLOSE-ROUND NUMBERING CHECK: a close that states a numbering of its own, names a D or C number above the declared ones, or gives a D or C label to a block whose wording and verses match another declared link or [DR3] block better than its own.
  - THE PRE-CHECK FINDS A QUOTE THAT STANDS AFTER "ASSUMES:" (pkgPCQ; preCheckSide in Panel Build and Build Independent Verdict):
    - Text: pkgPCQ. PRE-CHECK verbatim-quote finder (preCheckSide, Panel Build and Build Independent Verdict). The finder cuts each premise list item at "ASSUMES:" before it searches for the opponent's quote; after the cut point it also takes a quote introduced by "words:", "Defender:" or "Challenger:"; the ROWS GIVE verse quotes and the PRESSED WITH part are not searched; the charge words are cut as before. An item whose quote stands after the ASSUMES -- ROWS GIVE part is therefore found. Limit: a quote after ASSUMES with another marker is still not read.
    - Why: a judge who trusts a "no verbatim quote found" line could VOID a good premise on the ground "no verbatim quote"; the PRE-CHECK decides nothing, and in the stored rows the judges found the quotes themselves. WALL-5034
    - Size (measured): Panel Build and Build Independent Verdict +277 each, identical edits; code +554; connections equal; 418 nodes. The PRE-CHECK text grows +746 characters over the 7 lines. In the combined file with pkgDFR: code +614.
    - See the s145 entry in section 18.
    - Limits and unproven: see section 17. A paid call proves nothing here; code on stored text is the proof. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 39).
- GROUND-ONLY READING: K-C, K-S, K-S2, K-U and K-SC read only the judge's own ground clause (the text after its last GROUND:, or the line after its label where there is none). Quoted text, a round's words in single quotes, and any run of six or more words of a DECLARED CHAIN link are removed first, so a quotation of either side is never read as the judge's reasoning.
- No judge enforcer tests a judge against the record's guards (md_notes_rulings 382, 383).
- Review-line counting: K-U, K-S2, K-C, K-N, K-SC and K-K marks do not count toward `audit_flags` or move `data_class`; the cure and the uncounted seat carry their consequence.
- ENFORCER LOG: `debate_enforcer_log`, one row per flagged item and per stored reply: `debate_id`, `run_token`, `seat`, `seat_role`, `judge_model`, `kind` (`cure`, `alternate_reply`, `failed_reply`, `enforcer_check`), `rule`, `item`, `flagged_line`, `prompt_sent`, `full_reply`, `reply_error`, `response_line`, `cure_status`, `cure_reason`, `at`. Written by Write Enforcer Log (`rpc/record_enforcer_log(p_debate_id, p_run_token, p_rows)`, SECURITY DEFINER) between Write Judgment 3 and Derive Outcome, in one call; a failed write goes to Panel Failure Note and no outcome is derived (33222). Write Enforcer Log builds the log body itself; the Alternate Merge nodes do not carry it. In the body sent, a `prompt_sent` or `full_reply` of 2,000 characters or more that equals the same field of an earlier row is replaced by `[SAME <field> AS row <n> of the run log: seat, kind, rule, item]`, where n counts the run's rows by id from 1; read row n of the same `run_token` for the text. RLS on; anon and authenticated cannot read it; read it by SQL as owner. A dry regrade and a run that stops before Write Judgment 3 write no log.
- THE SECOND READING (DeepSeek, ruling 428): after the cure, Judge Enforcer Build sends every panel judge's lines to the `deepseek` model in calls of at most `CALL_CHARS` characters (`MAX_CALLS` calls; both in Judge Enforcer Build), one reading per item, identical items of several judges read once. The rules it reads: E-ANS, each ANSWER CHECK line (a list of rounds such as "[CR3; CR4]" is checked in every listed round); E-VOID, each VOID ruling against its premise's own list entry; E-COL, each FORCED line and each LINK BROKEN collision line (a rival reading of a contested word, footed by the answer's own layers, is not a break; the FAILS quote of a FORCED line ends at its closing quote mark, a CARRIED BY or GROUND tail is not part of it, and the request notes "The FAILS words are found in [..]" when those words stand in the answering round); E-HOLD, each STANDS or UNDERDETERMINED collision line with that judge's own ANSWER CHECK lines on its verses that are not MEETS; E-KEY, each KEYSTONE line with that judge's collision lines on that link; E-PREM, each listed premise with its answering link-round block and each judge's count line for that side (a NARROWED premise that keeps its footing is spared). On a count line the engine rewrote after a cure ("recomputed after the cure"), E-PREM is shown the judge's stored counted premises, whether premise N is in them, the judge's own line before the recount and its collision lines on that link; the ruling is COUNTED when N is stored as counted, else SPARED, and silence is not a SPARED ruling. E-KEY is shown the defender's own opening and the link's premise line, so a keystone ground that restates the defender's thesis is not read as the judge's own doctrine. The finders that send an answer in full (E-ANS, E-HOLD) match the answer's opening words only at word edges. An E-OOR sentence ends before the judge's ` -- ` tag, or at the closing quotation mark followed by that tag, the line end or the judge's own prose. An E-ANS DISAGREE (right: MISSES) whose reason calls the answer only a grant, says it gives no fit, or says it only restates, is HELD and not sent back (`ENFORCER HELD` in `cure_reason`) when the answer in the round the item names (5 or more of the quoted words in a row) carries 3 or more distinct [Ln] tags, or 1 or more and a fit sentence after the quoted words; a reason naming another defect (omits, ignores, nowhere, repeats, concedes or withdraws the press) is never held. When an E-PREM "right: COUNTED" is sent back on a block of two or more layer kinds and its reason names no layer the block lacks, Send Back Build adds the K-UNX note ("A count of UNEXHIBITED must name the layer the block lacks (K-UNX). A failure of fit is graded at the collision (a FORCED line, ruling 411), not counted. You keep the decision."); in every sent-back reason, a run of 6 words found in another judge's text and not in the record or this judge's own text is cut (shown as "[words from another judge's line cut: record words only (pkgEPK)]"), the full reason kept in reason_full (EPK, TRIAL; no push is stopped, ruling 428); E-RES, each judge's verdict word, RESULT, CLAUSE, KEYSTONE, standings and count lines against the judges' own rules; E-SYN, each synthesis sentence saying a side withdrew, conceded, granted, dropped or narrowed something, against that side's own words. Each reading answers AGREE or DISAGREE with the right grade and a quote; rows go to `debate_enforcer_log` (kind `enforcer_check`). A failed or empty call is recorded by its index, never thrown. The first call nodes (Judge Enforcer Call deepseek and its Alt and R2 Alt copies) carry no node retry; a failure is an output item. The Retry Build nodes then split each call that came back failed, empty, cut at the token limit, or short (two or more fewer ENFORCER lines than the call's item ids) into two halves of whole items and send the halves once through the Retry Call nodes (3 tries each); the reason is kept in `enf428.esr.why`, and the Record reads the halves in place of the call. The reply form asks for one line per item id, the id alone. The Record also reads `<id> (<seat>)`, `<id>, <seat>` and `<seat> (<id>)`; a bare id with no item number (`Y1`) only when the call holds exactly one such item; lines keyed by judge name only when their count and judge order equal the call's items exactly. Any other line leaves the item ENFORCER NO VERDICT, and the label fault is counted in `enf428`.
- CODE CHECKS SENT BACK WITH THE SECOND READING (Send Back Build; each item states ENFORCED or TRIAL in its own text): E-COL by layers (Commitment 13: a FORCED line, or a LINK BROKEN line not resting on an unanswered press, with no counted [L1], or turning on a word's meaning with no counted [L2]), sent back whatever DeepSeek read; E-HOLD on every UNDERDETERMINED line with no counted [L1] or not stating what the record leaves open; E-UNG, a link the challenger presses in [DR2] with no graded collision line (a [DR2] premise naming "LINK k" presses Dk; one with no LINK label keeps its place number); ABSENCE GRADES, a MEETS, STANDS or UNDERDETERMINED resting on an absence or non-denial with no counted [L1] positive fit; E-PAB, a break or count charging a press as unanswered where the press's MEANS rests on an absence; E-MNP, an ANSWER CHECK saying a press has no MEANS where the press ledger reads one (the MEANS cure check reads a MEANS that holds inner quotes; quoting the ledger's MEANS word for word cures it); E-IMP (Commitment 14), a sentence resting an answer on a verse not stating something with no IMPOSED line, or a MEETS on an answer the judge's own IMPOSED line flags NO ROW SETS IT; E-KSC (Commitment 15; LIVE), `KEYSTONE: Dn -- STANDS` while the judge's own defender count counts link n UNEXHIBITED, CONCEDED or WITHDRAWN. The count is read by the shared count-line reader (`CLR`): the last CONCEDED LINKS or DEFENDER CONCEDED LINKS line with its unlabelled continuation lines; counted tags are read in capitals only; NOT, none and no negate; a count of 0 counts nothing. "LINK n" and "Dn" name link n. A premise entry never counts: a premise conceded under a link is not the link withdrawn. A link counts only when, outside quotations, the line names "LINK n" or "Dn" directly followed by UNEXHIBITED, CONCEDED or WITHDRAWN, and not as a premise's link ("Premise 2, LINK 6", "Premise 2 (LINK 6)", "charged against D6", "under LINK 6"). Only the last plain KEYSTONE line is read; lines opening with `[` are not read. Cures of these items are CHANGE TO lines; a KEEP does not cure E-IMP, E-KSC, E-COL by layers or the absence grades. A collision line is regraded, never retracted: an E-COL or E-HOLD ask on a collision line offers the line with the pair regraded, with its GROUND. On a FORCED line the ask offers CHANGE TO: RETRACTED: <pair>, which applies only once the pair's collision line is no longer LINK BROKEN. When the judge's text grades that collision line LINK BROKEN and no ask for it is in the same send-back, a companion E-COL ask for the collision line is added (next S number), and the FORCED ask names it. A companion is record only: answered, it is CURED with a COMPANION ASK note; unanswered, it is not silence and is never counted. E-KSC is LIVE (`KSC` library, `LIVE=true`): an uncured item counts in `drv_uncured`. A TRIAL item left uncured is an AUDIT line and removes no seat. The single judge gets these as record-only review lines in Extract Independent Verdict.
- SEND-BACK AND APPLY (E-APPLY; `sbApply2024`, identical in Gate Build, Gate Record, Alt Gate Build, Alt Gate Record, Alternate Merge Seat and Alternate Merge): each item goes back to its judge as `S<n>` with the flagged line; the judge answers `SENDBACK: S<n> -- CHANGE TO: <line>` or `KEEP`. A change replaces a whole line, never a substring, and is unwrapped from quotation marks only when it both opens and closes with one. The rewrite finds the old line as a whole line, not as the first substring hit. A change with no line label that adds a "Premise N" to a CONCEDED LINKS count is appended to that CONCEDED LINKS line, not refused. A send-back or gate answer written with dashes in place of `||` is read in that form: the first dash after the item id opens the CHANGE TO line, and the reason opens at the last dash before a round tag or a quotation mark. For a CHANGE TO answer that dash must stand after the corrected line's last field: a dash followed by a round tag, MEANS:, ANSWER:, GROUND:, FAILS:, [L1] to [L4], or an upper-case grade word (MEETS, MISSES, FAILS, BROKEN, STANDS, UNDERDETERMINED, WITHDRAWN, FORCED, SPARED, VOID, COUNTED, UNEXHIBITED, CONCEDED, NOT HELD, DEFENDER or CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN; case-sensitive). If no dash qualifies, the whole rest is the corrected line (pkgGDC; Shared Library line 1757, `gdsDash2024`; read by Send Back Record, Gate Record and their Alt and R2 Alt copies). A KEEP answer and a line that already has `||` are never touched, and each answer so read carries a FORM NOTE in the audit and the logged line. When a recompute rewrites a judge line, it matches only at a line start and takes a whole-line match first, so a line is never rewritten inside a longer line with the same ending. In reading a count line's grade clauses (psGrades), a quotation that ends a sentence keeps the sentence break, so the grade before it does not run on to the next sentence. Two changes act inside one line: a change with no line label whose flagged text (30 or more characters) stands in exactly one line replaces that sentence in that line (an E-VOID correction of one count-line sentence); and over a labelled count line, a change replaces the one "Premise N" segment it names (none or several: NOT APPLIED, to the gate). A sentence change that moves its premise into or out of the count would leave the line's number stale, so it is NOT APPLIED and goes to the gate. A change whose flagged line is not in the text goes over the one line with the same label (CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS, CONCEDED LINKS, a STANDING line, KEYSTONE, CLAUSE, a collision pair); none or several: NOT APPLIED. For an E-UNG item, the change replaces the judge's line for the same pair or is added after the last collision line. A CHANGE TO a RESULT line is applied when it equals the result derived from the corrected lines; otherwise RESULT DIFFERS is raised and it stays unapplied. A CHANGE TO `RETRACTED: Dn vs Cm` on a FORCED line for that pair removes the FORCED line and keeps the collision line; when that collision line is still graded LINK BROKEN it is NOT APPLIED and goes to the gate (ruling 411). An E-PREM change that regrades a premise the item does not name, moves the count by more than the named premises, or moves the line to the other side is NOT APPLIED. When an applied change regrades a keystone collision and no change touched the KEYSTONE line, the KEYSTONE line is re-derived and recorded "KEYSTONE (derived)". The seat's counts, standings, verdict and RESULT are then recomputed from the corrected lines (§8 definitions).
- THE GATE (E-GATE): after the apply, each NOT APPLIED change and each line the corrected text still breaks goes back once more as `G<n>`; panel seats and alternates take the same path. A CHANGE answer cures when its item is no longer raised and its line stands in the corrected text, even when another change wrote it; an identical change on two items cures both. When a judge writes several gate changes over one line, the last applied one stands; each earlier change on that line, of any item kind, NOT APPLIED items included, cures when that later applied change wrote the line and its item is no longer raised. A change followed only by refused changes is not cured this way. A gate change over a count line that answers a refused E-PREM change takes the same premise-scope test as the send-back (side, moved premises, count jump), with the premises the E-PREM items on that line name. A gate change that raises a new LIVE K item does not cure; the new item counts once, unless the late gate (THE LATE GATE, below) cures it. A fresh LIVE E-GATE B contradiction raised after the gate is tied the same way to the gate change that flipped that collision's grade; a change that keeps the grade and only swaps the ground verse leaves the fresh item recorded only (GBC). In reading which premises a change regrades, a negated count ("not counted", "are not counted", "never counted", "uncounted") reads SPARED (PSN). A gate FORCED line that cures a K-G4 item is written after its collision line. A gate item counts as superseded only where the apply step's own record says its line was replaced. A gate change that repeats an original change already applied is cured. The gate treats a judge's "unanswered" claim as false only for a witness NAMED IN AN ANSWER (§8, the ledger); a later mention is not an answer. A gate K-G1 item lists the side's CONCEDED LINKS line (CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS for a challenger link) beside the collision line, so a count-line change replaces the count line. The gate's K re-read does not raise as new a K-S3 item whose sentence the text held before the change. A CHANGE TO `RETRACTED: Dn vs Cm` whose item quotes a FORCED line the text no longer holds word for word finds the FORCED line by its pair; when an earlier item of the same reply already retracted that pair, the item is recorded as covered by it. An E-OOR item takes CHANGE TO: STRUCK -- <why> (also "(struck: ...)"). An uncured gate item counts in `drv_uncured`.
- THE LATE GATE (pkgLGS rev 3; on each path P, which is the panel, Alt and R2 Alt: `P Late Gate Build` -> `P Late Gate Needed?` -> `P Late Gate Call openrouter`, wired between `P Gate Call openrouter` and `P Gate Record`): a gate change left UNCURED only because it raised a new LIVE item is not counted at once. Late Gate Build re-reads the gate answers, numbers the new items after the gate items (G n+1 and on) and sends the judge one message; the body is the gate conversation, the gate reply and the late message. Gate Record reads the gate replies by node name; its input is the late call. It runs the gate's cure reading (`gateCure2024`) on the late reply. When every late item raised by one change is cured, that change is CURED and its count comes off; the pass lowers only what it added itself, never below 0. Otherwise the count stays on the change and its reason gains a `[late gate (pkgLGS): ...]` note. There is no further round: an item the late change raises that no earlier pass raised is printed `E-GATE LATE NEW ITEM`, RECORD-ONLY. A late CHANGE is written into the judge's text as a gate CHANGE is, cured or not. A late call with no answer read writes one row `LATE (no answer read)` and leaves the gate changes as graded. Records: `E-GATE LATE` audit lines; send_back rows whose item opens `LATE` (RECORD-ONLY, the count is on the change that raised it); the "raised after the gate answers" row of a change cured late is CURED; `seats[].late` (items, cured_changes, error). With no late item, Gate Record's output is as before. Panel Tokens costs the three late calls. Size (measured against the flow before): stored code +29,774, of which Shared Library +16,140, Panel Tokens +116 and nine new nodes; rebuilt Gate Record +4,002, Alt +4,026, R2 Alt +4,041; each Late Gate Build about 240,700 rebuilt. Each late call resends about 304,000 to 331,000 prompt characters and has the gate call's timeout. Limits: see section 17.
- THE DEEPSEEK ENFORCER (TRIAL; md_notes_rulings 421, 424): Panel Cure Check -> Enforcer Build -> Enforcer Needed? -> Enforcer Call deepseek -> Enforcer Record -> Alternate Needed?. One call to the `deepseek` model checks every panel judge's K-W and K-S3 answer. Per item it is given the side's own blocks for that link from every round of that side, the side's sentence and the judges' answers. Each judge answer carries the judge's RESPONSE and its STATEMENT for the item; the form may stand on either, and NO ANSWER is written only when neither is in a form. K-W has four forms (STANDS WITHOUT IT because <a layer>; CONCEDED; REASSERTED IN <round> "<quote>"; NOT HELD quoting the flagged sentence) and K-S3 three (COUNTED; TOUCHES NO LINK: <why>; NOT HELD: <why>); a NOT HELD or REASSERTED answer is graded, never NO ANSWER. K-W: it decides first what fact the flagged sentence carried for the link and whether the side still claims it. The flagged sentence itself never counts as a still-claim; for a grant, the fact at risk is the link's footing the granted point answers, and the judge's layer is never required to carry the granted point. When the side still claims the fact, STANDS WITHOUT IT is right if words of the side outside the flagged sentence rest that fact on a verse whose own words state it (a bare restatement carries nothing): AGREE when the judge's layer carries it, otherwise DISAGREE with "right: STANDS WITHOUT IT because <the side's kept words>" (a wrong layer, not a concession). CONCEDED is right only when no such words exist. When the side no longer claims the fact, STANDS WITHOUT IT is right on footing the side itself gave. A NOT HELD reason must first quote six words in a row of the flagged sentence after its verb, across any inner quotation marks, ellipses and commas; a quote that stops short at an inner mark is DISAGREE. K-S3: when the concession gives up part of a declared link or listed premise, AGREE with COUNTED and DISAGREE with TOUCHES NO LINK or NOT HELD; otherwise the reverse. It answers one line per answer id `A<item>.<n>`, `ENFORCER: <id> -- AGREE | DISAGREE | NO ANSWER -- NEEDS: YES | NO -- <quote>`. The Record also reads an id written `G<k>/A<k>.<n>` (or `ITEM k/`, `k/`) when k is the answer's own item, and item lines `G<k> --`: one such line covers every answer of the item when the answers share a form; when their forms differ and the item has as many G<k> lines as answers, line i is answer i, unless a line quotes another answer's own opening words and not its own. Anything else is ENFORCER NO VERDICT. Its rows reach `debate_enforcer_log` (kind `enforcer_check`) through Alternate Merge. It moves no grade itself: its readings feed the send-back above, and the judge decides each item. A failed call is recorded, never thrown.
- A DEEPSEEK ENFORCER RETRY HALF WITH NO REPLY IS SENT ONCE MORE (pkgERS; the four retry paths Enforcer, Judge Enforcer, Alt Judge Enforcer and R2 Alt Judge Enforcer; Shared Library Records and new nodes):
  - pkgERS. A DeepSeek enforcer retry half with no reply is sent once more. Code +3,419 (measured, jsCode; 1,741 of it in four new Code nodes), eight new IF and HTTP nodes (4,538 characters of node JSON), prompt 0 (no Build or prompt text changes; a resend repeats one half's request only when that half failed).
  - Why: each enforcer retry path splits a failed call into halves and sends each once. The Retry Call nodes carry retryOnFail (3 tries), but with onError continueRegularOutput the HTTP node returns a transport error as an item and never throws, so n8n's retry never runs. WALL-5086
  - On each of the four retry paths (Enforcer, Judge Enforcer, Alt Judge Enforcer, R2 Alt Judge Enforcer): P Retry Call deepseek feeds the new Code node "P Resend Build". It lists the retry halves whose reply has no choices, with the same request body (else one item with ers_resend false). The new IF "P Resend Needed?" sends them to the new HTTP node "P Resend Call deepseek" (a copy of the Retry Call node), and both branches reach P Record. P Record puts each resent reply in the failed one's place before it reads the halves. The three Shared Library Records count the resends in esr.resent.
  - Limits: one resend only, so a resend that also fails leaves the items NO VERDICT, as before. A half that ended at the token cap or with empty content is not resent (the model failed, not the transport). A resend can add up to 15 minutes (the node timeout). esr.resent counts sends, not successes; the non-library Enforcer Record has no resend count. The source of the 35296 abort is not read. Only a live run shows the new nodes ran.
  - Limits: see section 17. Resend Call copies retryOnFail with continueRegularOutput, so its own n8n retry does not fire either and the one resend is the only retry; a resend that aborts again costs up to 15 more minutes for nothing. Routing is simulated offline; a stored early reply that is an error fails that seat (as live). Only a live run shows the new nodes ran. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 42).
- WHAT DEEPSEEK IS GIVEN AND HOW ITS LINES ARE READ: each E-ANS and E-HOLD item carries THE ANSWER IN FULL, read from the answering round (the pressed verse's line and every line under it, including the MEANS-header form `**MEANS [CR3] P5.4: "<press meaning>"**`), and the rule says the answer is all of it. DeepSeek is given the same witness ledger the judges saw, for E-HOLD and for E-COL items that name a pressed verse. E-B7: a premise counted UNEXHIBITED on a verse whose ANSWER CHECK lines by the same judge grade the answer MEETS is put to DeepSeek as an item (side-neutral), panel and alternate seats. Its answer lines are read by id in every form seen (`ID (seat)`, `ID, seat`, `seat (ID)`, seat-only lines by order, bare ids); each judge line has its own id (`P3.1`), and a bare item id that names several judge lines is not read as all of them. A DeepSeek verdict whose label contradicts its own stated reason is flagged and not used. Calls are sized by DeepSeek's reasoning cost (`CALL_CHARS`, `MAX_CALLS` in Judge Enforcer Build); the K-W / K-S3 call (Enforcer Call deepseek) is split and resent once when it fails (Enforcer Retry Build), as the second-reading calls are. A seated alternate's K-W cure answers get the same DeepSeek check (Alt and R2 Alt Judge Enforcer Build).
- ANSWER CHECK, FULL ANSWER SERVED (pkgEAF form AU; Shared Library):
  - Text: "Answer check, full answer served (pkgEAF form AU). When the code builds each E-ANS item for the DeepSeek answer check, it first reads the judge's quoted answer with every backslash-escaped quote mark turned back into a plain quote mark. It uses that text to find the side's lines the answer came from. It then serves the check the full block of the answering side's text that holds the answer (found by verse, by stated meaning, or by the answer's own words). It does this for every answer whose block is at least 60 characters longer than the quote. The block is cut at 4,000 characters. Cost: about +44K to +56K characters of check text per stored run, and one more DeepSeek call per run (MEASURED on stored runs). Limits: a block whose verse is written without its book name is not found. The check sets no grade itself (ruling 428, record-only trial); its DISAGREE and NO ANSWER rows go back to the judge in the send-back, and the judge keeps or changes its grade."
  - The send-back filter in Send Back Build (panel) and at library line 2891 (alternate) admits every enforcer row whose reason opens "ENFORCER DISAGREE" or "ENFORCER NO ANSWER" and excludes only rules K-W and K-CON; the check's row carries "(judge wrote ...; RECORD-ONLY TRIAL, ruling 428)". pkgEAF changes what text the check reads, not whether its rows are sent back. The first run lists the E-ANS DISAGREE (right: MISSES) rows on items that carried the full answer.
  - Size: Shared Library only; code 2,921,162 to 2,921,231 (+69). Fixed prompt 0. Effective source changes only in Judge Enforcer Build, Alt Judge Enforcer Build and R2 Alt Judge Enforcer Build.
  - Unproven until two clean runs; not yet run on the n8n runner.
- ANSWER CHECK, THE SIDE'S OTHER LINES (pkgA1N rev 3; Shared Library):
  - Text:
    - Answer check, the side's other lines (pkgA1N). The DeepSeek answer check (E-ANS) reads an answer the way the judges read it. An answer to a pressed verse counts from any later round of the same side (operator rulings, md_notes_rulings rows 86 and 373), and also from a block of the same round that the answer points to.
    - When the code builds each E-ANS item, it adds THE SIDE'S OTHER LINES ON THIS VERSE. That section holds two things:
      - (a) The LINK N section of the same round, for each pointer the answer gives. A pointer is written "under link N" or "(link N)", in any letter case. The section contributes its paragraphs that name the pressed verse, or its opening when none does.
      - (b) The paragraphs of the side's later rounds that name the pressed verse. The defender's later rounds are [CR2] to [CR4]; the challenger's are [DR1] to [DR3].
    - Press lists ("PRESSED WITH", "-- MEANS:") are left out. The section is cut at 900 characters. RULE E-ANS in the check's prompt says that an answer given there counts as the answer.
    - The check sets no grade itself (ruling 428, record-only trial); its DISAGREE and NO ANSWER rows go back to the judge in the send-back, and the judge keeps or changes its grade. It corrects in both directions: of 48 stored check-only send-backs, 39 were MISSES corrections and 9 were MEETS corrections.
    - The section is used by Judge Enforcer Build, Alt Judge Enforcer Build and R2 Alt Judge Enforcer Build (Shared Library).
    - Size: code +2,203; prompt +322 characters per check call.
    - ORDER OF A POINTED BLOCK (pkgOLK rev 3; Shared Library, olkOrder, in the same three nodes):
      - When the answer points to a link block ("under link N", "(link N)") and no paragraph of that block names the pressed verse, the block is served with the units that share a key word with the answer first, in their own order, then the rest in order.
      - A unit is a paragraph (lines up to a blank line) with any list under it. List items open with "-", "*", "•", "1.", "1)", "(a)" or "a."; a list is kept with the paragraph above it.
      - Key words are the answer's words of five or more letters, less a stop list of common stems, compared on their first five letters.
      - A unit holding a PRESSED WITH or "-- MEANS:" line is never moved forward.
      - Nothing is added or dropped; blank lines between units are kept; the 900-character cut is unchanged. When no unit or every unit shares a key word, the block is served as before.
      - Size: Shared Library code +1,228; prompt 0.
    - ANSWER IN THE SAME ROUND UNDER ANOTHER VERSE (pkgSRA rev 3a; Shared Library, sraSame, in the same three nodes):
      - Each E-ANS item may carry THE SIDE'S LINES ELSEWHERE IN THE SAME ROUND [round]: up to two lines of the answering round that stand under another verse or link. RULE E-ANS says an answer given there counts when, in its own words, it meets the stated meaning. The check judges; the code only picks.
      - Pick: key words are the stated meaning's words of five or more letters (first five letters, less a stop list) that the answer does not use. A line is a candidate when it has six or more words and does not name the pressed verse. A line is never served when it is a press line (PRESSED WITH, "-- MEANS:"), a heading, a LINK heading, a round header or a side header; when it begins with MEANS or with a number and a full stop or parenthesis (plain or bold); when it holds the stated meaning; when it holds six words in a row of the answer; or when it is already in THE SIDE'S OTHER LINES ON THIS VERSE. A line is scored by its key words, each weighted by one over the number of candidate lines holding it. A line is served when its score is 0.5 or more and it holds a key word found in three lines or fewer. A served line is cut at 400 characters, marked " ...". A line is never served when its text, in lower case with punctuation removed, lies inside THE ANSWER IN FULL of that item; the next line by score takes its place, and a section left with no line is not sent (pkgSRA rev 4; the answer block is passed to sraSame as a sixth argument; Shared Library +113, measured; on run 36212, 16 of 58 items lose one repeated line, 6 of them take the next line, and the same-round text is 1,880 characters shorter). Limits: the test reads the whole answer block, while the judge is shown that block cut at 900 characters, so a line past the cut can be left out of both places; a repeated line that is reworded is not caught.
      - When the call cap would push an item out, this section is dropped first, largest first, and then THE SIDE'S OTHER LINES ON THIS VERSE as before.
      - Size: Shared Library code +2,710. Prompt: only when a line is picked.
      - Limits: a restated claim of the other side or a grant under another verse is served when it shares the words, and the check judges it; the same answer in other words is not served; an answering sentence past the 400-character cut is lost; a key word found in four or more lines of the round picks nothing; on an error the section is left out without a mark; a side's own answer written as a numbered list item is not served. Whether DeepSeek's grade changes is unmeasured.
    - The added section never leaves an item unchecked: when the call cap (10 calls) would push an item out, the code drops the added section from the largest items first until no item is unchecked that would be checked without it (pkgA1N rev 3).
    - Cost: +35K to +146K check text per stored run, 0 to 2 more DeepSeek calls per run.
    - Limits:
      - A pointer in other words ("see link 2", "in link 4") is not followed.
      - A verse written without its book name is not found.
      - A link section is found only by a line opening "LINK N".
      - On a run near the call cap, some items are checked without the added section.
  - Size: Shared Library only (lines 3036, 3037, 3204 and 3219, counted from 0); code 2,921,231 to 2,923,434 = +2,203. Fixed prompt +322 characters per check call. Used by Judge Enforcer Build, Alt Judge Enforcer Build and R2 Alt Judge Enforcer Build. Check text per stored run +35,037 to +146,155 characters and 0 to 2 more DeepSeek calls.
  - Unproven until two clean runs; not yet run on the n8n runner. The first-run checks and the record-only list of check-versus-judge differences are in the grade plan (section 28). Whether the check changes any grade is not shown by any stored replay; it needs a paid replay.
- RULES WRITTEN AFTER s82 (each from the package named; where one differs from the text above it in this section, the later rule is right; library line numbers are those of the version the package was written on, so find a line by its text):
  - THE CLOSING ENFORCER (E-CLOSECON). Close Enforcer Build -> Close Enforcer Needed? -> Close Enforcer Call deepseek -> Close Enforcer Record, after Quote Report and before Regrade After Quotes?; made before the single judge and before the panel.
    - One call per side: Close Enforcer Build makes one item per side whose closing round has text, each with a one-side instruction, that side's candidates and that side's four rounds; the Call node runs once per item.
    - Two checks per side (pkgTWC rev 2): Close Enforcer Build sends each side's item twice, independently (the second copy at temperature 0.6). Close Enforcer Record reads both. A CONFIRMED answer stands only when the other copy also confirms the same side and link; otherwise it is written NOT CONFIRMED (one check of two), with a note. The second copy's agreeing row is written CONFIRMED (second check). Candidates and the logged prompt come from the first copy. The judge contradiction path (J rows) has its own two checks (pkgJC2 rev 2, below). Size: code +874 (Close Enforcer Build +216, Close Enforcer Record +658); one more DeepSeek call per side.
    - Inputs per side: its four rounds in full; its declared links (the [CR1] DECLARED CHAIN and the [DR3] LINK blocks); the candidates, read straight from the Oracle's OPPONENT'S CONTRADICTIONS lines in the [DR4] text (the contradictions field on C4, C4G and C4B Extract is a record only).
    - Reply line: `CLOSE-CONTRADICTION: <id|NEW> -- <DEFENDER|CHALLENGER> -- LINK <n> -- CONFIRMED | NOT A CONTRADICTION -- EARLIER [<round>] "<words>" -- CLOSING [<round 4>] "<words>" -- <why>`.
    - Round labels: "[CR3]" form; also "round N", "[round N]", and a bare number "[3]" or "3", each read as that line's own side's round N (CR N on a DEFENDER line, DR N on a CHALLENGER line). A number out of range, a verse reference or any other bracketed text is NOT CONFIRMED (form).
    - Code confirms a CONFIRMED line only when all hold: both quotes are found word for word in the rounds named; the earlier round is that side's round 1 to 3; the closing round is its round 4; the link is declared. Otherwise NOT CONFIRMED (form). A quote with "..." stands only when all its parts fall inside one sentence of the named round, in order. A candidate answered both CONFIRMED and NOT A CONTRADICTION is NOT CONFIRMED (conflicting answers) and breaks nothing.
    - Close Enforcer Record reads every Build item and every reply by position and judges each side's reply by itself: a failed call or a reply with no CLOSE-CONTRADICTION line logs a NOT CHECKED row naming that side; a NONE logs NONE FOUND for that side; "none" is true only when every side answered NONE. Its output item has one shape, so Panel Build, Build Independent Verdict and Judge Contradiction Build read it unchanged.
    - Failure: Close Enforcer Build and Record and Judge Contradiction Build and Record are wrapped; a failure in any of them writes one E-CLOSECON row "NOT CHECKED -- <reason>", confirms nothing, breaks no link, and the debate goes on. A NOT CHECKED row means the contradiction rule did not run; the run grade reports it as a failure of the check.
    - Log: debate_enforcer_log, kind enforcer_check, rule E-CLOSECON, seat_role panel, cure_status empty; the answer (CONFIRMED, CONFIRMED (second check), NOT CONFIRMED (one check of two), NOT A CONTRADICTION, NOT CONFIRMED (form), NOT CONFIRMED (conflicting answers), NONE FOUND, NO ANSWER, NOT CHECKED, NOT A CANDIDATE) leads cure_reason. Seats "close enforcer" and "judge contradiction enforcer". Alternate Merge adds the rows to enforcer_log.
    - Limits: the call's answers differ between sends; in the set C replay 3 of 23 confirmed lines were false or doubtful, and a confirmed line breaks a link by default (section 17).
    - LINK SET FROM THE CLOSING BLOCK (pkgCLK rev 3; Close Enforcer Record):
      - Close Enforcer Record sets a CONFIRMED row's link from the closing round itself, after the conflicting-answers check: it finds the one line of the closing round that holds the closing quotation, walks up from the line above it to the nearest heading (# to #### or a bold line; bold "**ANSWER: ...**" lines are skipped), and uses it when it begins with the side's own link (D<n> or LINK n for the defender, C<n> or LINK n for the challenger) and names one link. Otherwise the DeepSeek reply's link stands.
      - The walk stops at a "---" rule; a quotation found on two or more lines is not used.
      - A NEW pair is grouped for the conflicting-answers check by side and its two quotations, not by link: answered CONFIRMED on one link and NOT A CONTRADICTION on any link, it is NOT CONFIRMED (conflicting answers). A CONFIRMED row is not relinked when any row of the same side answers NOT A CONTRADICTION on the same two quotations. Lines are split on CR LF as well as LF.
      - The row keeps the reply's link as link_named; the note reads "LINK n set by the code: the closing quotation stands in the block headed for link n (the reply named LINK m; pkgCLK)".
      - The declared-link check applies to the link after the change.
      - Limits: a heading that does not start with the side's token, or names two links, leaves the reply's link; a quotation split across lines is not found; a missing heading in a round with no "---" rules falls to the heading above; the post-panel Judge Contradiction call is unchanged; a defender heading "## LINK 4 of the challenger" sets the defender's LINK 4; when the moved row lands on a link an earlier confirmed line already broke, the note reads "the same link is already broken by an earlier confirmed line" and link_named keeps the reply's link; a reclaim sentence standing in the D6 block ("I keep D1 to D6 as declared.") confirmed on LINK 1 is moved to LINK 6, by the prompt's rule as written; the close form "## ANSWERS (C3 against D2)" over bold ANSWER lines is never relinked; a candidate row and a NEW row on the same two quotations are not grouped.
  - CLOSING ENFORCER GROUPING, RELINK LIMITS AND THE CODE CUT (pkgCLG with pkgCDX; Close Enforcer Record; builds on pkgCLK rev 3 above):
    - Conflicting answers: a row is grouped both by candidate id and by its side and two quotations. A CONFIRMED row and any row answering NOT A CONTRADICTION on the same two quotations (candidate or NEW) are both left NOT CONFIRMED (conflicting answers).
    - pkgCLK's block relink is not applied to a code-built candidate (its "by" begins "the code", e.g. pkgRCL), nor when the closing quotation itself names a link of its side ("D1 to D6", "LINK 2").
    - The cut (pkgCDX): the unused declarations cecOracle, cecDeclared, cecDlinks, cecCblocks and cecLinks are removed from Close Enforcer Record, and "B.links||cecLinks(F)" becomes "B.links||{}". On every plant set and the six stored runs the node's output equals pkgCLG's.
    - Size: pkgCLG +65 code, pkgCDX -1,965 against pkgCLG, net -1,900 against s123 (measured); prompt 0.
    - Limits:
      - A DeepSeek NEW line quoting a bare "as declared" sentence in one block is still moved to that block's link.
      - A Close Enforcer Build item with no links: pkgCLG rebuilt them and broke D2; pkgCDX shows X2 NOT CONFIRMED (form) on LINK 3 and breaks nothing. The row is visible, not dropped. No stored run reaches this path.
      - pkgACX reading a record row that pkgCLG changes is not tested by plant.
  - CLOSING BLOCK UNDER EACH CANDIDATE (pkgCRB rev 2; Close Enforcer Build):
    - Under each named candidate the request shows the CLOSING BLOCK that holds its closing quotation. The whole quotation must be found in the close round (marks ignored; curly and straight apostrophes treated alike). Where it is found more than once, the hit not opened by a quotation mark is used.
    - The block runs from the heading above the quotation to the next heading, at most 1,500 characters before the quotation and 300 after it. A heading is a markdown heading, a line that opens with a bold phrase other than a row label ("**[L3]**", "**[L4] ...**"), "LINK n", "ANSWER:", or "Dn:" / "Cn:" / "Xn:" (bold or not). A bold lead-in on the quotation's own line does not open its block.
    - When several candidates share one closing quotation, the block is shown once and the later candidates read "CLOSING BLOCK: as under X<n>."
    - The instruction carries one sentence for the block: a closing sentence that draws its point from a row ("So", "Therefore", "Thus") asserts what that row says; if the earlier sentence withdrew, dropped or rested nothing on that row, the close asserts it again.
    - Candidates the judges name (Judge Contradiction Build; pkgCRX) are sent with the same shared rules, the same answer format and the same CLOSING BLOCK as the code candidates, built by the same block function; the judge request holds both sides in one call. A judge-named candidate whose closing quotation is not found in the close round gets no block. NEW items the enforcer finds itself carry no block.
    - Size of pkgCRX: +1,861 code (Judge Contradiction Build); prompt +587 to +1,650 per judge contradiction request on the five stored requests (measured).
    - Limit of pkgCRX: the shared rule text does not carry the words "of the same side"; the head line of the judge request says "the same debater". A pair that joins the two sides is read by hand at the run grade.
  - A WITHDRAWN VERSE CITED AGAIN AS A FOOTING IN THE CLOSE (pkgWVR rev 9; Close Enforcer Build):
    - The code names a candidate when a side, in round 2 or 3, withdraws a verse or stops resting on it, and its close cites the same verse as a footing of the side's own link. The candidate reads "named by the code (pkgWVR)" and the enforcer decides whether the close rests again on what the earlier line gave up. Where the finder cannot decide a form by its words, it names the pair and the enforcer judges the quoted sentence. At most 3 per side; a pair already named is not named again.
    - Withdrawn: "I withdraw / drop / set aside / retract <verses>", "I drop any reliance on <verses>", "I do not / no longer rest ... on <verses>", with one adverb allowed after "I" (now, hereby, therefore, thus, also, then, so); "<verses>: withdrawn" and "<verses> stay(s) / remain(s) / is / are withdrawn". Each verse of a list and of a range counts. "it" or "them" takes the verses of its own clause (after the last semicolon); when that clause is bare ("...; I withdraw it."), the clause before; when nothing stands before "I" in its own sentence, the sentence before, when at most six words and no quotation stand before "I" in its own sentence. A verse named only as the place of a clause, phrase, word, reading, sense, part, idiom or gloss ("the clause ... in Luke 1:27") is not taken by "it".
    - Not withdrawn: inside a quotation (a " after a digit is not a quotation mark); a clause opened by if, unless, provided, should, or once (not "once more/again"), with "And", "But" or "Even" allowed before it, and not "If anything"; the clause before a bare "it" clause when it is so opened; "if / unless / whether I withdraw"; "asks / wants / urges ... that I withdraw"; a condition right after the verse ("I withdraw Galatians 4:4 if he shows a row."); a partial withdrawal right after the verse ("alone", "by itself", "as sufficient", "on its own", "of itself", "only as"); "drop the claim/limb/reading that <verse>"; a list verse followed by "stays", "stands", "remains", "holds" or "is kept".
    - Footing in the close: "layer of" or "footing of/for" with the verse in the same clause, or "stands on" / "rests on" with the verse after the verb and no "not", "instead of" or "rather than" between. Where the verse opens the line as a short lead-in ("Galatians 4:4 -- D6.") the next sentence counts with it. Not named: a question; a clause that reports the opponent ("The challenger says ..."), has the opponent as subject ("The challenger rests on ..."; not across "that"), or names only the opponent's link; a past-tense report ("rested on").
    - Verses: a book name matches when one name is a prefix of the other (at least three letters) or by the short forms Jn, Mt, Mk, Lk, Phil (Philippians), Philem/Phlm (Philemon), Jud (Jude), Judg (Judges); a numbered book must carry the same number ("John 3:6" is not "1 John 3:6"); a bare "1:20" takes the book named before it on the line, else matches any book; a range in the close holds each of its verses; a list of bare verse numbers after a chapter:verse ("Matthew 1:18, 20", "Matthew 1:16, 18 and 20", "Matthew 1:18; 20", "Matthew 1:18 & 20") is read as verses of the same chapter, on both sides, but not when a book name follows the number ("Luke 9:34, 1 Samuel 16:13"). The link is taken from the sentence, else from the nearest heading of the close ("D6", "LINK 6").
    - A verse withdrawn in round 2, taken up again in round 3 and cited in the close is named.
    - The note to the enforcer: each named pair carries a note for that pair only. If the earlier line withdrew the verse itself or stopped resting on it (also when withdrawn "as" a witness, "as load-bearing" or "as a proof") and the close names it again as a layer or footing of the same link, the close rests on it even with less weight ("not alone", "a supporting layer", "not weight-bearing"); a lesser weight is still a rest. The note does not apply where only a clause, phrase, word, idiom or reading of the verse was given up and the verse kept, nor to any other pair.
    - Size: +8,639 code (Close Enforcer Build); prompt about +586 for each named pair (measured on the run 32924 request).
    - Regression set: repo pkgWVR/regress/; every revision passes all of it before it is posted.
    - Limits:
      - A footing stated with another word ("holds", "supports", "carries") or in a heading list ("D4: KEPT (...)") is not found; a verse cited only in a close heading line is not named.
      - A withdrawal scoped to one link is named against another link; the enforcer decides.
      - "<verse> stands and the <other> reading is weak. I withdraw it." is named; a condition the finder does not know ("Until ...", "In case ...") is named and left to the enforcer.
      - A bare number followed by a lowercase word ("Matthew 1:18, 20 years") is read as a verse; the enforcer clears it. A withdrawal with no "I" or "we" before the verb ("I keep Matthew 1:18 and withdraw 20") is not read.
      - The enforcer's own NEW lines carry no note and may confirm a pair the text does not support (replay of run 32924: a link 5 line on the reach of "till" in Matthew 1:25, on 1 of 2 sends). Every confirmed closing line is read by hand at the run grade.
  - A WITHDRAWAL IS READ BY ITS MEANING (pkgCEW; Close Enforcer Build and Judge Contradiction Build, the same paragraph in the shared rules of each):
    - The shared rules say: "Read a withdrawal by its meaning, not its words: a closing sentence that says again in other words what the earlier sentence withdrew, dropped or gave up, or rests its reason on it, asserts it again. "Differs only in wording" clears two sentences that make the same claim; it never clears a closing sentence that makes again a claim the debater withdrew. A closing sentence that states only what the debater kept, or rests the same conclusion on other rows, does not assert it again. When the earlier sentence rests no weight on a verse or row, a closing sentence that uses that verse or row as a footing for the same link or the same purpose asserts it again."
    - The paragraph stands before the sentence that begins "Read each candidate's closing sentence together with the rows of its CLOSING BLOCK". It reaches code-named candidates, judge-named candidates and the enforcer's own search alike.
