    - Size: code +664 in each of the two nodes; 660 characters in every closing-enforcer request and every judge-contradiction request.
    - Limits: two sends of one request can still answer differently. On stored requests, each on one send of two: a narrowing that dropped only part of a claim ("throughout Israel") was confirmed against a closing sentence that kept the rest, and a true line (a dropped limb against "Nothing is conceded or narrowed.") was cleared. A pair of the form "I drop the wording ... I rest it on these rows" against a kept conclusion has not been sent with this paragraph.
  - LAYER ROWS OF THE CLOSE, AND "REST NO WEIGHT ON" (pkgWVL rev 2; Close Enforcer Build; widens the finder of the item "A WITHDRAWN VERSE CITED AGAIN AS A FOOTING IN THE CLOSE"):
    - Two more forms count as withdrawn: "I rest no weight on <verses>" and "I rest nothing on <verses>", with "now" allowed before "on".
    - Second pass over the close. After the first pass (the footing words of the item above), the finder reads the close again for layer rows: a line whose first sentence opens "[L<n>] <verses>:" or "[L<n>] On <verses>:" names each verse of that list as a layer of the link. The link is the side's own link token in that sentence, else the nearest heading above it. The cap of 3 per side covers both passes.
    - Not named by the second pass: a line that says the side does not rest or stand on it, no longer, sets aside or withdraws; a line with "rest no weight" or "rest nothing"; a sentence that reports the opponent or names only the opponent's link; a verse and link the first pass already named.
    - Size: with the item below, code +1,274 in Close Enforcer Build.
    - Limits: a layer row that names the withdrawn verse only to answer the opponent, with no report word, is named and left to the enforcer. A row whose verses do not open the sentence ("[L4] The seed is the woman's (Genesis 3:15).") is not read by the second pass.
  - A WITHDRAWAL COVERS ONLY ITS NAMED USE (pkgWVS rev 3a; Close Enforcer Build, the note of each pair named by the finder above):
    - The note of each pair adds, with the verse named: "By the operator's ruling, a withdrawal covers only its named use: a link or purpose that the earlier line itself names as what <verse> is withdrawn from ("for the exclusion", "from D1"); a close that uses <verse> only under another link, for a purpose that line left open, does not rest again on it. A reason given for the withdrawal ("since ..."), a time, "the case", weight, load-bearing, proof, or what the earlier line says it keeps is not a named use, and a withdrawal that names no use covers every use of <verse>; naming <verse> again as a layer or footing of the link it was withdrawn from is a rest, whatever point the close draws from it".
    - The sentence is in the note only. A pair the judges name and a pair the enforcer finds by itself do not carry it.
    - Size: about 669 characters for each named pair (counted with "Genesis 3:15" as the verse).
    - Limits: the enforcer has taken the link heading above an earlier line for a named use, and so cleared a true line, on one send of two (run 32749, link 5: "I drop Galatians 4:4 and Genesis 3:15 as load-bearing." under a LINK 4 heading, against a D5 row of the close that draws its point from Genesis 3:15); the earlier line itself names no use. An enforcer NEW line has confirmed a pair whose closing sentence names neither verse of the earlier line, on one send of two (run 35856, link 6). Every confirmed closing line, and every cleared pair of a withdrawn verse, is read by hand at the run grade.
  - A JUDGE-FOUND CONTRADICTION NEEDS TWO CHECKS (pkgJC2 rev 2; Judge Contradiction Build and Judge Contradiction Record):
    - Judge Contradiction Build returns two copies of the check request: copy 1 as before and copy 2 at temperature 0.6.
    - Judge Contradiction Record reads both replies. A CONFIRMED row stays CONFIRMED only when the other check confirms the same candidate id on the same side and link. Otherwise it is written "NOT CONFIRMED (one check of two)", its note names the candidate id, and the link is not broken. The second check's agreeing row is written "CONFIRMED (second check)". Two different candidates on one link, each confirmed by one check, break nothing.
    - A check with no answer line or no reply gets its own log row and confirms nothing.
    - "CONFIRMED (second check)" and "NOT CONFIRMED (one check of two)" rows add no fault ledger row.
    - Size: code +1,934 (Judge Contradiction Build +256, Judge Contradiction Record +1,678); prompt text 0; one more DeepSeek request on each run where a judge names a candidate (126,744 to 142,924 prompt characters on the 7 stored calls).
    - Limits: the added time is not measured; whether deepseek-flash answers differently at temperature 0.6 is not measured; the pairing of replies to requests when one request fails was not run; when two requests were built and one reply arrives, no row says a check is missing. Not exercised by a run.
  - A PAIR ABOUT THE OPPONENT'S CONTRADICTIONS IS NOT CONFIRMED (pkgOPC; Close Enforcer Record and Judge Contradiction Record, the same line in the CONFIRMED form check of each):
    - A CONFIRMED close-contradiction line is set NOT CONFIRMED (form) when its earlier quotation or its closing quotation speaks of the opponent's contradictions: the words "OPPONENT'S CONTRADICTION" or "<the other side>'S CONTRADICTION", with or without "OWN" before "CONTRADICTION", in any letter case, with a straight or curly apostrophe or none. Such a quotation makes no claim of the debater's own link, so it breaks no link.
    - The row note reads "a quotation is about the opponent's contradictions, not a claim of the <side>'s own link (pkgOPC)". The row is recorded, not dropped.
    - Why it exists: the close heading "OPPONENT'S CONTRADICTIONS: NONE." was paired with a round 2 press on the other side and confirmed against the challenger's link 6.
    - Size: code +308 in each of the two nodes, +616; prompt 0.
    - Limits:
      - A true reclaim is refused when either of its quotations names the other side's contradiction (for example "I keep D3 as declared, whatever the challenger's contradiction charge says."). The row is visible with the pkgOPC note; the run grade hand-reads every such row.
      - A charge on the opponent in other words ("The defender contradicts itself") is not caught.
      - The whole node was not run on rebuilt rounds; the line was run as taken from the built node.
  - RECLAIM CANDIDATES AT THE CLOSE (pkgRCL rev 3; Close Enforcer Build):
    - Text:
      - Reclaim candidates at the close (pkgRCL rev 3). A part left out of a narrowed restatement counts as dropped.
      - Close Enforcer Build looks for a sentence in the defender's close round [CR4] that keeps the chain or links "as declared" (for example "My chain stands as declared, D1 to D6"). For each defender link that was narrowed earlier and is covered by that sentence, it hands the closing enforcer one candidate. DeepSeek decides each candidate; the code only proposes.
      - Size: code +2,154 in Close Enforcer Build only. Fixed prompt 0. The enforcer prompt grows only when the case occurs: about +1,239 characters in run 34852 (MEASURED).
      - Limits. Each one is a miss that leaves the earlier behaviour; no false candidate was found. These forms are not read:
        - "as declared, not as narrowed"
        - words in curly quotation marks
        - a sentence broken across lines
        - "as they should"
        - a question followed by its answer
        - a clause that names the other side
        - the heading form "LINK 4 (D4): CONCEDED"
      - A link narrowed and later restored still gets a candidate, and the enforcer decides it. One confirmed sentence can break every narrowed link at once, as the ruling applies.
    - Size: Close Enforcer Build only; code 2,923,434 to 2,925,588 = +2,154 on s129. Fixed prompt 0. The enforcer prompt grows only when the case occurs: about +1,239 characters in run 34852 (measured).
    - Unproven until two clean runs; not yet run on the n8n runner. First-run checks in the grade plan (section 28). The DeepSeek call itself was not made in any grade: whether the enforcer confirms a candidate is NOT SHOWN offline.
  - CLOSE ENFORCER, BRACKETED AND PARENTHESIZED LINK HEADINGS (pkgRCH; Close Enforcer Build):
    - Text:
      - Reclaim candidates at the close (pkgRCL rev 3, heading reader widened by pkgRCH). Close Enforcer Build finds the earlier rounds' narrowing lines by their heading: "LINK N: NARROWED TO", "LINK N: CONCEDED", "LINK N: WITHDRAWN" or "LINK N: DROPPED", in any letter case, after * and _ are stripped. Since pkgRCH it also reads one bracket or one parenthesis between the link number and the colon: "LINK 2 [premise 3]: NARROWED TO", "LINK 13 [§VII]: CONCEDED", "LINK 4 (premise 4, D2): CONCEDED", "LINK 5 (D5): CONCEDED", "LINK 2 ("carried by the woman"): CONCEDED", "LINK 7 (D5, Galatians 4:4): CONCEDED". The link number is the number after LINK, not the premise number inside the bracket.
      - Size: code +32 in Close Enforcer Build only; fixed prompt 0. The enforcer request grows only when the case occurs (+565 characters on run 35067).
      - Limits: two brackets in a row, a bracket and a parenthesis together, or words between the bracket and the colon are not read. A heading with no colon keyword is never read.
      - First-run check: when [CR3] or [DR3] has a bracketed or parenthesized narrowing heading and the close keeps that link "as declared", the closing enforcer's request lists a candidate for it; otherwise the request is byte-equal to s130. No Close Enforcer Build error.
    - Size: Close Enforcer Build only; code +32; fixed prompt 0. The enforcer request grows only when the case occurs: +565 characters on run 35067 (measured).
    - Limits: also not read: two groups, nested brackets, braces, a dash, and D/C labels. Why: on run 35067 the reclaim check (pkgRCL rev 3) raised no candidate for "LINK 2 [premise 3]: NARROWED TO"; the outcome was unchanged. WALL-4802
    - Unproven until two clean runs; not yet run on the n8n runner. The first-run check is the last paragraph of the text above and is in the grade plan (section 29).
  - CODE-FOUND CLOSING PAIRS (pkgCNP rev 4; Close Enforcer Build):
    - A function cecDeny runs after the Oracle's candidates and the reclaim candidates (pkgRCL). Its candidates join the "CANDIDATES ALREADY NAMED" list, marked "by the code (pkgCNP)". Nothing else in the enforcer changes; the enforcer still decides each candidate.
    - Closing sentences, in the side's round 4 (CR4 for the defender, DR4 for the challenger):
      - Denial: "I/we narrow (concede, withdraw, drop, give up, surrender, retract, yield) nothing / no link", "Nothing is/has been ... narrowed (conceded ...)", "No link (premise, limb, part, claim, ground, witness) of mine is ... narrowed", "I have not withdrawn anything", "without narrowing anything".
      - Keep-all: "I keep" a span, or a span that "stands / holds / remains", where the span is D1 to D6 (C1 to C6), "all/every/each (six) links", "my chain" or "the chain", ending "as numbered / as stated / as first stated / as written / as given / as I gave (stated, declared) it / exactly as stated / unchanged / intact / in full / whole / entire / undiminished / untouched". "As declared" alone is left to pkgRCL.
      - More denials: "Not one link has been narrowed", "None of my links is narrowed", "No concession has been made", "Nothing I said has been withdrawn".
      - Not taken: inside a quotation (odd number of quote marks before it), a question, a conditional (if, unless, whether, would, should, could, might), naming the other side, with an exception word (beyond, except, further, more, else, new, again, here, since, after, in this close), or negated before the match ("I do not say that nothing is conceded").
      - A close that names links ("I concede nothing on D5", "I keep D1 to D4 as numbered") pairs only with those links. A link the same sentence marks as narrowed ("D5 stands as narrowed", "D5 as narrowed") is never paired. An exception before the match in the same clause ("Apart from LINK 5, I keep ...") stops the pair. A LINK "?" withdrawal pairs with a named range only when the range covers every declared link.
      - Two rules for keep-all closes: (1) a link the sentence names on its own, outside a range ("Save D5, ...", "D5 aside, ...", "I keep D1 to D4 and D6"), is set apart and is not paired by that sentence; (2) a keep-all sentence that itself carries a word of the narrowing family (narrowed, amended, revised, restated, conceded, withdrawn, dropped) outside the matched phrase is not paired. A set-apart link also stops the sentence's LINK "?" pairs, since the "?" may be that link. Both rules apply to keep-alls only; a denial that names a link ("I concede nothing on D5") still pairs with that link. The enforcer still reads the whole close and can name such a pair itself.
    - Earlier lines, in the same side's rounds 2 and 3:
      - "LINK n: NARROWED TO / CONCEDED / WITHDRAWN / DROPPED" lines.
      - "I withdraw / drop / concede / give up / no longer claim ... <object>" anywhere in a sentence (not "I withdraw nothing", not conditional, not quoted). Its link is the nearest "LINK n" or "Dn" heading; with none, the link is "?" and the enforcer is asked to name it.
    - Pairs: each closing sentence with each earlier line of the same side, only for links the side declared. One earlier line per link (the latest round). At most 8 per side. A pair already named (same side, link and closing sentence; for "?" the same earlier text) is not repeated.
    - Size: Close Enforcer Build code 8,315 to 15,050 (+6,735); prompt text 0. More candidates make the enforcer request longer.
  - CONTRADICTIONS A JUDGE FINDS: Judge Contradiction Build -> Needed? -> Call deepseek -> Record, between Panel Payloads and Panel Cure Needed?. Candidates: each judge's CONTRADICTION line whose later sentence stands in that side's closing round (panel and single judge), and each contradiction question of the dry question round. A pair the pre-panel enforcer already answered is not sent again; no candidate means no call. Same prompt rules and code checks; the call answers candidates only. A confirmed line breaks its link in every panel, reserve and alternate seat's recompute. A contradiction confirmed only after the panel does NOT change the single judge's result (the single judge is graded before the panel sits); one confirmed before the judges sit breaks the link for the single judge too.
  - SHOWN TO THE JUDGES (pkgCEC):
    - Panel Build and Build Independent Verdict add a CONFIRMED CLOSING CONTRADICTIONS block under the side's close round. It holds both quotes and the link, and says:
    - "This link is BROKEN by the contradiction rule; the system sets every collision line of Dn to DEFENDER LINK BROKEN after you judge."
    - "Grade every collision line as usual on its arguments, these links included; do not write a grade for the contradiction itself."
  - CLOSING BREAK IN THE RECOMPUTE (pkgCEC; library cecBroken2024, cureRecompute, sbApply2024):
    - **Each seat's recompute.** It applies after the cure, and the send-back recompute applies it too. Every collision line of the broken link reads `<SIDE> LINK BROKEN`, with `[AUDIT: LINK n BROKEN BY A CONFIRMED CLOSING CONTRADICTION -- <SIDE> -- EARLIER [..] ".." -- CLOSING [..] ".."]`. The trigger reads `CLOSE-CONTRADICTION <line> (<old> to <new>)`.
    - **Recomputed after it:** counts, standings, CLAUSE, verdict and RESULT, by the section 8 definitions and the tie rule.
    - **When both sides' breaks reach one line:** the defender's break holds, and the trigger names the challenger's.
    - **A broken link that no line names:** it counts as one broken line.
    - **Seats with no rule item:** they still recompute when a contradiction is confirmed. This covers the panel, the alternates (rounds 1 and 2) and the single judge.
    - **No item or removal:** the break raises no judge item and removes no seat.
  - THE RECOMPUTED RESULT NO LONGER CARRIES A COUNT OF COLLISION LINES THAT STAND GRADED (pkgSRH; Shared Library line 1354, read by Alternate Merge Seat, R2 Alternate Merge Seat, Cure Check and Panel Cure Check):
    - Change: library line 1354 no longer adds ", N collision line(s) stand graded CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN" (or the zero form, "no collision line stands graded ...") to the reason of the recomputed RESULT. CHALLENGE STANDING stays, as before. Nothing else changes; no node and no part of the transcript reads the phrase.
    - Why: the count was right when written at the cure and went stale later in the same run (run 35173: the text carried "5 collision line(s) stand graded CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN" while only one such line remained). WALL-5008
    - Size (measured): code -138 (four readers, -138 each); prompt 0. In the joint with pkgA21: code -44.
    - See the s144 entry in section 18.
    - Unproven and limit: the recomputed CHALLENGE STANDING could in principle go stale the same way; not seen in stored text. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 39). Limits: see section 17.
  - KEYSTONE AFTER A CLOSING BREAK, AND THE ENGINE'S BREAK LINES (pkgKSR, pkgECB rev 3; the sentence on the judge's own break line is replaced by pkgRTE rev 3, part EJ, below):
    - KEYSTONE AFTER A CLOSING BREAK (pkgKSR; section 9, RECOMPUTE, after the closing-contradiction sentence): when a confirmed closing contradiction breaks the defender's keystone link Dn and the judge's KEYSTONE line reads STANDS, the cure recompute replaces that line with "KEYSTONE: Dn -- BROKEN -- LINK n BROKEN BY A CONFIRMED CLOSING CONTRADICTION" and adds "KEYSTONE Dn (STANDS to BROKEN)" to the trigger. It reads every KEYSTONE line form the live line reader reads, a leading bullet included. The judge's own line is kept in a SUPERSEDED LINE audit. The K-RECHK K-CMT row that the new line raises is record-only.
    - After a send-back on a confirmed broken link Dn (pkgKSR guards): the engine does not derive KEYSTONE Dn STANDS from the corrected collision lines (K2); does not send a KEYSTONE item asking the judge to unbreak Dn (K3, K5); and raises no RULING card item (E-GATE D38 KEY) against the KEYSTONE line on Dn (K6). The confirmed contradiction decides the link, whatever the collision lines read after the send-back.
    - THE ENGINE'S BREAK LINES ARE NOT SENT BACK (pkgECB; section 9, SEND-BACK AND APPLY, after "each item goes back to its judge"): the three Send Back Build nodes send no item on (a) a collision line of link n that carries the engine mark "[AUDIT: LINK n BROKEN BY A CONFIRMED CLOSING CONTRADICTION -- <side>" for a confirmed side and link; (b) the FORCED line of a pair whose collision line is withheld under (a); (c) the engine's own line "KEYSTONE: Dn -- BROKEN -- LINK n BROKEN BY A CONFIRMED CLOSING CONTRADICTION" for a confirmed defender link. Each withheld item is listed in sb_not_sent with the reason "the engine's closing-contradiction break; a send-back does not undo it". A judge's own line that quotes those words, or a mark for a link not confirmed, is still sent.
  - E-COL IS NOT ASKED ABOUT A BREAK THE ENGINE MADE BY A CONFIRMED CLOSING CONTRADICTION (pkgECS; the three Judge Enforcer Builds through the Shared Library):
    - Text:
      - Where: the three enforcer builders (Judge Enforcer Build, Alt Judge Enforcer Build, R2 Alt Judge Enforcer Build), through the Shared Library.
      - What changed: before the DeepSeek E-COL items are built, a LINK BROKEN collision line (not a FORCED line) is set aside when it grades a link that the Close Enforcer or the Judge Contradiction check CONFIRMED broken by a closing contradiction for that side. The builders use the same test the send-back uses (pkgECB, lines __cc and __ecC). A line that is set aside is not sent to DeepSeek. Each judge who wrote it gets a code row (rule E-COL, kind enforcer_check) with the reason "NOT ASKED (code, pkgECS): the line grades a link the engine broke by a confirmed closing contradiction; E-COL does not apply to that break, as the send-back does not send it (pkgECB)". The three stubs now name Close Enforcer Record and Judge Contradiction Record, so the task runner sends those two nodes.
      - Why: E-COL asks whether the judge's break rests on an answer that cannot agree with a pressed meaning. When the engine has broken the link by the contradiction rule, DeepSeek reads the break as having no such ground and answers DISAGREE (right: STANDS). In the 12 stored exports this happened on 47 set-aside seat-lines (41 distinct texts) and an AGREE came back on 3. The send-back already refuses to send these rows (pkgECB), so they were wrong records and wasted enforcer input.
      - Size (measured): Shared Library +1,371 characters (two reused library lines added to three node maps, the loop test, the code row); the three stubs +59 each; all Code nodes +1,548; prompt instruction text 0; enforcer input smaller in the stored runs that have such lines except run 34726, +353 characters (run 35173: -6,503 characters; run 35067: -49,237 characters and one call fewer; run 34703: -34,625 characters and one call fewer).
      - Limits: a FORCED line on the same link is still asked. The code rows are written to enf428.code_violations with the other code rows; nothing reads that count and the rows count toward nothing (the send-back takes only rows that begin "ENFORCER DISAGREE" or "NO ANSWER"; the uncured count and the E-COL REVIEW line come only from send-back items; the rows carry cure_status null; record_enforcer_log is a plain insert). A judge's other ground on such a line (a pressed witness) is no longer checked; it changes nothing, because the link is broken by the rule either way, but 3 correct AGREE rows in run 34726 would no longer be written.

  - E-KEY AND E-RES ARE NOT ASKED ABOUT A BREAK THAT RESTS ON A CONFIRMED CLOSING CONTRADICTION (pkgEKR; the three Judge Enforcer Builds through the Shared Library, lines 3100, 3108, 3131 and 3159; needs pkgECS):

  - THE E-SYN LIST OF A SIDE'S OWN GRANTS (pkgSGR; the giveUp function, Shared Library line 3163, and its call site, line 3169, in the three Judge Enforcer Builds):
    - Text:
      - **What it changes.** The DeepSeek enforcer's E-SYN item checks a synthesis sentence that says a side granted, conceded, dropped, withdrew or narrowed something. The item shows DeepSeek "THAT SIDE'S OWN SENTENCES THAT GIVE GROUND", built by giveUp (Shared Library line 3163) from that side's four rounds. pkgSGR changes which paragraphs giveUp lists.
        - 1. It now lists grant forms the old test missed:
            - "It is granted that"
            - "This is granted"
            - "<verse> is granted"
            - "which I granted"
            - "I have conceded"
            - "I keep my earlier grants"
            - "I keep what I granted"
            - "I keep dropped"
            - "stays dropped"
            - a sentence that opens with the label "Grant.", "Granted:" or "Concession:"
        - 2. It no longer lists the other side's grants:
            - Quoted text is not read.
            - A sentence whose subject is the other side is not read. Examples: "the defender grants it to", "The defender narrowed", "conceded by the challenger".
            - So a challenger's list of the defender's grants ("G3 [CR4] "I grant that ..."") no longer fills the challenger's own list.

        - Code: +770 characters in the Shared Library.
        - Prompt fixed text: 0.
        - DeepSeek input, judged from the replay of the 12 stored exports: -5,873 characters in total.
          - 35173: +8,817, and no extra call.
      - **Limits.**
        - A gerund grant ("Granting that ...") is not read, now or before.
        - A grant inside quotation marks in the side's own text is not read. A side that quotes its own earlier grant is still listed when it states the grant outside the quotes.
        - The 3500-character cap can push lower-ranked entries out (21 entries over 38 items in the replay). This happened before too.
        - Record-only trial: no seat effect.
        - Whether DeepSeek's verdict changes needs a paid replay. It is not measured.
    - Limits found by plants: "If this were granted" and "Nothing is granted here." are read as grants.

  - REPLACED LINES ARE READ (pkgVP1; RECORD-ONLY; md_notes_rulings 459):
    - **E-ANS and E-PREM rows:** the engine's cure reading of the replaced line is written to cure_status (CURED or UNCURED).
    - **E-OOR rows:** code runs the E-OOR checks again on the replaced line and writes CURED or UNCURED with the reason.
    - **Second reading (E-REREAD):** Send Back Record -> Reread Build -> Reread Needed? -> Reread Call deepseek -> Reread Record -> Gate Build (the same four nodes on the Alt and R2 Alt paths).

  - THE SECOND READING DOES NOT ASK DEEPSEEK ABOUT A RETRACTION (pkgRRT; Reread Build, Alt Reread Build, R2 Alt Reread Build and the three Record nodes; the pkgVP1 second reading, record-only): the lines of the pkgVP1 entry about the second reading ("Asked: each replaced line whose send-back reason opens (right: and whose answer is CHANGE") are read together with this entry: a replaced line that begins "RETRACTED:" is no longer asked.
    - Text:

A retraction carries no grade to read again. Each line set aside is written as a reread row "SECOND READING NOT MADE (pkgVP1; RECORD-ONLY): the replaced line is a retraction, which carries no grade to read again (pkgRRT): <the line>". The Record also writes the not-made rows when the Build skips the call altogether, so that a reread in which every line is a retraction is still recorded.

Stored forms (25 stored send-back retraction answers in the 12 exports): "RETRACTED: Dx vs Cy" 20, "RETRACTED: FORCED: Dx vs Cy" 3, "RETRACTED: Dx vs Cy -- reason" 2; all caught.   - ANSWER OPEN (pkgVP1; E-GATE M, LIVE):
    - **Rule:** "An answer that MISSES or FAILS leaves the witness unanswered (md_notes_rulings 372)". A judge that grades an ANSWER CHECK on a proven press MISSES or FAILS and leaves that link's collision UNDERDETERMINED, with no line of that link graded LINK BROKEN for the pressed side, gets a gate item.
    - **Link of an answer check:** a [DR2] premise entry labelled "LINK k" gives Dk; an unlabelled defender entry gives no item; challenger entry j gives Cj.
    - **The judge keeps the decision (ruling 428).** The item is cleared by either:
      1. changing the answer check to MEETS; or
      2. changing the collision line to LINK BROKEN for the pressed side, with one FORCED line for that pair as the reason of the GATE line: `FORCED: D<i> vs C<j> -- MEANS [<round>]: "..." -- ANSWER [<round>]: "..." -- FAILS: "..."`, each quotation word for word in the round named (ruling 411). The engine writes the FORCED line under the collision line and derives the counts, the standing and the RESULT.
    - **Several items on one link:** one cured change to LINK BROKEN, with its FORCED line, answers every ANSWER OPEN item that no longer stands after it; those need no GATE line. A change back to MEETS cures only its own item.
    - **With the closing contradiction rule:** a link broken by a confirmed closing contradiction is read as BROKEN for that side; no ANSWER OPEN item is raised on it.
    - **UNCURED:** KEEP, silence, a change that stays UNDERDETERMINED, or LINK BROKEN without a FORCED line that passes.
    - **Limits:** the gate runs only for a seat that had send-back items; an answered-with-a-fit collision does not count toward a defender win (row 459; not built).
  - ASSUMPTION CHARGE CHECK (pkgVP4):
    - ASSUMPTION CHARGE CHECK (pkgVP4, E-ASSUME; P4 of the verdict proposal, row 459; TRIAL, record-only). After the panel and the judge-contradiction check, one DeepSeek call reads every UNSUPPORTED-L4 premise that carries ASSUMES (the list against the defender in [DR2], against the challenger in [CR3]) against the answering side's round 3 LINK block. For each assumed fact it answers STATED (with the block's own quoted verse words), NOT STATED, or INFERRED ACT (never an ASSUMES, DG-61). Code is the second reader: every proper name of the fact must stand among the block's quoted verse words, read together (Commitment 3). A judge that SPARED the premise gets an E-ASSUME item only when both readers say NOT STATED. In the TRIAL nothing is sent back and no seat is removed; one log row per fact carries both readers' answers and each judge's grade. The judges' grades are read after the panel and before the cure, so a grade changed at the cure is recorded as first given. Log rows carry seat_role panel (single for a single-judge item) and an empty cure_status; the reading leads cure_reason.
  - THE ASSUMES FORM IS NEVER VOID FOR ITS WORDS, IN DEEPSEEK'S RULE E-VOID (pkgA21; Shared Library line 3205, read by Judge Enforcer Build, Alt Judge Enforcer Build and R2 Alt Judge Enforcer Build; RULE E-PREM reaches it through its pointer to RULE E-VOID):
    - Change: DeepSeek's RULE E-VOID gains one sentence, which carries the judges' Commitment 6 wording: "A charge in the form ASSUMES: ... -- ROWS GIVE: ... is never VOID for its words (ruling 421)." Nothing else changes. Every enforcer request carries the whole RULES text, and RULE E-PREM already points VOID rulings to RULE E-VOID, so the sentence reaches E-PREM items through that pointer (not tested on DeepSeek).
    - Why: the judges' Commitment 6 (library line 2974) ends "A charge written as ASSUMES: ... -- ROWS GIVE: ... is never voided for its words; you check it against the rows (ruling 421).", but DeepSeek's RULE E-VOID (line 3205) and RULE E-PREM (line 3210) did not carry it, and E-PREM quotes the VOID rule ("charged in terms stronger than its quote, is VOID") with no exemption. WALL-5011
    - Size (measured): code +94 (one library line); prompt +94 characters per DeepSeek enforcer request that carries the rules text. Replay over the 12 stored exports: 121 requests, +10,716 characters in all (about +890 per run); run 35173 +1,128 over 12 requests. The three enforcer builders rebuild +94 each.     - See the s144 entry in section 18.
