  - BARE ANSWER CHECK (pkgVP3 rev 3, pkgVPD rev 2):
    - Send-back, P3 TRIAL (pkgVP3 rev 3): an E-ANS or E-HOLD DeepSeek DISAGREE is a two-reader item when a code check finds the answering side's answer to the same witness bare: every line naming the verse in that round and later rounds is only a grant or a short deferral, and a deferral to a LINK is followed in that round and then every round of the side (a LINK block with its own footing or 25 or more words, or no block found, is not bare; a deferral to a round that holds text is not bare). For one run it is record-only: each such send-back row notes whether the judge's reply cured it or WOULD REMOVE the seat, and the run report lists every WOULD REMOVE row. Enforcement needs the operator's approval after the trial.
    - pkgVPD rev 2: in the pkgVP3 TRIAL code check, a deferral to a LINK no round of the side holds defers to nothing; a deferral to a same-side round is followed once and makes the answer substance only when that round answers the verse with substance. Before a named same-side round is read as holding no answer, the verse is looked for there by other marks: the reference written another way ("1:34", "Luke 1, verse 34", "v. 34") or a run of three words of the verse's KJV text (Cited Verses). Each followed deferral is named in the TRIAL note. Limits: a paraphrase with no three KJV words and no reference is not found; a bare grant under an another-way reference is read as an answer.
  - READING A KEEP ON E-ANS (record-only, ruling 428): a KEEP is read CURED when its reason quotes six words in a row of the answering side's own words. Not counted toward the six:
    - the flagged answer when it is bare (only grants, or a short deferral): the answer is cut out of each round before the search, so quoting it back, or a window running into it, is not a fuller answer; with a bare flagged answer the six words must also lie outside every bare grant or deferral sentence of that side;
    - any verse of six words or more that the side quoted on a verse line; and, on a line with no verse reference, a quoted span of six words or more whose words stand whole and in order in the KJV text of a verse the run serves (Cited Verses). Verse text is never taken from what a debater writes, so a debater's own quoted claim beside a reference is not scripture.
    - Sentence tests: opening labels and bullets are ignored. A grant joined to a further clause of its own is not bare; a clause that only continues the grant, only says the side keeps its position (also when it names a link by number), or only names a verse as agreeing is not a clause of its own. A sentence of 15 words or fewer that reads "X is answered under / with / in / by <verse>" and ends at the reference is a deferral when X is only a verse reference, this, it, that or the same.
    - Verse labels are taken off before the bare test: a lead-in ("On / In / At / For / Under / As to / Regarding Luke 3:23, I grant ..."), a label ending in a comma, and a bold label whose emphasis closes before or after the punctuation ("- **On Job 14:1**, I grant ..."). The reason text reads "another bare grant, affirmation or deferral".
    - Limits of the KJV reading: a verse the run does not serve; a quotation crossing two verses; wording that differs from the KJV text; the served text carries the translators' bracketed notes.
  - AN E-ANS SEND-BACK CHANGED TO MEETS ON THE SAME ANSWER IS CURED (pkgAMG; the cure reader enf428Cure, Shared Library line 2669, used by Send Back Record, Alt Send Back Record and R2 Alt Send Back Record; record-only):
    - Text:
      - **What it changes.** This is the E-ANS cure reader, enf428Cure (Shared Library line 2669). It is used by Send Back Record, Alt Send Back Record and R2 Alt Send Back Record.
      - Take this case:
        - the judge's ANSWER CHECK line graded the answer MISSES or FAILS;
        - DeepSeek's E-ANS check said "(right: MEETS)";
        - the send-back reply changes the line to MEETS, quoting the same answer or a shorter cut of it.
      - That reply is CURED: the change of grade is the cure. The test "the CHANGE TO MEETS quotes the flagged answer again" applies to a line already graded MEETS, which must cite a fuller answer.
      - A flagged MEETS line keeps the old test, and so does a change to MEETS that the enforcer did not call MEETS. Every other E-ANS rule is unchanged: the answer must still be found word for word in the answering side's named round, and the verse and side must match.
      - **Why.** Grok's 5 E-ANS rows in run 35173 read UNCURED although grok made the change DeepSeek asked for. The stored exports hold 14 such rows: 33149 1, 33371 1, 33490 1, 33497 1, 34780 4, 34852 1, 35173 5.
      - **Size, measured.**
        - Code: +203 characters in the Shared Library. Each of the 3 Send Back Record stubs rebuilds +200.
        - Prompt: 0.
      - **Effect.** Record-only. In the replay, nothing in the Send Back Record output changes except these cure rows.
      - **Limits.**
        - It reads the enforcer's grade from the item's "(right: MEETS)" text. An item without it keeps the old test.
        - Observation, not built: a FAILS line that DeepSeek called MEETS is also CURED by a CHANGE TO MISSES. That is the rule before and after.
    - Why it is record-only: enf428Cure is called only in those three stubs; seat removal (Alternate Merge Seat), Gate Build and Reread Build read sb_items, which is equal in every replay; sb_rows go only to the enforcer log and the transcript.
    - See the s143 entry in section 18.
    - Unproven: proven only on the 14 stored rows; a live run does not verify it. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 38). Limits: see section 17.
  - K-S3 FOLLOW-UP READING: a K-S3 follow-up answer reads CURED as COUNTED when it holds COUNTED as a whole word, in any case, anywhere in the answer; it is read as live when it opens with NOT COUNTED or holds TOUCHES NO LINK. The strict first-word reading and the whole-answer reading do not refuse: where either would, the log reason carries "(NOTED, record-only: ...)" and the ruling stays CURED. An empty answer is asked once. A close-round concession COUNTED with a CORRECTED COUNT line sets the side's conceded count; when the follow-up's corrected count is not kept, the K-S3 log row says COUNT NOT WRITTEN with the reason.
    - The reading is given the RESPONSE line plus up to three unlabeled lines written right under it; any labeled line ends the run. A RESPONSE line ending in a full stop, question mark, exclamation mark or semicolon joins the next line with a space; any other ending, a colon included, joins with a full stop. An unlabeled line that names another item ("#2", "Item 2", "point 3", "no. 2", a rule code or a side and round not in this item's label) ends the run and stays prose; a line naming the item's own number continues. COUNTED must still open the RESPONSE line for the strict reading.
    - Limits: a plain refusal that holds the word "counted" reads CURED with a note (79 of 88 refusals on the 287-case set, 68 of them noted); enforcement of those is a later package. A line naming another item or round hides every line after it from the reader.
  - FORCED NONE-LINE (pkgKNL rev 5): the none-line allow-list takes the FORCED LINES / FORCED LINKS head, a leading dash, "none of the collisions/links/lines", "there are no forced links", and a closing parenthesis only in the fixed nothing-graded forms; any other text makes it a checked FORCED line.
  - ANTI-CHEAT ANSWER LINES (pkgACM): a line may end with one period after its last quote mark and is still read. Still not read: more than one period, any other mark or text after the quote, EARLIER without quote marks, a lower-case answer word. A REVERSED answer is shown only when both quotations stand in the side's own rounds.
  - FORCED LINE INSIDE A GATE CHANGE (pkgGFC rev s):
    - Gate answers, FORCED line inside the change: when a judge answers a gate item with CHANGE TO and writes, after the collision line "D<i> vs C<j> ...", a FORCED line for the same pair inside the same field, the collision line is read alone and the FORCED line is read as if it stood in the reason field.
    - Read after any of: ";", "/", "|", "--", or a space. Not read: a FORCED line naming another pair, the pair written differently in the two places, lower case "forced:", the word FORCED in prose.
    - A FORCED line that fails its own checks (a quotation not in its round) still leaves the item UNCURED.
    - Send-back replies are not touched. Stored cases: 4 lines in 2 of 45 stored gate replies (all luna). Unproven until two clean runs.
  - A GATE CHANGE AND THE SEND-BACK COUNT (pkgGCCN rev 3):
    - A GATE CHANGE TAKES OFF THE SEND-BACK COUNT OF THE SAME ITEM (section 9, GATE, after "the gate's uncured LIVE items are counted in drv_uncured"): when the gate answer is CHANGE, the gate change was applied, the item was raised from a send-back change ("E-APPLY Sn"), and Send Back Record counted Sn in drv_uncured for that seat (a counted audit line of the send-back rules, tags v41 to v46, v48 and v51), drv_uncured drops by one for Sn, never below 0. One counted Sn comes off once, however many gate items name it. A line that reads "not counted in drv_uncured" is not a count. The E-GATE audit line of the first such item adds "; its send-back count comes off".
    - Not affected: a gate KEEP; a cure because another change already wrote the line; a silence count; record-only and TRIAL lines; uncured gate items; other seats.
    - Form (b) below is closed by pkgGUB rev 2 for pkgUND counts (see the pkgGUB section). Two forms are still forgiven (the count comes off): (a) a judge who refused the change at the send-back and yields at the gate with a change the engine applies; (b) a gate change that is applied although its new line would fail the send-back rule of the item it cures (the gate does not run that rule; pkgGUB is a separate closure for the pkgUND rule only).
    - Also taken off: a gate item cured because a later gate change of the same judge rewrote the same line and was applied. Stored: 10 such items in 2 judgments, none with a counted send-back.
    - Stored: 1 judgment changes (run 33490, gemini, 12 to 11, removal unchanged). Not covered: a count set at the cure step. Unproven until two clean runs.
  - A pkgUND SEND-BACK COUNT AT THE GATE (pkgGUB rev 2):
    - Section 9, GATE (replaces, in the pkgGCC narrow paragraph, the sentence on form (b), a gate change applied although its new line would fail the send-back rule):
    - (b) A SEND-BACK COUNT FROM pkgUND COMES OFF ONLY WHEN THE LINE IN EFFECT PASSES pkgUND (pkgGUB): when the counting line for Sn is a pkgUND line ("[AUDIT v42: COLLISION GRADE UNCURED (pkgUND) ... ITEMS: ... Sn (...)"), the gate re-runs the pkgUND cure rule (the same code Send Back Record uses) before taking the count off. It runs on the line in effect: when a later gate change of the same judge rewrote the same line ("CURED: the later gate change Gn ... rewrote the same line"), the rule reads Gn's line, following such links to the last change. The count comes off only if that rule reads CURED: a BROKEN grade must pass the layer check; an UNDERDETERMINED grade must carry a counted [L1] and state what the record leaves open; an E-UNG item must carry a graded Dn vs Cj line. Otherwise the count stays. A reading error keeps the count.
    - THE GATE-CHANGE CHECK READS THE REASON TOO (pkgGUR; Shared Library): the check of (b) hands the gate row's reason to the same pkgUND rule together with the line. An UNDERDETERMINED gate change that states its open point (and its [L1]) only in its reason, after "||", is read as a send-back answer of the same wording is read.
    - Counts from the other send-back rules (v40, v41 pkgECF, v43 pkgABS, v44 pkgPAB, v45 pkgMNP, v46 pkgIMP, v48 pkgKSC, v51 pkgEB7) keep the pkgGCC narrow behaviour: the gate does not re-run those rules. Form (a) of that paragraph (refused at the send-back, yielded at the gate) is still forgiven.
    - Record: when the [L1] verse was not read in the run, or the verse rows are not available and the line is otherwise good, the count comes off as on s111.
  - WHAT THE RECORD LEAVES OPEN: THE OPEN TEST READS A LONGER PHRASE (pkgUOW revision 2; Shared Library, UND.OPEN, used by the pkgUND send-back cure, the E-HOLD send-back cure and pkgUNA):
    - Text:
      - Where: Shared Library, UND.OPEN (the pattern undOk uses). undOk is called by the pkgUND send-back cure (MISSING COLLISION GRADE), by the E-HOLD send-back cure on an UNDERDETERMINED line, and by pkgUNA (UNDERDETERMINED WITHOUT ITS GROUNDS) on every UNDERDETERMINED collision line of an alternate's verdict.
      - What changed: an UNDERDETERMINED line states what the record leaves open when it says "leave" or "leaves", then up to 8 words, then open, disputed, undecided, unsettled, unresolved, contested or in dispute. Before this package the limit was 3 words, each of letters, digits or underscore only, so a possessive with a curly apostrophe ("Joseph’s") or a longer phrase failed. A word here is any run of characters without a space, period, semicolon, colon, comma, exclamation or question mark, parenthesis, em or en dash, and not starting with the engine's "--" separator, so the phrase cannot run across a clause, a sentence or a field (rev 2, on the first grader's finding). Every other form the pattern accepted is unchanged.
      - Why: run 35173, luna's send-back item S11. The line "D4 vs C6 (Luke 3:23): UNDERDETERMINED -- ... leave the biological status of Joseph’s fatherhood open -- GROUND: [L1] Luke 3:23 ..." was counted uncured with only the fault "the line does not state what the record leaves open".
      - Size: +19 characters of code (measured), prompt 0.
      - Limits: the pattern stays a word list. It still misses "disputing", "differ on" and similar forms. A negated phrase such as "leaves nothing open" was already accepted before this package and still is.
    - A word excludes "!", "?", parentheses, the em dash and the en dash, and may not begin with "--", so the window does not cross the engine's own "--" separator. Size, measured: code +19 over s140; prompt 0.
    - Rule for Shared Library edits: any Shared Library edit must go through the pkgLIB form-3 map (add the length change to the map n of each node that uses the edited line, refresh the library sha header and rebuild all 42 stubs, each checked against its map length and compiled); a grade of one must rebuild the stubs and compare each length with its map n. The line edited by pkgUOW is used by 9 nodes (Send Back Build, Send Back Record, Gate Record, Alt Send Back Build, Alt Send Back Record, Alt Gate Record and the three R2 Alt copies).
  - UNDERDETERMINED GROUNDS MAY STAND IN THE REASON AFTER "||" (pkgUOR; Shared Library, the UND block of pkgUND):
    - For an E-UNG item graded UNDERDETERMINED, and for an E-HOLD item kept UNDERDETERMINED, the cure reads the open point from the CHANGE TO line or, if the line has none, from the reason after "||".
    - If the line has no [L1], the [L1] is read from the line and the reason together.
    - Everything else is unchanged: the graded "Dn vs Cm" line must stand in the CHANGE TO, KEEP does not cure, RETRACTED does not grade a pressed link, and BROKEN, STANDS and MEETS regrades are read as before.
    - The cure reason says when the reason supplied the open point or the [L1] ("in the reason after "||", pkgUOR").
    - Size: Shared Library code +495; prompt 0.
    - See the s141 entry in section 18. The stub "Early Alternate Build" has no map entry, in live s141 as in its parent, which predates pkgUOW and is not a fault of it.
    - The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 36). Limits: see section 17.
  - THE OPEN TEST ALSO READS "DISPUTING" AND "DIFFER ON, OVER, ABOUT OR AS TO" (pkgDFR; Shared Library line 1898, the OPEN pattern read by undOk, UND.OPEN; it continues the pkgUOW revision 2 entry above, whose limit it lifts):
    - Change: library line 1898, the OPEN pattern behind the fault message "the line does not state what the record leaves open" (undOk). After "disputes" it adds "disputing" and "differ(s|ed|ing)" followed by "on", "over", "about" or "as to". Nothing else changes: no prompt text, the fault message and its examples are as before.
    - Why: UNDERDETERMINED collision lines that do state what is open, in the words "differ on ..." or "disputing ...", were refused by the pkgUOW revision 2 pattern, so a send-back item on them stayed uncured for want of the word list. WALL-5036
    - Size (measured): code +60 (line 1898 +58); prompt 0. Nine stubs that use the line rebuild +58 each: Send Back Build, Send Back Record, Gate Record and their Alt and R2 Alt copies. In the combined file with pkgPCQ: code +614, prompt 0 for pkgDFR.
    - See the s145 entry in section 18.
    - Limit and unproven: "The sides do not differ on the timing." passes (a negation reads as open), the same kind as the pkgUOW "leaves nothing open"; no stored line has that form. The pkgUOW revision 2 limit above that "differ on" and "disputing" are missed is lifted by this package. Stored text is all the replays prove; a live send-back is the first live evidence. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 39). Limits: see section 17.
  - A LATER CORRECTION AND A CURE-STEP COUNT (pkgGCP rev 1b):
    - Section 9, GATE (after the pkgGCC narrow paragraph):
    - A LATER CORRECTION TAKES OFF A CURE-STEP COUNT (pkgGCP): in Gate Record (and its alternate and round 2 copies), also when the gate is skipped, a cure item that counted at the cure step (a "[AUDIT v21: DEBATE RULE VIOLATION REPORT" line, RESULT not CURED, LIVE rule) comes off drv_uncured once, never below 0, when (a) an applied send-back or gate change replaced the line it names, (b) the gate's rule re-read raises that rule on that item in the text before the changes, and (c) the same re-read does not raise it in the corrected text. Mark: "[AUDIT v52: CURE COUNT OFF (pkgGCP) ...]". A seat whose count falls to 0 this way is not replaced by an alternate.
    - Not affected: silence at the cure ("WHY: no written response"); K-C "NOT COUNTED"; TRIAL rules; rules the re-read does not produce (K-W, K-RUL, K-CMT, K-CMTL, K-AG, K-KJV and the like); a line not replaced; an item still raised after the change; other seats.
    - Limits: (1) in practice only K-B, K-G1, K-G4, and K-F when its FORCED line is itself replaced, can come off; (2) K-G3 stays counted though the re-read no longer raises it (it is cleared by a field, and its report has no LINE); (3) K-S3 stays counted though the judge's line is later ended with COUNTED: the report LINE is written "DEFENDER CR4: ..." while the judge's line is "DEFENDER | CR4 | ...", and K-S3 items are numbered by position, so K-S3 must not be opened by loosening the line match alone; (4) a FORCED line left behind after the collision line is changed keeps K-F raised and counted.
    - Limit: the rule assumes every counted v21 UNCURED line is still inside drv_uncured; a counted line planted without raising the count still takes one off. Stored check: of 41 stored judgments with a counted v21 UNCURED line, none has more such lines than its drv_uncured.
    - Limit: K-C counts are not taken off (the report reads NOT COUNTED while drv counts it). Stored check: 10 stored judgments carry a K-C count, 1 had a send-back applied (run 33497, seat gemini), and a variant without the K-C skip changes nothing in 28 replayable node runs. The gap can keep a count and cannot drop one wrongly.
  - K-G7 AND K-G4 ON A LINK BROKEN BY THE CONFIRMED CLOSING CONTRADICTION (pkgK7C rev 2):
    - K-G7 OR K-G4 ON A LINK BROKEN BY THE CONFIRMED CLOSING CONTRADICTION (pkgK7C rev 2): when the link of a K-G7 or K-G4 item (DEFENDER: Dn; CHALLENGER: Cm) is in the engine's confirmed closing-contradiction list (Close Enforcer Record and Judge Contradiction Record, the list pkgCEC uses), the cure check reads the item CURED: "the link is broken by the engine's confirmed closing contradiction, which no count carries (pkgK7C)". A K-G7 premise there is not added to the K-G8 count expectation. Reason: the engine's own recompute breaks that link by the contradiction rule, so a judge that keeps its own break line on it has nothing to cure; PRESSED, FORCED and RETRACTED do not fit.
    - The test runs after the existing checks for a failed cure call, no written response and a malformed RETRACTED line, and before the K-G4 and K-G7 rules. Those checks are unchanged.
    - Not covered: K-F. A K-F item carries side BOTH, so its link alone does not say whose link the FORCED line breaks; excusing it could excuse a malformed FORCED line that breaks the other side's link. Real case: 1 (Haiku, 34703, cured by its retraction).
    - Unchanged: the items are still listed in the cure request, so a judge may still answer them (for example by RETRACTED). Every other K-G4 and K-G7 item follows the rules as before.
    - Limits: (1) K-F on the confirmed link is not covered: a judge that keeps its line there can still be counted once by K-F ("no FORCED line"); (2) the cure goes by link, not by the ground the judge wrote; (3) the judge is still asked, and no written response to a K-G4 item stays UNCURED; (4) NOT SHOWN by any run: a judge that actually keeps its line (all stored judges retracted).
  - A DEFERRAL READ AS THE WHOLE ANSWER (pkgEAD rev 3b):
    - Where: Send Back Build, Alt Send Back Build, R2 Alt Send Back Build (Shared Library). +1,875 code measured on s113; prompt 0. Revert copy wall2024/prewrite_s114_EAD3b.json.
    - What it does: a DeepSeek E-ANS or E-HOLD item is not sent to the judge when both hold:
      1. its reason has deferral words ("under/at/in/see LINK n" or "premise n", "answered at/under/in", "answer it fully", "pointer", "defers");
      2. the pkgVP3 code check on the same rounds does not agree with the enforcer, and its finding, read from its start, is positive: the deferral to LINK n names an answer; the answer carries its own layer, runs n words, or a sentence is neither; a one-hop deferral whose target answers; or the answer stands without its exact reference.
    - The withheld item is listed in sb_not_sent and written as a send_back row "SEND-BACK NOT SENT: ... (pkgEAD) (ruling 428)". An item that also carries an engine check (pkgUND, pkgUNA, pkgABS, pkgECL) is still sent. A seat whose items are all withheld gets no send-back call.
    - It is a TRIAL-class withholding: no count is changed by it directly; the item is simply not put to the judge.
    - Limits:
      1. The code checks bareness, not fit: an answer of filler words behind the deferral is also withheld.
      2. E-HOLD items are in practice still sent (they carry engine checks).
      3. A misreading that rides on an enforced engine item is not withheld (34108 sol, E-HOLD with pkgUND).
      4. NOT SHOWN: that terra keeps its seat in 34703 with the item withheld.
  - OUT-OF-ROUND STRIKE WORDS (pkgOON rev 2; two of its limits are replaced by pkgOON3, next):
    - What it does:
      1. Library line 1633 (the send-back applier, 10 gate and merge nodes): NONE is added to the strike words for rule E-OOR. Nothing else in the rule changes; a row that ends COUNTED is still refused with the pkgOOS reason.
      2. Library line 3610 (Judge Enforcer Build, Alt Judge Enforcer Build, R2 Alt Judge Enforcer Build): the reason "the sentence is not found verbatim in [CRn] ("the verbatim sentence in which the ground was granted")" becomes "the sentence is not found verbatim in [CRn]; quote it verbatim, or CHANGE TO: STRUCK".
    - Limits:
      - Any answer on an out-of-round item that begins with the word NONE strikes the row, whatever follows ("NONE; the row stands as written" strikes).
      - "OUT-OF-ROUND CONCESSIONS: NONE" given as the answer to one row is still not read and still counts.
      - NOT SHOWN: that a judge told the form writes it. Only a live reply shows it.
      - The record-only out-of-round cure reading (pkgVP1) still reads NONE as UNCURED; it counts nothing.
  - SEND-BACK AND GATE CHANGE TO IN ROW FORM (pkgRPS; Shared Library, the send-back applier):
    - A CHANGE TO for an out-of-round row is placed when it names the side, the round and the quoted sentence of a row in the judge's text.
    - These forms are read as the row form "SIDE | [Rn] | “sentence” ...":
      - "SIDE | [Rn] “sentence” ..." (second separator left out);
      - "SIDE | Rn “sentence” ..." (brackets left out);
      - "SIDE Rn: “sentence” ..." (the item label form);
      - side written in lower case.
    - The quote must open right after the round. Side, round and sentence must still match the judge's row.
    - When the row is found, the line is applied in full row form.
    - Size: Shared Library code +407; prompt 0.
  - OUT-OF-ROUND STRIKE AFTER ITS LABEL, AND THE FORMS TOLD (pkgOON3):
    - An out-of-round row answered "OUT-OF-ROUND CONCESSIONS: NONE" is struck. The send-back applier reads the strike words (STRUCK, STRIKE, REMOVE, DELETE, NONE) also after that label.
    - The out-of-round reason "the line names X but the round is the other side's" tells the judge: write the corrected row, or CHANGE TO: STRUCK.
    - The out-of-round reason "COUNTED ends a line from [round]" tells the judge: CHANGE TO: the row without COUNTED. The quoted rule words are removed.
    - Where: Shared Library lines 1635 (applier; Gate Build, Gate Record, the alternate gate and merge nodes: 10 nodes), 3340 and 3342 (Judge Enforcer Build and its two alternate copies), counted from 1 on s117.
    - Limits:
      - A row ending COUNTED is still not struck by a strike word; its reason tells the form that is read.
      - The record-only re-reading of a replaced line still reads only STRUCK as a strike; it counts nothing.
      - Not reached by any run: that a judge told the form writes it. The dry regrade of 34726 (execution 34780, on s115) showed a judge writing STRUCK when told, under pkgOON rev 2; the NONE branch was not reached.
  - A K-G1 RAISED BY THE GATE'S OWN CONTRADICTION ITEM (pkgGK1 rev 1):
    - What it does: library line 1903 (the filter of new K items charged to a gate change; the same 10 gate and merge nodes). A new K-G1 raised by an applied change on an E-GATE M CONTRADICTION item is no longer charged to that change. The gate item is CURED; the K-G1 is still written as an "E-GATE NEW ITEM" row marked RECORD-ONLY.
    - The earlier rule stands otherwise: a gate change that raises a new LIVE K item does not cure and is counted once. K-G4, K-F, K-G7, K-B and every other K item raised by a gate change stay counted. A change that does not cure its item stays counted.
    - Limits:
      - The verdict keeps a link BROKEN on an unanswered witness with a conceded count of 0. The inconsistency is recorded, not repaired.
      - The exemption is not tied to the link the gate item names.
      - The K-G1 row keeps cure_status UNCURED with "(RECORD-ONLY)" in its reason; a count of UNCURED rows in the log over-counts by one.
      - NOT SHOWN: that the seat is kept through to the outcome row.
  - K-C AND A LINE THAT COMPARES A CHARGE WITH A QUOTATION (pkgKCQ rev 2):
    - What it does: library line 563, the first NET_STATE form. "does not ... assert" is not matched when its subject is "(that|this|its|the) [round] quotation" and its object is "the added ...". Nothing else in K-C changes.
    - Limits:
      - A true K-C ground worded with all three parts (a quotation as subject, "does not assert", "the added ..." as object) is not raised, and nothing reports it. Found only by plant; no stored case. Graders read for it by hand at each run.
      - The NOT HELD answer form is still not read for K-C; only a RESTATED line is.
  - K-W ANSWER WITH SINGLE QUOTATION MARKS (pkgKWQ rev 2):
    - What it does: library line 1403, the K-W reader accepts single quotation marks, straight or curly. The closing mark must not be followed by a letter, so an apostrophe inside a word does not close the quotation.
    - Limits:
      - An unclosed opening single mark is accepted when a later apostrophe stands before a non-letter ("'the readings' meaning is kept").
      - The reader checks form only. Whether the quotation stands in the round named is read by hand at each run grade.
  - A CHANGE TO THAT OPENS WITH THE OUT-OF-ROUND HEADING (pkgOAD, pkgOSP; library line 1413, counted from 1; the send-back applier in the 10 gate and merge nodes): when a CHANGE TO opens with the out-of-round concessions heading and DEFENDER or CHALLENGER follows, the heading is dropped before the line is read. The row is then matched to its row in the judge text and replaced. Forms of the heading that are read: upper or lower case; with or without the hyphens; CONCESSION or CONCESSIONS; bold or underscore marks around it; a bullet before it; a colon, "--", a hyphen, an en dash, an em dash or nothing after it; the heading repeated. A quotation mark around the whole line is dropped first.
  - A CHANGE TO THAT OPENS WITH A CONCEDED LABEL (pkgCLP; library line 1413, counted from 1; the same applier): when a CHANGE TO opens with the word CONCEDED, then a colon, "--", a hyphen, an en dash or an em dash, and a row with bars that begins with DEFENDER or CHALLENGER follows directly, the label is dropped before the line is read. The row is then read as any other row of a CHANGE TO. Forms of the label that are read: upper or lower case; bold or underscore marks around it; a bullet before it. The label is looked for before the out-of-round heading is.
  - A FLAGGED OUT-OF-ROUND HEADING (pkgOHK rev 2; library lines 1414 and 1427, counted from 1; the same applier): when the flagged line is the out-of-round concessions heading itself (with or without "#" marks before it, bold or underscore marks, a colon after it, the hyphens, or the final S) and the CHANGE TO begins with DEFENDER or CHALLENGER, the heading is kept. A change that matches an existing row replaces that row. A change that the row reader reads as an out-of-round row and that matches no row is added on the line under the heading. Any other change is NOT APPLIED with the reason "the flagged line is the OUT-OF-ROUND CONCESSIONS heading and the change is not an out-of-round row (pkgOHK)".
    - A COUNTED row asked for by the close-round concession check (TRIAL) may stand in the judge text once the judge writes it.
    - Limits:
      - Not read, and the change stays NOT APPLIED with its reason: the heading with no colon, with a dash, in bold, after a bullet, in the singular, written twice, or before a row written without the bars.
      - When the flagged line is the heading line itself and the change is a row, the heading line is replaced by the row. The same holds for a change written without the heading.
  - A REASONED RETRACTION, AND THE JUDGE'S OWN BREAK LINE ON A CONFIRMED LINK (pkgRTE rev 3):
    - Part RT (library lines 1628, 1630 and 1638, counted from 1; the send-back applier in the 10 gate and merge nodes): nothing is applied that was not applied before. When a CHANGE TO is "RETRACTED: Dn vs Cm" followed by more text and the change is NOT APPLIED for "the CHANGE TO line has no line form the engine reads" or "the flagged line is not in the judge text", the reason gains: "; a retraction is written "RETRACTED: <pair>" with nothing after the pair". The judge reads it on the gate item and keeps the decision: the bare form, or KEEP.
    - Part EJ (library line 3091; Send Back Build, Alt Send Back Build, R2 Alt Send Back Build; extends pkgECB): an item is also withheld when its line is the judge's own collision line "Dn vs Cm (...): DEFENDER LINK BROKEN" (or CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN) for a side and link in the confirmed closing-contradiction list, with no engine mark needed, and so are the items on the FORCED lines of that pair. Each is listed in sb_not_sent with the pkgECB reason.
    - Reason for EJ: the confirmed contradiction decides the link. Sending the judge's own break back under an enforced rule pressed the judge to unbreak a link the engine breaks again, and then raised a gate item between its RULING card and its line.
    - Limits:
      - Part RT saves no item: the reasoned retraction still costs a gate item, and a judge that repeats the form at the gate is still counted.
      - The clause also appears on a reasoned retraction of another pair, where the bare form would not apply either.
      - Part EJ does not match a lowercase grade, nested brackets after the pair, or a leading "-": those lines are still sent back.
      - The judge's own ground on a withheld line is no longer checked by the enforcer or the layer check (the link is broken either way).
      - Not shown: any live reply, and that a judge told the form writes it; the alternate and round 2 copies ran only as the same library lines.
  - THE DEEPSEEK PREMISE ITEM AND THE LINK TAG (pkgPLT):
    - Change (library lines 3275 and 3286, counted from 1; answerFor in the E-PREM item builder; Judge Enforcer Build and its two alternate copies): when the list entry begins "n. LINK k", the fallback looks for LINK k. Entries with no LINK tag keep the number fallback. A "[premise n]" tag still wins first.
    - DeepSeek-read text: only the E-PREM item's answering-block text, and only in these cases. Judge prompts unchanged.
    - Limits:
      - Needs the "LINK k" tag at the start of the list entry.
      - A block tagged "[premise m]" for another premise is not taken by the LINK fallback.
      - Not reached on a live run; a run shows only whether DeepSeek reads the new block.
      - Forms: "4. LINK1:" (no space) and a LINK tag not at the start of the entry keep the lookup by number (0 of 720 stored entries); "4. LINKS 1 and 2" keeps the lookup by number; where two blocks carry the same LINK tag the first is shown.
  - RULING 372 IN DEEPSEEK'S RULE E-PREM, THE COUNTED KIND (pkgP72 revision 6; Shared Library, library lines 3122, 3125, 3126, 3129, 3212, 3393 and 3411; read by Judge Enforcer Build and its alternate copies):
    - pkgP72 revision 6: code +3,334, prompt +42,784 over 12 stored runs, run 35173 +4,311. Library lines 3122, 3125, 3126, 3129, 3212, 3393, 3411. A ruling-372 block (that judge's own ANSWER CHECK lines graded MISSES or FAILS) is shown under a premise only when: the verse is among the premise's PRESSED WITH verses (first 8; ledger-proven when the ledger lists it); the round is the answering side's; the line's MEANS equals that press's MEANS after lower case and punctuation are removed; neither the judge's CONCEDED LINKS line nor the ANSWER CHECK line rules that premise VOID (wide reader: ranges, lists, "VOID: Premise N", "void ruling(s) on premise(s) N", negation only directly before VOID); at most 3 per judge. Rule E-PREM adds the ruling-372 exception, written (UNEXHIBITED: <verse> unanswered, ruling 372); the pkgEPS hold, TRIAL since s191 (pkgEPT), no longer stops any push; it records "(TRIAL pkgEPS: would not be sent: ...)" and the push is sent. Limits: a paraphrased MEANS gives no block; VOID in another sentence from its number, or with no number, is not read (unsafe side, 0 stored); safe-side false reads withhold a block; the 8-verse cap applies before the ledger filter; a press MEANS past the 1,200-character cut is shown in part; the Record's verse test reads the whole reason ("Matthew 1:190" passes); what DeepSeek writes needs a paid replay.
    - The VOID reader is wide: "Premise(s) N ... VOID" or "VOIDED" within one clause of up to 60 characters, with no full stop, semicolon or other "premise" between them and no "not", "never" or "no longer" in that span; also "VOID: Premise(s) N". A false read only withholds a block, which leaves the behavior before pkgP72. Limit: a range ("Premises 1-5 VOID") reads only its end numbers.
    - Number ranges in a VOID ruling are expanded: "Premises 1-5", "1 to 5", "1 through 5", and the same with an en dash between the numbers, read every premise in the range (a range wider than 20, or reversed, reads only its ends).
    - The block in the DeepSeek prompt (line 3129) tests the judge's CONCEDED LINKS line, as the ids at line 3126 do. The negation guard applies only when "not", "never" or "no longer" stands directly before VOID (allowing "be", "been", "ruled", "held" or "found" between), so a "not" in a relative clause does not release the block. "void ruling(s) on premise(s) N" is read, and bold markers are removed before reading. Limits: VOID in another sentence from its premise number is not read; an unnumbered "the charge is VOID" is not read; a false read withholds a block.
    - Size (measured): code +3,334 over s145. Build stubs +2,904 each, Records +320 each. Prompt +42,784 over 121 requests, run 35173 +4,311. Revision 6 reads 10 new (line, premise) pairs over the 12 exports, 3 in the 33490 grok CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS line, whose block stands under premise 3 and is unaffected, and 7 in DeepSeek text that p372 never reads.
    - THE HOLD WHEN THE SAME PASS RULES THE ANSWER CHECK MEETS (pkgP72 hold, rev 3; this revision number belongs to the hold and is apart from revision 6 above; Shared Library, the two library lines that carry the pkgP72 marker in the Records, lines 3394 and 3412 counted from 1; read by Judge Enforcer Record, Alt Judge Enforcer Record and R2 Alt Judge Enforcer Record): an E-PREM push (DeepSeek DISAGREE, right COUNTED) written in the ruling-372 form, (UNEXHIBITED: <verse> unanswered, ruling 372), is recorded by the pkgEPS hold as "(TRIAL pkgEPS: would not be sent: <reason>)", and is sent to the judge all the same (EPS_STATUS is 'TRIAL' since s191, pkgEPT), when both are true: (1) every block verse that its reason names has an E-ANS item of this same pass, for the same judge seat and the same answering side, that DeepSeek rules DISAGREE with right MEETS; (2) none of those verses has an E-ANS item of that seat and side that the judge did not grade MEETS and that this pass does not rule MEETS. An E-ANS item with no DeepSeek ruling counts under (2) and stops the hold. A push whose reason carries "footing borrowed from contested Links <n>" is not recorded as held (pkgBFX rev 2). The hold reason reads "the push counts the premise on this judge's ANSWER CHECK on <verses>, which the E-ANS item of this same pass rules MEETS; the count follows that corrected grade". The test runs before the release of the ruling-372 form; when it does not hold, that release stands as before. Side: a premise listed "against the defender" reads the CR answer checks, one "against the challenger" the DR answer checks. A verse is found in the reason only when no letter or digit, and no digit followed by a space, stands before it and no digit stands after it ("John 5:1" is not found in "1 John 5:1"; "Matthew 1:1" is not found in "Matthew 1:18").
      - Size (measured): Shared Library +1,609; each of the three Records +1,576 when rebuilt; prompt 0.
      - Limits: the test is by verse, not by MEANS, so a second answer check on the same verse from another press that stays MISSES stops a hold that would be right, and the push goes out as before (1 of 13 stored ruling-372 pushes; 8 held, 5 kept); two references with no comma between them ("Romans 5:1 John 3:16") are not found; the release test that follows still reads the whole reason. Unproven until a run carries a ruling-372 push.
    - See the s146 entry in section 18. Limits and unproven: see section 17. A paid call is needed for what DeepSeek answers to a ruling-372 block; a live run verifies no enforcer change by itself. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 41).
  - CURE READER, NEGATED GRADES AND RANGES IN WORDS (pkgNVR; Shared Library):
    - Text:
      - Node: Shared Library only (no other node changed). Built on s130. Code +163 characters (measured), prompt 0.
      - What it does:
        - 1. psGrades (the reader of a CONCEDED LINKS line, used by 15 nodes) reads "not void", "never void", "not unexhibited" and "not conceded" as SPARED, as it already did for "not counted".
        - 2. psGrades expands a range in words after "premises": "Premises 1 through 6" and "premises 2 to 4" read as every premise in the range, as "1-6" already did.
        - 3. The E-PREM cure (enf428Cure) has its own fallback reader for a CHANGE TO with no CONCEDED LINKS heading ("Premise 2 is NOT VOID and is spared"). It now reads "not" or "never" before the grade word as SPARED.
        - 4. The E-PREM cure reads the enforcer's own right grade "NOT VOID" as SPARED. Before, any right grade containing VOID was taken as VOID.
      - Limits:
        - "premise 1 through 6" (singular "premise") is not expanded.
        - "is not VOID but UNEXHIBITED" reads SPARED (the negation turns the whole clause to SPARED). No stored line has this form.
        - "not voided" is not a grade word before or after; such a clause gives no grade, as before.
    - Size: Shared Library only; code +163 (measured); prompt 0. The s132 write as a whole is +558 code (163 + 395).
    - Limits: the first grade wins, so "Premises 1 through 6 are spared; premise 4 is VOID" reads premise 4 SPARED (the digit form "1-6" already did this); a second range without the word "premises" gives the end numbers only; a reversed range "premises 6 to 1" gives no grade; "not void but UNEXHIBITED" and "not void; ... counted" read SPARED; "no longer void" and "cannot be void" still read VOID; "not voided" is not read.
    - Unproven until two clean runs; not yet run on the n8n runner. The first-run checks are in the grade plan (section 29).
  - THE CONCEDED LINKS READER CUTS A CLAUSE BEFORE ", AND PREMISE", AND THE CURE COPY READS "NOT COUNTED" (pkgPCS; Shared Library lines 1381 and 1643):
    - Text:
      - Node: Shared Library only (lines 1381 and 1643; MAP lengths recomputed; 42 stubs rebuild and compile). Built on s141 (ff40f39f). Prompt: none.
      - What it does:
        - 1. psGrades reads a CONCEDED LINKS line clause by clause and gives each clause's FIRST grade word to every premise number in it. A clause is cut at ";" and "." and also before ", and premise(s) <n>", ", but premise(s) <n>", ", while ..." and ", whereas ...". A comma part with no grade word of its own is joined to the next part, so "premises 1 and 6, and premises 2 to 5, are spared" reads as before. Both copies: line 1381 (Gate Build, Gate Record, Send Back Record, Alternate Merge Seat, Alternate Merge 1 and their Alt and R2 clones) and line 1643 (Cure Check, Panel Cure Check).
        - 2. Line 1643 (Cure Check, Panel Cure Check) carries the negation rule line 1381 already had: "not/never counted, void, unexhibited, conceded" and "uncounted" read as SPARED.
      - Effect (measured, 12 stored exports, 1,764 distinct CONCEDED LINKS strings):
        - Part 1: 2 lines change in each copy, both now right: 35173 sol ("premises 1 and 6 remain VOID ..., and premises 2 through 5 remain spared": 2 to 5 were VOID, now SPARED) and 33149 ("premises 3 is spared, and premises 1, 4 and 6 remain VOID": 1, 4, 6 were SPARED, now VOID).
        - Part 2: 7 more strings change in the Cure Check copy only (33490, 34626, 35173), each a "not counted" read as COUNTED, now SPARED.
        - No other string changes.
      - Limits:
        - No comma, no split: "premise 1 is VOID and premise 2 is spared" still reads both VOID.
        - Only "and", "but", "while", "whereas" before "premise(s) <n>" start a new clause.
      - Size:
      - +579 code characters (measured: part 1 +204 on each of two lines, part 2 +171); prompt 0.
    - Why: the clause split has two copies: line 1381 serves Gate Build, Gate Record, Send Back Record, Alternate Merge Seat, Alternate Merge 1 and their Alt and R2 clones (13 stubs), and line 1643 serves Cure Check and Panel Cure Check; the gate and the cure readers read the same rule. Line 1643 never got the pkgNVR negation rule (nor the older "not counted" rule), so the live cure readers read "Premises 1-6 are not counted as conceded or unexhibited" (run 35173) as all COUNTED and "premise 4 is not counted UNEXHIBITED" (run 33490) as COUNTED; the pkgNVR entry above says the reader serves 15 nodes. WALL-4977
    - See the s142 entry in section 18: mapcheck on the fetch-back 0 length or compile faults; code +3,705 for the three packages; the pkgPCS share is +579 (15 rebuilt sources change: 13 by +204 and Cure Check and Panel Cure Check by +361; measured), prompt 0.
    - The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 37). Limits: see section 17.
  - K-W NOT HELD AFTER A LEAD SENTENCE (pkgKWN; Shared Library, the cure reader for rule K-W):
    - What it does: the K-W reader accepts NOT HELD at the start of the RESPONSE or STATEMENT line, as before. It also accepts NOT HELD after a lead sentence, after a ";", or after a ":" that ends a lead clause. Example: "Fact granted: ... . NOT HELD: ...".
    - The test that the reason quotes the flagged sentence (six words in a row) reads only the text from NOT HELD on.
    - Why: the K-W rule text tells the judge to "first name the fact the withdrawn sentence carried". In run 34852, sol answered "Fact granted: ... NOT HELD: [CR2] grants "<the flagged words>"", and the reader, which looked for NOT HELD only at the start of the line, counted it uncured. The seat was not counted.
    - Size: +141 code measured (one library line, +136, plus map lengths); prompt 0.
    - Limits:
      - NOT HELD inside a sentence with no sentence break before it ("This is NOT HELD by ...") is still not read.
      - A lead joined by a colon or dash with no full stop ("Fact granted: NOT HELD: ...") stays refused.
      - NOT HELD inside a quotation, after a sentence end inside that quotation, is read as the judge's NOT HELD when six flagged words follow; a line opening "**NOT HELD:**" is CURED.
  - K-W NOT HELD QUOTE TEST WITH PARENTHETICALS REMOVED (pkgKWP; Shared Library, the cure reader for rule K-W, and the follow-up ask):
    - A NOT HELD reply to a K-W item cures when its reason quotes the flagged sentence: a run of six words in a row, or the whole quote when it is six words or fewer.
    - The runs are made from the flagged quote and from each nhqFull part: with the lead removed ("I grant", "I concede", "I withdraw", "I drop", "I do not claim", "I no longer claim"); with the verse references removed and with them kept; and with every parenthetical "( ... )" removed, but only when four or more words remain.
    - The follow-up ask (NO QUOTE) uses the same test.
    - Size: code +350 (one library line +340), prompt 0.
  - K-W REASSERTED QUOTE TEST (pkgRQI; Shared Library, the K-W cure reader):
    - A K-W reply that answers REASSERTED IN <round> passes the form test when a quote follows the round tag.
    - The quote opens with a straight or curly quote mark and closes with a straight or curly quote mark that is not followed by a letter, with at least six characters between.
    - The quote may hold quoted words of its own, provided the whole quote is on one line.
    - A quote that holds no inner quote mark may run across lines, as before.
    - Size: code +126, prompt 0.
  - THE KEY-WORD READER TAKES THE GRANT WORDS OF THE GRANTS LIST (pkgKGL, pkgKNC rev 2, pkgKAG; Shared Library, kwLines421, library lines 2219, 2220, 2222 and 2223, counted from 1; read by the three Payloads nodes and Extract Independent Verdict):
    - The verb line is the one of the grants list (section 6, THE GRANT WORDS): up to two adverbs between "I" and the verb, and the verbs admit, retract, give up and no longer rest beside the earlier ones. "set aside" is not on the verb line.
    - "I accept that" and "I agree that" are also read, with the same adverbs (pkgKAG); "accept" and "agree" are read only when "that" follows. The grants list does not read them.
      - Skipped as a pointing word before a noun: "that", then one lowercase word that is not it, he, she, they, we, you, this, these, those, there, then, so, is or was, then at most "in", "of" or "on" with "the", "a" or "an" and one or two lowercase words, then a stop mark, a dash, "and" or "against" ("I accept that standard -- and apply it.").
      - Pointing back: when "that" is followed by a stop mark, a comma, a semicolon, a dash or "as", the item takes the writer's own clause before "I", or when there is none the sentence before. With nothing before it, the match is dropped.
    - Skipped, as in the grants list: a verb list that ends in none, nothing or no; a question; a sentence opened by If, Unless, Should or Nor with no comma, semicolon or colon before the verb.
    - The heading test takes the same verbs as the grants finder's heading test. The heading rule of pkgKWH stands: a heading is skipped only when a grant verb follows under it.
    - The cap (pkgKNC rev 2): only a plain "I grant" or "I concede" (no adverb between) takes one of 10 places per side, and only when the text it lists names no link (D or C with a digit 1 to 9, "LINK" or "link" with a number). When the 10 places are used, a further such grant is passed over and the reading goes on. A skipped match takes no place. Every other form, and a plain grant whose listed text names a link, is always read.
    - Size: code +1,220 (pkgKGL), +23 (pkgKNC rev 2), +462 (pkgKAG); prompt, one K-W item line and its audit mark for each sentence newly read.
    - Limits:
      - A side can carry more than 10 items.
      - A plain grant or concession that names no link in its listed text is passed over after 10 such items of its side ("I concede the link.", or "I concede the Matthew 1:25 limb." under a link heading: the heading is not read for the test).
      - The grants finder cuts a lead longer than 160 characters before the If / Unless test; the key-word reader tests the whole lead from the last sentence end. The two can differ on a very long lead (not tested).
      - "I do not rest" and "I rest nothing" are read by neither reader. "I accept that" and "I agree that" are read by the key-word reader only.
      - "I accept that" / "I agree that": a pointing "that" with more words after the noun, or before a capitalised noun, is listed ("I accept that standard for both sides."); reported speech without quotation marks and an "only if" tail are listed; "I accept, as I must, that ..." and "I accept and agree that ..." are not read; a grant of the shape "that" + one lowercase word + a two-word phrase + a stop is skipped; pointing back takes the sentence before, which may not be the thing accepted.
  - K-W HEADING CLAUSE (pkgKWH rev 3; Shared Library, kwLines421):
    - A K-W match ("I grant", "I withdraw", "I concede", "I drop", "I do not claim", "I no longer claim") is skipped when all three hold:
      - it is a heading-shaped clause: "What" begins the sentence or line, and the clause may chain ", what I <verb>" or "and/or what I <verb>";
      - the clause ends at once with . ! or ?, or with the end of the line (with or without a colon);
      - a K-W verb follows it before the next "#" heading line, the next "What I <verb>" clause, or the end of the round.
    - Otherwise the item is raised as before. Raised as before: "What I withdraw is LINK 3 ...", "LINK 3, which I withdraw.", "That is what I grant.", "What I grant: <text on the same line>", the real "I grant that ..." after the heading, and every heading with no K-W sentence after it.
    - Size: Shared Library code +848; prompt 0.
  - K-UNX-A PATH B AND THE JUDGE'S OWN ANSWER CHECK (pkgU72 rev 2; Shared Library, unxAcFail):
    - K-UNX-A (path B, the "unanswered" count) does not fire for a press when the judge's own ANSWER CHECK on that reference in the answering round ([DR3] for a challenger premise, [CR3] for a defender premise) grades the answer MISSES or FAILS and no such line grades it MEETS. That witness is unanswered, so the UNEXHIBITED count stands.
    - The ANSWER CHECK line is read only in this form: the round tag follows the reference directly ("Matthew 1:25 -- [DR3]"), the line holds "-- ANSWER:", and the grade is the first capitalised word after dashes that follows that label.
    - Path A (ASSUMES), the layer-name path and the plain path are unchanged.
    - Size: Shared Library code +945; prompt 0.
  - A K-G4 RETRACTION AGAINST THE JUDGE'S OWN ANSWER CHECK (pkgCNV rev 3; Shared Library, library lines 677, 788, 1072, 1074 and 3323):
    - What it does: a retraction of a K-G4 meaning break is cured by a quoted reason the system verifies. One such reason is that a later round of the holding side names a verse of the line. That reason is refused when the judge's own ANSWER CHECK on that verse, for an answer of the item's side, reads MISSES or FAILS: the retraction then reads UNCURED, and an uncured K-G4 item counts against the seat.
    - At the raising check the K-G4 item records each such verse (`acm`). Only ANSWER CHECK lines whose round label belongs to the item's side are read: a C round for a DEFENDER item, a D or G round for a CHALLENGER item.
    - The record of a verse is dropped when the cure reply holds an ANSWER CHECK line on that verse, for a round of the same side, that reads MEETS; the line may follow "RESTATED:". With no recorded verse left, the named-verse reason is read as before.
    - The other cures are unchanged: a FORCED line, and a retraction that quotes 6 or more words of the holding side's answer from its own round.
    - Prompt: the MEANING BREAK note of a recorded item adds "Naming the verse does not cure it here: your own ANSWER CHECK on <verses> reads MISSES or FAILS."
    - Size: Shared Library code +1,334; prompt +89 characters plus the verse names for each recorded item, 0 otherwise.
  - A K-G4 RETRACTION THAT QUOTES AN ANSWER THE JUDGE GRADED A MISS (pkgRMQ rev 2; Shared Library, library lines 677, 788, 1071, 1074 and 3323; it stands beside the pkgCNV rule above and closes the quotation reason):
    - What it does: a retraction of a K-G4 meaning break is not cured by a quotation of 6 or more words when the quotation is an answer that the judge's own ANSWER CHECK graded MISSES or FAILS on a verse of the line. The retraction then reads UNCURED with the reason "so quoting it is no reason to retract", and an uncured K-G4 item counts against the seat.
    - At the raising check the K-G4 item records each such answer with the rounds of its ANSWER CHECK line (`acq`, entries of answer and rounds), side filtered as for `acm`.
    - A quotation is that answer only when it stands in a round recorded with the answer and one text holds the other whole: the quotation holds the whole answer, or the answer holds the whole quotation of 6 or more words. Any other quotation of the holding side's round cures as before.
    - The grade as raised governs: an ANSWER CHECK restated as MEETS in the cure reply does not lift this block. The pkgCNV rule differs here: there a restated MEETS drops the recorded verse.
    - Prompt: the MEANING BREAK note of an item that carries `acq` adds "Quoting an answer your own ANSWER CHECK grades MISSES or FAILS does not cure it either."
    - Size: Shared Library code +1,882; prompt +88 characters for each item that carries `acq`, 0 otherwise.
  - A GATE K-G4 ITEM ON A MEANING BREAK SAYS SO (pkgG4M rev 2; Shared Library, library lines 1134 and 1139):
    - What it does: when the gate re-read raises K-G4 on a line whose GROUND names no pressed witness left unanswered (a meaning break, ruling 421), the item now carries: "THE ENGINE READS THIS LINE: its GROUND names no pressed witness left unanswered, so the break rests on meaning (ruling 421): a KEEP, a PRESSED line or a CHANGE TO that still breaks the link stays uncured without a FORCED line." It is followed by the FORCED form already given by the answered-witness note.

    - Why: in run 34852, terra's two gate K-G4 items said only "counts it only with the quoted pressing sentence and its round". terra answered with that form (a PRESSED line). Gate Record then counted both as meaning breaks that only a FORCED line cures. The item never said so, and the seat was not counted.
    - Size: +295 code measured (two library lines), fixed prompt 0. The judge-visible note appears only on such an item: +499 characters per item; terra's 34852 gate message grows by 998 characters (two items).
    - Limit: the E-GATE M rule (ruling 372: an answer graded MISSES leaves the witness unanswered) and the K-G4 re-read (a witness named in a later round was answered) still disagree. pkgG4M only tells the judge which cure the engine will accept; it does not settle that disagreement.

  - ANTI-CHEAT CHECK RUNS BEFORE THE SINGLE JUDGE AND THE SAVE (pkgACO; connections only; NOT LIVE: removed at s138; the removal and its reason are in the sub-bullets below and in section 17):
    - Text:
      - Ruling 427 (md_notes_rulings) says the anti-cheat check runs "after both closes and before the judges are seated". Before this package, the Anticheat nodes ran after Save to DB. So the readers already written into Build Independent Verdict (the ANTI-CHEAT CHECK note in the single judge's transcript) and Build Save Body (the AUDIT ANTI-CHEAT (TRIAL) rows in the review notes) never fired on a live run. Only the panel read the result.
      - Change: connections only, with no code and no new node.
        - Close Enforcer Record leads to Anticheat Build.
        - Anticheat Only? (false) leads to Regrade After Quotes?.
        - Regrade After Quotes? (true, regrade) leads to Panel Build.
        - Regrade After Quotes? (false, live) leads to Build Independent Verdict, as before.
        - Check Save (ok) leads to Panel Build.
      - Effect on a live run:
        - The single judge's requests built from Build Independent Verdict (Verdict, the re-ask when it runs, and Cure through its prefix) carry one note per REVERSED close sentence whose two quotes code found in the record. The note sits at the end of that side's close round.
        - The stored review notes carry the AUDIT ANTI-CHEAT rows.
        - On a regrade, the same nodes run in the same order as before.
        - An anti-cheat-only dry regrade no longer passes through the Regrade After Quotes? If node, which has no side effect and no reader.
      - Size: code +0, nodes +0, workflow JSON -4. Fixed prompt text +0. The variable text added to the single judge's request was measured on stored live runs at 0 to 1,824 characters (mean 709 over 7 runs). The review notes grow by 196 to 1,596 characters.
      - Limits:
        - The single judge and Save to DB start later by the Anticheat time, measured at 102 to 233 seconds over 11 exports. The total run time is unchanged because the same nodes run one at a time.
        - If Anticheat Build or Anticheat Record throws, the run now stops before the single judge and before Save to DB. Before this package it stopped after the save. Anticheat Call deepseek continues on error, with 3 tries and a 900-second timeout.
        - A note is shown only when code finds both quotes. A REVERSED answer with QUOTE NOT FOUND stays record-only (2 of 5 on run 35067).
        - The note changes a judge's input, so a live run does not verify it. It needs a paid replay of the single judge's Verdict request on stored text.
    - Note: two earlier lines of this manual give the path without the Anticheat nodes: THE CLOSING ENFORCER entry (Close Enforcer Record after Quote Report and before Regrade After Quotes?) and the PATH line of section 12 (Quote Report -> Regrade After Quotes? -> Build Independent Verdict). From s136 to s137 (not from s138, see the removal entry below) the order on a live run was Close Enforcer Record, Anticheat Build, Anticheat Record, Anticheat Only?     - Shown offline: of 303 stored E-SYN rows, 8 change side, all now right; of 56 synthesis sentences with two side words before a give-ground word (30 days), none goes from right to wrong.
    - Limits: "The challenger answered the defender and conceded C3" now reads as the defender (wrong); a passive "conceded by the challenger", and a sentence whose only side word is possessive, stay as before. Not shown by a run.
  - A CITATION OF ISAIAH 28:10 OR 28:13 AS METHOD IS NOT A NEW PRESS IN THE CLOSE (pkgACT part 1, ASK 276; fresh() in Build Synthesis, Build Independent Verdict, Build Save Body, Panel Build, Format Markdown, D4 Extract, C4 Extract, C4G Extract, D4B Extract and C4B Extract):
    - Why: DG-61 holds Isaiah 28:10 as the method. A close line such as "Precept upon precept (Isaiah 28:10), the rows stand together" was listed as a new press.
    - What it does: in a sentence with no MEANS and no [L1] to [L4] tag, an Isaiah 28:10 or 28:13 reference is replaced by "(method verse)" before the new-press test. A row with "-- MEANS" or a layer tag still counts, as do other verses on the same line. A bare "I press Isaiah 28:10 against the reading" also leaves the list: it lays out no reading of the verse, and it weighs nothing as a chain.
    - Size: +294 in each of the 10 nodes (one fresh() body, identical in all).
    - Shown offline: of 22 close-round lines citing Isaiah 28:10 or 28:13 in 60 days, the 21 with no MEANS or tag leave the list; the tagged one stays. On run 37032 the new-press list goes from ["Isaiah 28:10"] to empty.
    - Limits: a row whose "-- MEANS" sits on the next line also leaves the list (0 stored); "(Isa. 28:13)" and "Isaiah 28:9-10" still count, as before; a sentence that applies the verse's words to the topic with no MEANS is not seen. No footing check reads the new-press list.
  - A COUNT THAT STANDS AFTER ITS ONLY NAMED VERSE STOPPED FAILING IS RECORDED (K-CNT; pkgACT part 2, ASK 274; Shared Library line 1562, cureRewrite, after the K-RECHK lines; 6 users; TRIAL, record-only):
    - Why: in run 37032 luna kept premise 2 counted UNEXHIBITED although the change moved its only named verse, Luke 1:13, to MEETS.
    - What it does: when a RULING card "Premise N -- UNEXHIBITED" names verses in its NAMED BY line, every one of them had a BROKEN collision line or a MISSES/FAILS answer check moved to a non-failing grade in this change, and no failing line on that verse remains, a K-RECHK row is written: "Premise N is still counted UNEXHIBITED, but its NAMED BY line names only <verse>, and this change moved that grade (...); the count is not re-derived". counted_premises, conceded_links, the RESULT line and the ruling cards do not change.
    - Size: LIB 1562 536 to 3,513; Shared Library 928,397 to 931,428, sha a894ff926b24be72.
    - Shown offline: of 71 change pairs in the 7 matching judgments of the newest 40, 1 flag (luna, run 37032, premise 2).
    - Limits: RESULT and ruling-card prose are not re-checked; the count is not re-derived. Not shown by a run.

## 10. The panel
- JUDGE ORDER: `ORDER0` in Init Seats, filtered to the seats that accepted the agreement (§8). The PANEL ORDER is that order without the seated engines, without `NOT_PANEL` (qwen, md_notes_rulings 362, time rule; deepseek, which judges nowhere, md_notes_rulings 421) and without a seat of the same `FAM_PANEL` family as a debater (sol and chatgpt are both openai). The panel is the first three of the panel order; the RESERVE is the fourth, unless it is time-removed (no reserve then). `ORDER0` is `['chatgpt','sol','luna','haiku','qwen']`. The PANEL is terra (seat key `chatgpt`, openai/gpt-6.1-terra), sol (openai/gpt-6.1-sol) and luna (openai/gpt-6-luna); `FAM_PANEL` maps all three to openai. The ALTERNATE is Haiku (anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5) through the OpenRouter Alternate Call, never on the panel; qwen stays in ORDER0 but NOT_PANEL keeps it off every seat. Gemini, grok, mimo and glm are in no order and judge nowhere. The model_config rows `glm_flash` and `glm` are inactive and `glm` is out of `odv1_record_panel_substitution`'s seat list. Record Alternates (`odv1_record_panel_alternates`) records a seated alternate whose `model_config` row is active, role engine, and provider openrouter, or provider xai with an `x-ai/` model (grok through the openrouter Alternate Call); G1, G2, G3, MiniMax and the model match still apply. A pool model already offered to an earlier removed seat is not offered again; Build Save Body writes one review line per removed seat that ends with no alternate. The single judge (and so the synthesis and the diagnostic) is the first free seat that is not time-removed and not of a debater's family. Claude judges only as the Haiku alternate; DeepSeek is the enforcer (§9). Sol (md_notes_rulings 378) takes the place its position gives it. `panel_expected` is the three seats, sorted. The live order is the array in Init Seats; read it there.
- FAMILY RULE: no judge, reserve or alternate sits on a debate in which a model of its own `model_config.family` debates: a panel or single judge fails the run closed; a reserve is dropped; an alternate is skipped. Two seats of one family may judge together.
- FLOW: Check Save -> Panel Build -> Panel Judges Prep -> Panel Judges Parallel -> (Panel Judges Wait -> Poll -> Done?) -> Panel Judges Fetch -> Panel Judges Collect -> Panel Judge 1-3 -> Panel Check -> Reserve Needed? -> Panel Payloads -> Panel Cure -> Alternate Needed? -> Alternate Build/Call/Payloads/Cure -> Alternate Merge 1 -> Second Alternate? -> Second Alternate Needed? -> (R2 Alternate Build ... R2 Alternate Merge) -> Alternate Merge -> Dry? -> Record Substitution -> Record Alternates -> Write Judgment 1-3 -> Write Enforcer Log -> Derive Outcome -> Panel Done.
- PARALLEL JUDGES: Panel Judges Prep builds the three judges' request bodies (one per slot) with a one-use nonce. Panel Judges Parallel posts all three at once to the Panel Judge Call webhook workflow, which answers at once, calls the judge and stores the reply (`ppj_put`). The main flow waits 15 s at a time and polls `ppj_status` until every slot has a reply or an error, then reads them (`ppj_get`); Collect hands each slot's reply on in the form the Panel Judge nodes gave it. No HTTP call is held open while a judge answers.
- EARLY ALTERNATE: when a panel seat's first cure reply already leaves it removable, Early Alternate Build starts the alternate's call at once (Early Alternate Reply) instead of waiting for the whole cure stage; the alternate's reply is used only if the seat is in fact removed.
- RESERVE: Panel Check classifies each slot (error, timeout, refused, no usable judgment). One failure with a reserve free: the reserve judges the failed slot's own prompt and `rpc/odv1_record_panel_substitution` records it before any judgment row is written. Two failures, or none free: fail closed at Panel Failure Note. The failed seat's first reply is kept in the enforcer log (`failed_reply`). The substitution is printed in the transcript as one line: the seat, the reason (error, timeout, empty reply, unreadable, refused), the reserve, the HTTP status where the node exposes it, and the reply length.
- ALTERNATES: a seat with uncured items is offered, in pool order, a model that passes G1 (its family does not debate), G2 (not the removed seat's model) and G3 (not already on this panel). The alternate reads the removed seat's own prompt fresh, is checked and cured as any seat, and is SEATED only with no uncured item; a model is seated once. `rpc/odv1_record_panel_alternates` records the seating; the removed judgment is kept in `drv_removed` and `odv1_drv_removals`; each tried alternate leaves an AUDIT line (SEATED or NOT SEATED and why). Each `drv_removed` entry names the rules that removed the seat (`removed_rules`, for example "K-W (cure) x4", with `removed_rules_total` and whether it equals `drv_uncured`), read from the seat's own stored lines; each ALTERNATE TRIED line ends "; REMOVED FOR: drv_uncured N: ...". SECOND ROUND: when a removed seat's first alternate ends NOT SEATED, the seat is unfilled, fewer than two alternates sit, and the pool holds a model not tried in the first round that passes G1 and G3, Second Alternate? sends that seat once more through the R2 chain (a copy of the first round's chain; G5 refuses a model tried in the first round; no early start). Alternate Merge then joins the rounds: a seated second alternate takes the seat; otherwise the first round's text stands with the second round's ALTERNATE TRIED lines added. At most two alternates sit. Every node after Dry? reads `Alternate Merge`.
- DEBATER FAULT LEDGER (pkgDFL rev 3; database only, record only, no penalty): every confirmed closing contradiction in `debate_enforcer_log` becomes a `debater_fault_ledger` row (trigger `dfl_from_log` on rows of rule E-CLOSECON, which never blocks the log write). A row is recorded when kind is enforcer_check, the run token is a real run, the item reads "X<n>", "J<n>" or "NEW", then DEFENDER or CHALLENGER, then "LINK <n>", and cure_reason begins CONFIRMED. The row holds the run, the side, the link, the source seat and the debater's model, read from `debate_results`. It is a table apart from `judge_fault_ledger` and feeds no prompt. `dfl_strike(run, side, item pattern, reason, grade file)` marks a row struck when a grader's hand reading finds the confirmation false; struck rows are kept and leave the view `dfl_report`, which counts runs, lines and links (run, side and link) per debater model. EXECUTE on the functions is held by lab_seat and service_role only.
- A new judge model is seated only after its first stored reply is read and shows real line breaks in its reasoning field.
- JUDGE FAULT LEDGER: every counted UNCURED judge item in `debate_enforcer_log` becomes a `judge_fault_ledger` row (trigger `jfl_from_log`, which never blocks the log write). The view `jfl_report` counts runs per judge model and fault kind, struck rows left out, and sets each count's state: COUNTING, EXAMPLES or ALTERNATE (the run thresholds are in the view). At EXAMPLES, Panel Build (and Reserve and Alternate Build, for their own seat) puts the model's own past items of that kind first in its user turn, as `jfl_block(model)` writes them. At ALTERNATE, Init Seats moves the seat to the end of the panel order, so it takes no panel place and is not the reserve while three unmoved seats remain; it stays in the alternate pool. A row found to be an engine fault is struck and stops counting.
- VALIDATION (Panel Payloads): one executed node per slot, a recognised envelope, parseable JSON (unescaped quotation marks inside `reasoning` are repaired once; a judgment keyed `reason` is read and flagged), closed token sets, integers >= 0, three distinct models, three distinct texts. Review lines written to `verdict_raw`: WITHDRAWN MISSED, SPLIT LINK, STANDING COUNT, SAME-MODEL DIVERGENCE (a seat running the single judge's model, grades compared).
- AGGREGATE (`aggregate_debate_panel`): a row is counted when untruncated, its tag in HELD/PARTIAL/BROKEN, both standings valid, and `drv_uncured = 0`. No counted row: UNGRADED. Fewer than two counted rows, or a tie at the top: CONTESTED. Otherwise the most frequent tag. `panel_split` shows the counts with "(n unreadable)" and "(n not counted: debate rule)".
- OUTCOME (`derive_debate_outcome`, `d5_outcome_for`): HELD -> DEFENDER PREVAILS; BROKEN -> CHALLENGER PREVAILS; PARTIAL -> DEFENDER PREVAILS, WEAKENED; UNGRADED, CONTESTED or UNKNOWN -> `UNGRADED — REVIEW` (the stored value joins the words with a long dash, U+2014; match it exactly in SQL). TIE: a HELD or PARTIAL panel whose counted seats of that verdict mostly wrote RESULT: UNDERDETERMINED gives UNDERDETERMINED; an exact half does too for debates after the tie-option cut-off in the function. CLOSE FORFEIT (pkgCFO rev 3; function `cfo_sides`): for a debate created at or after the write time held in the function (`v_cfo_from`), when the outcome is not the review outcome, the sides with a confirmed closing contradiction are read from the close enforcer rows only (seat "close enforcer", the debate's own run token, the DFL reading; a CONFIRMED (second check) row is not read twice; J rows are not read). Both sides: UNDERDETERMINED. The challenger alone: DEFENDER PREVAILS, and a DEFENDER PREVAILS, WEAKENED stays as it is. The defender alone: CHALLENGER PREVAILS. The sides found are returned as `close_forfeit`. Then the citation rule applies as below. Size: function +920. Limit: a judge-found contradiction never forfeits a side. CITATION RULE: a side that would prevail on a load-bearing link-round quotation the citation check reports WORDING NOT FOUND or CITED VERSE DOES NOT EXIST, or with the check unavailable, gets the review outcome and `citation_block` says why; WORDS ARE AT does not trip it.
- WHAT THE PANEL WRITES: at the first stamp only, `panel_verdict`, `framework_verdict` (the panel's verdict; the flow saves it null), `panel_fingerprint`, and the four standings fields as the counted seats' majority (null with a review line where no majority holds). `panel_split`, `outcome`, `judged_by`, `underdetermined_seats` and `citation_block` are re-derived on every call. Fences refuse on no rows, no declared panel, a roster mismatch, an incomplete panel or an undispatched seat.
- PANEL LATENCY: the row lands at Check Save with the panel fields NULL; they fill only after every dispatched seat has a `debate_judgments` row. Cures, the reserve and alternates add calls of up to 900 s each. A run is failed only when `debate_errors` holds its token or the execution status (status only) is error. Live figures: `SELECT r.run_token, max(j.created_at) - r.created_at FROM debate_results r JOIN debate_judgments j ON j.debate_id = r.id WHERE r.run_token LIKE 'ODV1-%' GROUP BY r.id, r.run_token, r.created_at;`
- RULES WRITTEN AFTER s82 (each from the package named; where one differs from the text above it in this section, the later rule is right; library line numbers are those of the version the package was written on, so find a line by its text):
  - EARLY ALTERNATE (pkgABH): Alternate Build: the stub runs the library source with Alternate Needed? as its input (Get Early Alternate sits between them), then uses the early alternate replies only when every alternate call has a matching early reply (same removed seat, same payload hash); otherwise the normal alternate call runs.
  - UNUSABLE ALTERNATE REPLY (pkgF3A rev 2s):
    - Alternate, unusable reply: when a removed judge's alternate returns a reply that cannot be used (no content, no JSON object, JSON that does not parse), round 2 of the alternate step may call the SAME model once more for that seat. Before, there was no retry and the seat stayed unfilled, so the panel counted two judges.
    - A usable reply that the rules refuse (uncured items, guards) is not retried. At most one retry.
    - The family rule in Second Alternate? now carries the same Haiku exemption that R2 Alternate Build already had. Without it the retry never offers Haiku when an Anthropic model debates.
    - Record: the row of the seat keeps both ALTERNATE TRIED lines when both replies were unusable; alternate_round2.retry names the retried model.
    - Left as is: with two unfilled seats the retried model goes to the first in panel order; the tried list of round 2 does not name the retried model (retry does).
    - There is one round 2 only: when the pool holds a second, untried model and the first reply was unusable, the first model is called again and the untried model is not called.
    - Cost: one more full alternate call when it fires (dry regrade of debate 34108: 107,977 prompt tokens, measured). Other failure texts are not retried. Unproven until two clean runs.
  - ALTERNATES GROK, ARGON AND HAIKU (pkgALT; Init Seats, Resolve Seats, Accept Build; where it differs from JUDGE ORDER, FAMILY RULE and ALTERNATES above, this rule is right):

The cap of two stands only in Second Alternate? (round 2). Live code did this already; the three-deep pool makes it reachable. A cap of two in round 1 is an option, not built.

  - ALTERNATE HYDROGEN (pkgHYD rev 2; Init Seats, Resolve Seats, Accept Build, and the request finisher `__orc` in 14 plain nodes and the Shared Library; where it differs from the pkgALT entry above, this rule is right):
