In a mixed case the early seat's cost is now read by Panel Tokens.
  - Limits: routing (an IF that routes item by item, and a branch that ends at a false IF) is simulated offline and only a live run shows the paths ran; R2 has no early path; in a mixed case Panel Tokens counts the early seat's cost. Limits: see section 17. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 42).

## 11. Persistence and the column map (crossed columns: read before any query)
Saved by `rpc/record_debate_row` with `{payload: saveBody}` BEFORE the panel runs; idempotent on `run_token` (a repeat returns the existing row).
| Column | Holds |
|---|---|
| `claude_r1`, `claude_r2` | DEFENDER rounds 1 and 2, whatever engine defended |
| `engine_r1`, `engine_r2` | CHALLENGER rounds 1 and 2 |
| `engine_r3` | DEFENDER link round (crossed: the name says engine) |
| `challenger_r3` | CHALLENGER link round |
| `defender_r4` | DEFENDER close round |
| `challenger_r4` | CHALLENGER close round |
| `engine` | challenger engine |
| `claude_model` / `engine_model` / `judge_model` | defender model / challenger model / single judge model |
| `defender_*`, `challenger_*` (`engine`, `model`, `profile`, `served`) | per-seat truth |
| `independent_verdict_raw` | the single judge's text, cure and review lines (second opinion); null while the single judge is switched off (§7) |
| `framework_verdict`, `panel_verdict`, standings and counts | the panel's, written by `derive_debate_outcome` (§10). `panel_verdict` carries a column comment: the panel's verdict on the defender's chain (HELD, BROKEN, CONTESTED, PARTIAL or UNGRADED; NULL until a complete panel is stamped), stamped once and frozen; it is not the debate's result, so `panel_verdict` HELD beside `outcome` UNDERDETERMINED is correct by design. Read `outcome` for the result |
| `review_notes` | forfeit notes, fence override reason, REVIEW THIS ROW lines, synthesis checks, C4 check, synthesis usage line |
| `citation_check`, `citation_block` | the KJV citation report and any prevail block; a row that is not MATCHES carries the `quote` it checked (300 characters) and the `alt` reference it tried |
| `conceded_split` (also on `debate_judgments`) | RECORD-ONLY jsonb {reader, defender, challenger}, each {stored, stated, granted, unexhibited, unnamed, granted_items, unexhibited_items}, read from the final verdict text by the shared count-line reader. granted is the side's own CONCEDED or WITHDRAWN items; unexhibited is the premises counted UNEXHIBITED. `conceded_links` is their sum, so it never alone means that the side conceded |
| `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_write_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens` | every model call of the run: rounds, synthesis, single judge, diagnostic at the save; panel, reserve, cure and alternate calls added after the panel by `rpc/record_debate_row_tokens` (totals, never lowered, row younger than 6 hours) |
| `data_class` | `study` or `contested` |
| `call_costs` | RECORD-ONLY jsonb (pkgRCS): one entry per model call of the run, written after the panel by `rpc/record_debate_row_tokens` from its argument `p_calls` (the fields of an entry are listed in the pkgRCS entry of section 13). NULL when the run stopped before Panel Tokens |
| `run_cost_usd` | RECORD-ONLY numeric (pkgRCS): the sum of the priced calls of `call_costs`; a call with no price is left out, so the figure reads low while a model has no row in `model_prices` |
- `variant = 'ORACLE_DEBATE_V1'`. `debate_results_write_guard` quarantines a row whose rounds, close rounds included, carry a truncation marker; `record_integrity_scan` checks the close rounds.
- Readers: `dash_odv1_by_id` returns `def_r4` and `cha_r4`. `debate_variant_canon` holds the ODV1 crossed columns (§17).
- Judgments: `debate_judgments` (one row per panel seat, `record_debate_judgment`, replace on debate and seat), `debate_replay_judgments` (dry regrades), `debate_enforcer_log` (§9).
- REMOVALS: `debate_removals` (a read-only view) lists every judge removed from a run, one row each: `debate_id`, `run_token`, `created_at`, `seat`, `api_model`, `drv_uncured`, `kind`, `replaced_by`, `replaced_by_model`, `removed_rules`. `kind` is `replaced` (a seat in `debate_results.drv_removed`: an alternate took it) or `not_counted` (a `debate_judgments` row with `drv_uncured > 0` whose seat is not in `drv_removed`: no alternate was seated). A truncated judge with `drv_uncured = 0` is not listed. Count removals from this view, not from `drv_removed` alone.
- When grading dies after the rounds are saved, the `debate_results` row is marked failed (`quarantine_reason`), record only.
- `model_prices` (table, pkgRCS; created with row-level security on and 3 rows): one row per model, columns `model`, `input_per_m`, `cache_read_per_m`, `cache_write_5m_per_m`, `cache_write_1h_per_m`, `output_per_m` (USD per million tokens), `peak_mult`, `peak_utc_hours`, `source` (the provider page) and `as_of` (date read). Row-level security is on with no policies. The 3 rows are claude-opus-5-5, claude-sonnet-5-5 and deepseek-flash, as_of 2026-11-11. `record_debate_row_tokens` (SECURITY DEFINER) reads it to price a call that OpenRouter did not bill; a model with no row gets no price, never 0. How a price is kept current: section 17, pkgRCS line. Rule entry: section 13, pkgRCS.
- SNAPSHOT EXPORT PICKS ITS ROWS ONCE (pkgGPS parts A and B; database function get_pending_snapshots; part C, the 20-minute schedule, is held):
  - Why: the function returned rows with one SELECT and marked rows exported with a second UPDATE whose own subquery chose them again. A queue row committed between the two statements, with an earlier triggered_at, was marked exported and never returned, so it never reached Drive; one returned row stayed pending and was saved twice.
  - What it does: (A) one UPDATE ... RETURNING over rows chosen once, ordered by (triggered_at, id), FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED; signature, the five keys per row, SECURITY DEFINER and search_path unchanged. (B) EXECUTE is held by postgres, service_role and lab_seat only; anon and authenticated are revoked. The workflow credential is service_role.
  - Size: prosrc 617 to 572 (-45); functiondef 816 to 785 (-31).
  - Shown live: the 17:00 CST 2026-11-13 hourly run, the first on the new function, marked 100 rows exported (table_snapshot_queue, last exported_at 17:00:28).
  - Limits: whether a gap commit ever happened live is not measured; tie groups alone did not reproduce the loss. 2,537 rows were pending at 17:36 CST (oldest 2026-11-09 19:39) at 100 rows per hourly call. Revert: wall2024/pkgGPS/revert_AB.sql.

## 12. Citation check against the KJV
- PATH: Final Output -> Citation Scan -> Cited Verses -> Quote Compare -> Find Words -> Quote Report -> Regrade After Quotes? -> Build Independent Verdict (or Panel Build on a regrade). The report goes to every judge, to `citation_check` and to the document.
- BOOK FROM THE PROSE: a bare reference (chapter:verse with no book) also gets an alt reference: the same chapter:verse in the last of the 66 books named as a plain word in its clause (quoted words masked; a name followed by its own chapter:verse does not count; John, James, Jude, Mark, Timothy, Titus and Philemon are read as person names). Quote Compare tries the alt only when the first reference fails, and records `rebound_from`.
- Citation Scan pairs each quotation with its reference in all eight rounds (reference then quote, quote then reference, short book names; a line opening [L2] is a gloss and skipped). A bare reference takes its book only from the same sentence and clause; with none it is left unchecked. The verse read lists one condition per book and chapter while the request stays within 7,000 characters; a quotation whose chapter was not read is NOT CHECKED, never "does not exist".
- [L2] TAG CHECK, ROOTS: in an [L2] tag, a word named only as the root of another word ("from *nomos*", "from the root X", "derived from G3551", "cognate of", "related to", and a word or number in parentheses right after such a word) is a root. A root that stands in none of the cited verses does not refuse the tag; the tag's other words decide it. Debater rounds and judge lines use the same library. Each refused tag's review line names its words, the verses it was bound to, and each word's map verdict.
- The [L2] tag check reads a tag's words from the text before the tag as well as after it, so a tag written after the words it labels ("... with *ek* (out of) [L2]") is read. A quoted English word beside an [L2] tag is English, not a transliteration, and is not matched against the original words.
- [L2] TAG CHECK, VERSE SCOPE (pkgL2S rev 2; Citation Scan only): a word tag is bound to the verses of its own block. A line that opens with a bold MEANS or ANSWER (`**MEANS`, `**ANSWER`, any case, also after `>`, `-` or `_`; bold "Answer to [CR2] press" and bold "ANSWER CHECK" are such lines) starts a new block, so a tag under it binds to that block's verses and not to the whole LINK section, and a tag above it loses the verses below it. A MEANS or ANSWER line without bold, a bold MEANS in the middle of a line, and a markdown heading (`## ANSWER ...`, `# MEANS ...`) start no block. A tag under a bold ANSWER line takes that whole block, with no narrowing to its paragraph. The same block reader in the 20 other nodes (Extract, Build, L2 Count Plan) is unchanged, so the in-round checks keep the wider scope. Size: code +71, prompt 0. Limits: see section 17.
- A single-quoted quotation after a verse reference pairs with that reference in the ECL pair reader (Send Back Build and Record, Alt Send Back Build and Record) and in the Commitment 12 KJV check; an in-word apostrophe (Mary's) neither opens nor closes it.
- Statuses: MATCHES; WORDS ARE AT <ref>; WORDING DIFFERS FROM <ref>; WORDING NOT FOUND IN <book>; CITED VERSE DOES NOT EXIST; NOT CHECKED. A quote of the opponent's own earlier words is a phrase, not a citation.
- To the judges: WORDING DIFFERS, WORDING NOT FOUND and DOES NOT EXIST are marked NOT COUNTED (not footing, witness or answer). WORDS ARE AT is real KJV text under the wrong reference: named as a citation error and still counted as [L1] footing (§17).
- ONE REFERENCE PARSER (`REFP`, Init with Guards) serves every check: the ledger, the close-round checks, the citation scan, K-G4, K-G6, K-G9 and the C-label record. A reference is `Book c:v`, with a range and a comma list after it: a bare verse number after a full reference takes that book and chapter ("Luke 21:26, 35" is Luke 21:26 and Luke 21:35); a comma list of full chapter:verse references is several references of that book ("Isaiah 7:11, 7:14"). A reference with no book takes the last book named before it only when no sentence ends between them ("(1:24)" alone names none). Each node keeps its own caps through an adapter.
- Backslash-escaped quotation marks are read as the mark they stand for before matching.
- MATCHES (MARGIN): a quotation matching the verse's 1611 margin text is a match.
- Switches in Quote Compare, both off: `HYPHEN_JOIN` (when true, a hyphen inside a word is removed before matching, both sides; awaits the operator's ruling) and `SHORTENED_CHECK` (when true, a strict part of the cited verse is marked SHORTENED and NOT COUNTED; off under the ruling that a partial quotation counts).
- Known limit: a quotation whose reference stands on the line above it is not paired.
- A BARE "verse N" IN AN [L2] ROW BINDS TO THE CHAPTER OF ITS BLOCK'S REFERENCES WHEN THEY ARE IN ONE CHAPTER (pkgVBN, part of pkgEPC rev 2; Citation Scan only; ASK 270):
  - Why: in run 37000 a challenger block had [L1] Luke 1:32 and an [L2] row "The dio kai ('therefore also') of verse 35 ..."; the binder tied the tag to Luke 1:32 and Luke 1:34 and marked the word not in them, though dio kai stands in Luke 1:35. Rule: an [L2] row may name any verse the same block cites, not only the block's [L1] verse.
  - What it does: a bare "verse N", "verses N" or "v.N" in an [L2] row binds to a chapter only when every full reference the tag is already bound to (its row and its block, as the binder reads them) lies in one chapter; otherwise nothing is added. The lexicon map still decides whether the word stands there.
  - Size: Citation Scan +573 characters; calls 0.
  - Shown offline: over 5,472 stored tags 11 change, all same-chapter adds in Luke 1 or Matthew 1, among them 37000 dio kai (+Luke 1:35). A wider form using the [L1] rows bound "verse 20" to Luke 1:20 and "Verse 35" to Matthew 1:35 and was dropped.
  - Not shown by a run: in run 37032 the DR2 dio kai tag sits in a block that spans many chapters, so no binding was made and it stays NOT COUNTED.
  - Limits: a block whose full references span more than one chapter is not resolved.

## 13. Debate documents
- Panel Done (or Panel Failure Note) -> Panel Tokens -> Write Tokens -> Get Row -> Get Judgments -> Get Verse -> Get Guards -> Format Markdown -> Plain Rule Lines -> Save to Drive (one `.md` file in the study folder, operational_doc §11) -> Raise If Incomplete. A failed panel saves the document with `INCOMPLETE - ` and Raise If Incomplete fails the execution. A dry regrade writes no document.
- Contents: topic, seats, judges and result table with review flags, forfeit notes, citation check, how to read the debate (Isaiah 28:10 from `kjv_verses`, the layers, the rules), all eight rounds (Round 4 printed when present, with the new-press list), synthesis, diagnostic, each panel judgment, the single judge as second opinion, run time. The RESULT reason keeps escaped quotes inside its JSON string.
- EXPORT, CURED K-W AND K-S3 FLAGS PRINT AS CURED (pkgRVF; Format Markdown):
  - Text:
    - Node: Format Markdown (export only; feeds Plain Rule Lines, then Save to Drive). No other node, no prompt, no judge or enforcer text changes.
    - What it does:
    - The export prints each extractor or audit flag that says REVIEW THIS ROW. A flag prints as "Cured flag" when its key is in the set of the same judge's CURED rule reports; otherwise it prints as "Review flag". Before this package, flagKey gave no key to two flag forms, so every such flag printed as "Review flag" even when the seat's own cure report said CURED:
      - K-W: `(K-W; ruling N) -- <ITEM> #N: "..."`. Key K-W|<ITEM #N>, matched to the report row with RULE K-W and the same ITEM.
      - K-S3: `(S3; ruling N) -- <SIDE> <ROUND>: <sentence>; a close-round concession ...`. The flag carries no #N, so the key is K-S3|<SIDE ROUND>|<first 40 letters and digits of the sentence, lower case>. The report side reads the same key from the report row's LINE field (`<SIDE ROUND>: <sentence>; STATEMENT: ...`). The sentence ends at "; a close-round", "; STATEMENT" or "; JUDGE STATEMENT".
    - Limits:
      - Two K-S3 flags of one judge whose sentences share the same first 40 letters and digits share one key; if one is CURED and the other is not, both print as cured. Not seen in stored data (0 of 842 S3 flags in 30 days matched both a CURED and an UNCURED report).
      - Report reading: a K-S3 report row whose LINE does not begin with the side and round adds no sentence key (its flag stays Review).
      - Record and export only: no grade, count or verdict reads this label.
    - Size:
    - Format Markdown code +590 characters (measured on the live fetch-back); prompt 0.
  - Size: Format Markdown only; code +590 (2,926,178 to 2,926,768, measured); fixed prompt 0.
  - Why: the result table printed "left uncured after the cure, it removes the seat. REVIEW THIS ROW." on items the violations table shows CURED (run 35067: 75 flag rows, all printed "Review flag", 0 "Cured flag"). Limits: see section 17.
  - Unproven until two clean runs; not yet run on the n8n runner. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 31).
- EXPORT AND SAVED REVIEW NOTES FIND FLAGS WITH AN INNER BRACKET (pkgNBR rev 2; Format Markdown and Build Save Body):
  - Text (rev 2):
    - Nodes: Format Markdown (export) and Build Save Body (the single judge's flags copied into review_notes). Prompt: none.
    - What it does:
    - Both nodes find REVIEW THIS ROW flags with one pattern. The old pattern stopped at the first "]", so a flag holding an inner bracket (for example a K-W item "DEFENDER CR3 LINK 6 [premise 1] #5", or "pressed: [DR2] premise 5") was not found at all: it was left out of the export's flag table, and the single judge's copy of it never reached the stored review notes. The pattern is now one alternation: first a form that allows one level of closed inner brackets, else the old form, so every flag the old pattern found is still found (for example a quotation cut off after an open "[" with no "]"): `\[(?:EXTRACTOR|AUDIT) v\d+(?:[^\]\[\n]|\[[^\]\[\n]*\])*REVIEW THIS ROW(?:[^\]\[\n]|\[[^\]\[\n]*\])*\]|\[(?:EXTRACTOR|AUDIT) v\d+[^\]\n]*REVIEW THIS ROW[^\]\n]*\]`.
    - Limits:
      - Two levels of inner brackets ("[[CR3]]") and an open "[" followed later by a closed pair ("[CR3] AND [CR") are still missed, as before. Stored in 30 days: 3 single-judge flags of these forms; 0 panel flags.
      - Build Save Body cures only the flag forms its own key reader knows; it has no K-W or K-S3 key (pkgRVF changed only the export), so a single judge's K-W flag is copied as REVIEW THIS ROW even when that judge cured it.
      - Record only: no grade, count or verdict reads these flags. Failure Salvage only appends to review_notes.
    - Size:
    - +102 code characters per node, +204 in all (measured on live s133); prompt 0.
  - Note: the Limits item of the text that Build Save Body has no K-W or K-S3 cure key was true on s134; pkgSJK adds those keys to Build Save Body (entry in section 7).
  - Size: Format Markdown +102 and Build Save Body +102, +204 in all (code 2,926,768 to 2,926,972; +102 per node measured against live s133); fixed prompt 0.
  - Unproven until two clean runs; not yet run on the n8n runner. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 32).
- RUN COST RECORDED AND PRINTED AT THE END OF THE TRANSCRIPT (pkgRCS revision 2; Panel Tokens, Plain Rule Lines and Write Tokens; with the database objects debate_results.call_costs, debate_results.run_cost_usd, model_prices and record_debate_row_tokens):
    - Text:
      - Purpose: track and record the cost; the cost goes at the end of the debate transcript.
      - Flow change (3 nodes, no new node, connections equal):
        - Panel Tokens lists every model call of the run (108 call nodes named literally, as the task runner requires; each item once, by response id). Per call: node, item number, model, family, uncached input, cache read, cache write (5-minute and 1-hour), output, and for OpenRouter the billed USD. Billed USD is usage.cost plus, when the call is on the lab's own provider key (is_byok), cost_details.upstream_inference_cost.
        - Write Tokens sends the list as p_calls to record_debate_row_tokens.
        - Plain Rule Lines prints "Cost of this run" as the last section of the transcript, read back from Get Row: the total, its billed and estimated parts, and one row per model. The per-call list is stored in the database only.
        - The Panel Tokens list loop is inside try; a fault there leaves the old fields as they were.
        - EARLY ALTERNATE COST ROW (pkgEAC; Panel Tokens): Panel Tokens costs each stored early alternate reply (Get Early Alternate) that carries usage; a reply id already costed through Early Alternate Reply is not costed twice. Size: code +223, prompt 0.
      - Database change (one transaction, before the flow write; file w1/schema_RCS.sql):
        - debate_results gains call_costs jsonb and run_cost_usd numeric.
        - New table model_prices (per million tokens: input, cache read, cache write 5-minute and 1-hour, output; peak multiplier and peak hours; source page; date read), row-level security on with no policies.
        - Fill (written with the schema): claude-opus-5-5, claude-sonnet-5-5 (platform.claude.com pricing page) and deepseek-flash (api-docs.deepseek.com pricing page, off-peak rates, 2x in peak hours 01-04 and 06-10 UTC Monday to Friday), read 11-11.
        - Revert: w4/rcs/revert_schema_RCS.sql restores the live five-argument function and grants.
        - record_debate_row_tokens gains p_calls jsonb DEFAULT NULL. It prices each call: billed if OpenRouter gave a figure; otherwise from model_prices ("price list", an estimate); a model with no row gets no price (NULL, never 0). It stores call_costs and run_cost_usd (sum of priced calls). The old grants are restored exactly. The old five-argument call still works.
      - Sizes (measured, rev 2): code +6,374 (Panel Tokens +4,849, Plain Rule Lines +1,525); Write Tokens body +47; model prompts +0; nodes +0. Transcript +877 to +1,026 characters on the 7 stored runs. Data the task runner sends to Panel Tokens grows by the outputs of the call nodes (2,412,847 bytes on 35067); Plain Rule Lines receives the Get Row row (281,961 bytes on 35067).
      - Limits:
        - No price is held in the code. A model with no row in model_prices shows "no price" and is left out of the total; the transcript gives the count. Prices change: a seat must update the rows when a provider changes its page.
        - DeepSeek peak pricing ignores Chinese public holidays (a call then is priced at peak, the higher figure). A DeepSeek call with no time is priced at peak.
        - Estimates follow list prices; no invoice is read. Anthropic 5-minute and 1-hour cache writes are priced apart; OpenRouter calls are billed as returned.
        - A call node run more than once in one execution is read on its last run only (none in the 11 stored runs).
        - The four existing token totals (input, output, cache write, cache read) are unchanged and undercount: they leave out the resend rounds, Accept, every DeepSeek call, Send Back, Gate, QR and the single judge's re-ask.
        - A run that stops before Panel Tokens (failure salvage, dry, regrade) stores no cost; its transcript says "No cost record is stored for this run."
    - Prices in the fill: USD per million tokens, input, cache read, 5-minute cache write, 1-hour cache write, output. claude-opus-5-5 4, 0.20, 5, 8, 20; claude-sonnet-5-5 2, 0.10, 2.50, 4, 10 (page platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing, no long-context surcharge listed for these two models; the 1.1x US geo rate and fast mode do not apply because every Anthropic call checked reports inference_geo "global", service_tier "standard" and speed "standard", measured on 35067, 34852 and 33371). deepseek-flash off-peak cache miss 0.15, cache hit 0.003, output 0.6, and twice that in peak hours, 01:00 to 04:00 and 06:00 to 10:00 UTC, Monday to Friday, excluding Chinese public holidays (page api-docs.deepseek.com/quick_start/pricing, which gives no effective date); stored as peak_mult 2 and peak_utc_hours {1,2,3,6,7,8,9}. The peak test in the SQL is isodow below 6 and the UTC hour in that set. The figures are the providers' list prices read on 11-11 and are estimates; OpenRouter figures are the billed amounts as returned (measured).
    - Why the record reads only the call nodes (measured on run 35067 and live s139): record_debate_row inserts only keys that are real debate_results columns and drops a new key silently, so the package needs the new column; the same usage object is copied into Panel Judge N Route, Panel Judges Collect, the Normalizers and SJ Re-ask Pick and must not be counted twice; the Extract nodes carry their own usage objects that differ from their Call node; panel judges go through the Panel Judge Call subflow and their usage is in Panel Judge N openrouter. On 35067: 20 OpenRouter calls billed $1.394 (OpenRouter's usage.cost is 0 because the calls are on the lab's own key, so the figure is cost_details.upstream_inference_cost), 11 Anthropic calls and 18 DeepSeek calls with no price returned.
    - Size (measured): code +6,374 (Panel Tokens +4,849, Plain Rule Lines +1,525), Write Tokens body +47, model prompts +0, nodes +0, connections and settings equal. Transcript +877 to +1,026 characters on the 7 stored runs that reach Panel Tokens. Code total of s140 2,934,536, which is the code total of s139, 2,928,162, plus 6,374 (measured). Panel Tokens receives about 2.4 MB late in the run (2,412,847 bytes on 35067) and Plain Rule Lines receives the Get Row row (281,961 bytes on 35067) (measured).
    - Order and revert: the schema and the price fill are written before the flow, and the old five-argument call still works, so a flow revert needs no database revert; the database revert is run after the flow revert.
    - Unproven: the first proof is the next run: a call_costs list whose count equals the run's call items, run_cost_usd, and the "Cost of this run" section at the end of the transcript; a live run proves the record, not the estimates. The first-run check is in the grade plan (section 35). Limits: see section 17.
- When the panel verdict and the outcome differ, the outcome comes first, with one plain line computed from the row's fields. The reading guide prints ruling 372 and the close-round rule. The synthesis checks S-A to S-H are printed after the synthesis.
- Every DG number is printed with the guard's subject; Plain Rule Lines turns each CLAUSE line into a "Rule applied:" sentence.

## 14. Regrade and dry regrade
- REGRADE: POST the normal webhook with the run's original `topic` and seats plus `regrade_run_token`. Init Seats keeps the token. Path: Regrade Row -> Regrade Judgments -> Regrade Served Conclusions -> Regrade Served Sights -> Regrade Load -> citation check -> Panel Build -> panel, cures, alternates -> Dry? -> write path. Rounds, synthesis, single judge and save are not re-run. A row saved without close rounds is regraded on six rounds.
- REGRADE LOAD refuses unless: one saved row; variant ODV1; same topic, seats, profiles and panel; no existing judgments (except dry); all six rounds and the synthesis present. Named lists are rebuilt from today's corpus, so they are approximate for old runs.
- DRY REGRADE (`regrade_dry: true`): writes three rows to `debate_replay_judgments` only (`record_replay_judgments`); no judgment, outcome, enforcer log or document. Only a dry regrade takes `rulebook:'draft'`, the test inputs and `qualify_alternate`.
- RULES WRITTEN AFTER s82 (each from the package named; where one differs from the text above it in this section, the later rule is right; library line numbers are those of the version the package was written on, so find a line by its text):
  - THE QUESTION ROUND (QR Ask Build, QR Check, QR Final, QR Merge): runs only in a dry regrade (question_trial true, dry true); running it in a live debate needs the operator's approval.
    - The ask carries the watch-and-question paragraph; the JSON form takes optional earlier_round and earlier_quote. qrCheck keeps ruling 415's checks and, only when an earlier field holds text, checks that the round is that debater's closing round (CR4 or DR4), earlier_round is one of its earlier rounds, the earlier quote is word for word in that round, and the new-verse check reads the earlier quote too. The debater sees both quotes. The judges' text labels a CONTRADICTION QUESTION with both quotes.
  - REPEATED CONTRADICTION QUESTION (pkgQRD rev 5):
    - Question round, repeated contradiction question: a judge whose closing contradiction question to a debater repeats an earlier judge's is returned once and asked for a different contradiction if it sees one. A question repeats when it is put to the same debater and both of its quotes (earlier and closing) lie inside the earlier judge's quotes, equal or narrower. The earlier judge must be free of faults on both sides and not itself returned on that side. A wider question is never returned against a narrower one.
    - If the second question fails its checks or the judge gives none, the first question stands. Every question asked by at least one judge is still put to the debater.
    - Cost: each returned judge is one more full judge call, about 76,000 prompt tokens (estimate from the stored calls).
    - Acts only behind the question trial switch, which is on only in dry regrades. Not active in live runs. Unproven until two clean runs.

## 15. Verifying a change
- operational_doc §27 (Validating a change to the debate engine) governs. A change to a judge instruction, enforcer or extractor is verified by REPLAY over stored rows and their stored fields, before it goes live; a live run proves only that the wired flow executes.
- Instruments: the dry regrade (§14) for judge text; for a Code node that writes no model prompt, run the candidate and the live code in a harness over every stored ODV1 row and compare outputs line by line. Every check needs a control that proves it fires. A Code node is replayed on a finished run's saved node outputs (`/mnt/project-files/wall2024/inputs/<run>_<Node>.json`): its real code runs with `$()` reading those files (a node with no saved output throws as unexecuted), with Date fixed and `$execution` stubbed. The base code must reproduce the saved output before the candidate is compared. A firing path no stored run reached is tested by writing one true breach into a saved input and checking that only that item changes. THE REPLAY GATE (`gate/replay.cjs --base <strip> --cand <strip> [--targets <file>]`, in the build tree) runs the library nodes in dependency order on every saved run with an index file and on every near-miss case in `gate/cases/`, once with each source, each library node reading the recomputed output of the library nodes upstream of it. Outputs are compared byte for byte (timestamps ignored), then item row by item row; a changed, new or missing row passes only when it matches the package's targets, and a changed text line outside the rows only when an `allow_lines` entry allows it. Each near-miss case edits a saved judge reply and states what the candidate must give. Each node run has a time limit; an overrun is a failure. The exit code is 0 only on GATE PASS. A change on a path no saved run reaches passes the gate, so a package brings a near-miss case for each such path. The gate does not recompute past a non-library node. THE PACK COMMAND (`gate/pack.cjs`) packs a strip folder into stubs and the Shared Library in a temporary folder, requires every stub to rebuild byte for byte and the stub rebase to pass, and creates only the new output folder. THE LIBRARY SOURCE (`gate/src.cjs split|join|check`) holds each statement shared by two or more library nodes once (`lib/<name>.js`) and joins the node sources from it byte for byte; `VARIANTS.txt` lists names that have different text in different nodes, each of which a fix must reach on purpose.
- Before writing: read the live node code verbatim, guard the write on its md5, change only the named nodes, read the workflow back (versionId, md5 of each changed node, connections). A saved draft is not active. Code reads another node's output only by its literal name, `$('<name>')`. A `$()` on a computed name makes the task runner send every node's data to that node, at the run's end where the run is largest (an out-of-memory stop). Where a node must choose among nodes, it uses a table of literal `$('<name>')` calls. Check before writing: no code node has a computed `$()`.
- Apply a database change that the flow needs (a new column, function or table) BEFORE the flow change; `record_debate_row` drops payload keys that are not columns, silently.
- AFTER GOING LIVE, a live run is owed and is READ WHOLE: every round, every judgment with its audit, cure and alternate lines, the single judge, the synthesis, `review_notes`, the citation check and the enforcer log, each pressed witness checked by name in the holding side's later rounds. Read `length()` of each round first; a forfeited or cut round grades as a clean win and says nothing about the change. Report by run token what was read and what was not.
- The two failure classes are reported apart: the judge misread the transcript; or its verdict contradicts the rule it was given (computable from stored text).
- RULES WRITTEN AFTER s82 (each from the package named; where one differs from the text above it in this section, the later rule is right; library line numbers are those of the version the package was written on, so find a line by its text):
  - THE SHARED LIBRARY (stub form 3): the Shared Library node holds the shared lines once (/*LIB*/) and, for each library node by name, its line list, own lines and source length (/*MAP*/). A library node's stub reads its entry by name, rebuilds its source, checks the length and runs it. A stub changes only when the nodes it reads change; every other library change is a change to the Shared Library node alone. Packing: gate/pack.cjs, form set by gate/STUBV.
  - Library nodes include the 12 three-copy nodes Send Back Build, Send Back Record, Judge Enforcer Build, Judge Enforcer Record, each with its Alt and R2 Alt copy; each rebuilds its former code byte for byte. A change to any library node is made in the Shared Library sources and repacked.
  - Library write rule: a library write may change only the Shared Library; packs are made on the live fetch-back.
  - No comments: the live flow carries no code comments (plain Code nodes and library sources alike). The commented sources stay in git, in the pre-strip library sources and in the revert copy. A comment-strip is verified by equal syntax trees, stub rebuild and byte-equal replay of saved runs; a changed node with no saved run rests on tree and token equality alone.

## 16. Troubleshooting (symptom -> cause -> what to do)
1. A run stops at a round Extract with "refused by the provider: finish_reason=content_filter" -> the provider's filter refused the round -> a refusal is not a round; the row is contested with no verdict. Repeated refusals by one model fall under §17 (MiMo).
2. A panel seat fails closed with "an envelope this parser does not recognise" -> often `finish_reason: length` with empty content: the reasoning used the whole limit -> read the seat's `failed_reply` in `debate_enforcer_log`; the parser's message misnames the cause.
3. `panel_split` shows "(n not counted: debate rule)" -> those seats left enforcer items uncured -> read `debate_enforcer_log` by run token (`cure_status`, `cure_reason`, `response_line`) and the AUDIT lines in `verdict_raw`.
4. Panel UNGRADED or CONTESTED -> no counted seat, fewer than two, or no majority -> read the counted seats; check whether an alternate was tried and why it was not seated.
5. Outcome is the review value with a HELD or BROKEN panel -> the citation rule blocked the prevailing side -> read `citation_block`.
6. Close rounds missing from a new row -> `defender_r4`/`challenger_r4` absent from the table, or Regrade Load on an old row -> check the columns exist; old rows have none.
7. A "Conclusion #N" that kb_conclusions does not hold -> may be sight N cited in the wrong namespace -> check `kb_sights` before calling it fabricated.
8. Code-node edits, escapes and nested parameters -> operational_doc §7 (Editing gotchas).
9. A run fails with "FAIL-CLOSED (pkgRPN) ... routed to a removed direct provider call", at a round, judge or cure call node -> a seat resolved to openai, gemini, grok or mimo as a family (model_config provider, Init Seats `ENG` or Resolve Seats `JFAM`); at a cure call the child ODV1 Cure Call raised it -> set the seat's provider to openrouter (or deepseek for the enforcer) in model_config; do not re-add a direct call node.

- SAME FAMILY ON ONE BENCH: sol may judge beside the chatgpt seat (both openai) when ChatGPT judges or is the reserve; the code allows it.

  - pkgBMH rev 2 (barred-move finder phrases; the 14 B nodes): on 467 stored rounds that hold the phrases (of 3,675 rounds, 854 rows) the harvest goes from 276 to 282, none lost, the 6 new each read by hand as a sentence saying the other side's claim has no verse or row that states it; an absence sentence with no heading, or after an "against C4" written inside a line, is not harvested; the harvest only lists candidates and the later check that decides each was not replayed; no run has carried it.

  - pkgSRA rev 4 (no line of THE ANSWER IN FULL in the same-round section): the test reads the whole answer block, not the 900 characters shown; a reworded repeat is not caught; no run has carried it.
  - pkgOWR rev 3 (grants worded "is granted" or "are conceded" in the own record): a present-tense report of the other side's grant with no "by the ..." after it is listed as the writer's own (1 of 55 added entries on 94 stored debates; "The defender has granted the register. The register is therefore granted" is listed); an entry can begin inside a quotation or after an ellipsis (2 of 55); no run has carried it.
  - pkgOWR (own record in the close request): a narrowing or concession written in another form is not listed (measured on 95 stored runs: 16 of 440 such lines, among them "PARTLY CONCEDED, NARROWED", "SUPPORTED, narrowed" and a dash in place of the colon); the grants part carries the grants finder's limits and has no cap; a debater-input change is proven only by debater output, and no run has carried it.
  - pkgDFL rev 3 (debater fault ledger): a row records the engine's confirmation, not a hand reading; n_lines counts log rows, so repeat rows on one link count more than once; a dry regrade records again under its new run number; stored runs before the write are not in the table; berean_ro reads the view, not the table.
  - pkgCNV rev 3 (K-G4 retraction against the judge's own ANSWER CHECK): an ANSWER CHECK line with no C, D or G round label records nothing, and a reply that restates MEETS with no round label does not drop the record; a round written "[GR2]" is not read; a list that mixes rounds of both sides records the verse; a short book name, a verse range and a verse list are not matched to the line's verse; MISSES and MEETS on one verse for two presses records the verse; MEETS inside "CHANGE TO:" or in prose does not drop the record; a MISSES on the answer verse of the line records it. Not run from the raising check through to the cure check; no paid check.
  - pkgMUS rev 3 (ember, gemini and spark as alternates, reasoning effort low): one run seated ember: its reply carried its line breaks as escaped text, the line-anchored judge checks read no line, and its verdict entered the panel with only the single-verse and withdrawn-footing checks run; ember is out of the seat lists permanently (§10, EMBER REMOVED). No run has gone through since ember's entries were removed. No run has seated gemini or spark. Each model's fitness rests on one full-body check call at effort low. The follow-on calls to a seated alternate are read from the code, not run. The reserve path is open: the reserve is the first free alternate (hydrogen in the usual debate), and the database function odv1_record_panel_substitution holds a seat list that names no alternate seat, so a reserve substitution would be refused there; no run has reached it. Three more agreement calls per debate.
  - pkgHYD rev 2 (hydrogen as second alternate, reasoning effort low): no run has seated hydrogen. The model's fitness rests on one full-body check call at effort low. The follow-on calls to a seated hydrogen (send-back, gate, accept-return, question) are read from the code, not run. Nothing after the alternate step (the judgment rows of an alternate seat, the panel aggregate, the outcome) has run with hydrogen. One more agreement call per debate.
  - pkgCLP (a CONCEDED label before a row in a CHANGE TO): not read, and the change stays NOT APPLIED with its reason: the label written twice; the label together with the out-of-round heading, in either order; "#" marks before the label; a bold side name after the label; CONCEDED with no colon or dash after it. A label on a second line of the change is joined onto the row with the label.
  - pkgOHK rev 2 (a flagged out-of-round heading): a flagged line "OUT-OF-ROUND CONCESSIONS: NONE" and any other flagged heading are still replaced by the row; a row that restates an existing row in another form is added as a second row; a row with bars and no quotation is taken as a row; a second line in the CHANGE TO is joined onto the row.
  - pkgWVR rev 9 (withdrawn verse cited again in the close) and pkgCRX (judge-named candidates get the shared rules and the closing block): the finder's unread forms are listed in section 9; an enforcer NEW line may confirm an unsupported pair; a judge-named candidate whose closing quotation is not found gets no block.
  - pkgKGL, pkgKNC rev 2, pkgKAG (the key-word reader): a plain grant or concession that names no link is passed over after 10 such items of its side; a side can carry more than 10 items; "Granted" and "Conceded" openers are read by the grants list only; "I accept that" and "I agree that" are read by the key-word reader only, with the limits listed in section 9.

## 18. Write register
- The write register is engine_manual_log: one row per manual version, with what that version added. Section 0 sets the rule: each new version is entered with its log row, and no write history goes in the body.
- The register entries up to version 126 are in the engine_manual_log row for version 127 and in engine_manual version 126 section 18. A reference elsewhere in this manual to "the entry in section 18" means that row.
- Live flow: s200 (n8n version 47809843, 455 nodes; Shared Library 931,428 characters, sha a894ff926b24be72). s189 = s184 + pkgABR rev 3 + pkgGKV rev 2 (s185) + pkgKDN rev 3 (s186) + pkgERC rev 2 (s187) + pkgJ2 rev 2 (s188) + pkgONR + pkgAJN (s189). s198 = s189 + pkgD22 (s190) + pkgEPT (s191) + pkgPWF rev 2 (s192) + pkgBCL rev 4 (s193) + pkgJBC rev 2 (s194) + pkgPSN (s195) + pkgBFX rev 2 (s196) + pkgEPC rev 2 (s197: pkgECB, pkgPWS, pkgCLN, pkgVBN) + pkgNRW rev 3 (s198). s200 = s198 + pkgACT part 1 (s199) + pkgACT part 2 (s200). `derive_debate_outcome` md5 4ad4e2ab746eb2cdf124836daf731196.
- Everything from s91 on is unproven until two clean repeated debates. A dry regrade is not a run.
